# Modular Ship Assembly

## Summary

Modular Ship Assembly is the system behind the game's first promise, "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships": it owns the three physical acts that change the ship's shape (weld a derelict section onto the home-ship, cut a welded section away along its seam, and seat a carried module into a pre-shaped bay) and the single number of workmanship behind every weld. It does so as a thin orchestrator of one atomic, no-rollback command per act across the Life-Support Graph, the ship layout and the ship body, with no menu and no meter: the player sees a bead, a scar, a seated module and a seam that weeps, and learns whether a cut will be clean from the world before they press the button. Read this GDD if you touch welding, cutting, module install, the channel-then-apply handoff, the seam's life after the weld, or the riskiest MVP build item (the first spike).

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed, hard); Life-Support Graph (designed, hard); Item & Module Database (designed, hard); Interaction System (designed, hard); Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed, hard); Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed, hard); Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed, soft); Player Controller & Input and Suit & Equipment (designed, soft); Web Map & Transit (designed, hard for the offer); Boarding & Salvage (designed, hard: Boarding depends on Assembly); Adaptive Audio & Tells (designed, soft); HUD (undesigned, soft); Sanity & Hallucination Rules (draft in flight, soft; the Perception Filter itself has no file); Horror Entities (draft in flight, soft)`

## Overview

**Modular Ship Assembly is the system that changes the shape of the ship: it owns the three player commands that weld a derelict section onto the home-ship, cut a welded section away along its seam, and seat a carried module into a hardpoint, plus the one number of workmanship (`q`) that colours every weld.** At the data layer it is a thin orchestrator over four other owners' state. A weld or a cut is one atomic cross-block command whose `apply` calls published mutators on the Life-Support Graph (M2 `weld_join` / M3 `cut_detach`), Ship Section & Layout (LM2 `attach_section` / LM3 `detach_section`) and Ship Body (BM3 `rebase_on_attach` / BM4 `rebase_on_detach`) in one `WriteScope`, in that order, with no rollback (kernel CR-9); an install is a smaller atomic command that calls the Graph's M5 `install_module`, the Suit's `release_carried`, the Controller's `clear_carry_ref` (proposed name) and Interaction's `sync_section`. Assembly owns no geometry (Layout), no seam edges or atmosphere (Graph), no pose or actors (Body), no item vocabulary (Item DB), no channel clock or noise (Interaction). What it does own is small and exact: the five command types, the weld quality `q` (`WELD_Q_DEFAULT`), the channel durations (`WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS`), the offer predicates (`weld_offer`, `seam_cut_offer`), the derivation of the cut mode from true state, the order of calls inside each apply, one outcome block (`assembly.state`) with one hook (`assembly.step`), and its own reason codes (`MSA.*`). Its hard job follows from the kernel's failure model: because there is no rollback, every precondition of every call inside an `apply` must be provable by a pure read in `validate`, so most of this document is the list of those proofs and of the places where the proof deliberately stops (module-body state, which a player-origin check must never read).

The player meets Assembly as a physical act, never as a menu. Docked beside a dead ship's airlock, lit and holding a welder, they hold `E · Weld` for six seconds, standing still while the web may be listening; a four-pixel Hull Grey bead appears across the frame and two hulls become one body. Nothing else happens: a weld moves nothing, the hatch is still shut, the valves still closed, the breaker still open, and the seam weeps faintly through the area the Graph gives it. Making the graft live is the player's own later work (open the hatch, open the valve, close the breaker, each a lit Interaction verb with its own physical answer). With the cutter they can later cut the section away along that same seam, clean if they prepared it and ragged (a breach on both sides) if they did not; the cut leaves a cap plate or a ragged scar that never goes away. Carrying a salvaged scrubber or heater home, one press seats it into a pre-shaped hardpoint with a clunk, and the machine either turns or does not. The bead, the cap plate, the scar and the seated module are the player's visible history on the ship; none of them is ever a number.

Without this system the game's first promise has no verb: "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" is a sentence, derelict sections could only be stripped and abandoned, a salvaged module would have nowhere to go, and Pillar 5 (salvage integrates into the body that survives; scarcity pushes inward; loss costs ship and body, never knowledge) would have no ship-shaped record to point at. It is also the kernel's stress test (kernel Interactions row 5: "Atomicity (G2, CR-9), one notification batch, validate-then-apply") and the riskiest MVP build item (index risk PR-SCOPE), so it is the first spike.

**Index reconciliation.** The index carries this system under three numbers that all name the same row: bullet 13 of the Systems Enumeration, entry 9 of the Feature layer in the Dependency Map, and row 16 of the Recommended Design Order. The index wording ("Weld and cut sections as atomic one-tick commands across graph join and layout change: join validation is a pure Graph query; seams set edge properties; cap plates; detach into a new body. Also owns module install/uninstall ... as a graph mutation distinct from section welding") is honoured with five readings, each routed where it differs. (1) *Atomic one-tick*: the **completion** command is atomic in one tick; the player-facing act is a four-to-six-second channel followed by that tick (MSA-3). (2) *Seams set edge properties*: the Graph sets them (`seam_at_weld`); Assembly supplies `q` and the plan and cites the formulas. (3) *Cap plates*: a cap plate is Layout's presentation of a socket in state `CAPPED`; Assembly causes the state change and owns no cap-plate mutator (Layout has no `install_cap_plate`). (4) *Uninstall*: there is no uninstall command in MVP; V8 module salvage (Interaction) is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED` (MSA-28). (5) *Distinct from welding*: install is its own command family with its own apply (MSA-1).

**MVP slice.** One weld (the authored pair `sock_airlock_e` on the home-ship and `sock_b_w` on derelict B, offset `(696, 384)`), one player-facing seam cut (SEALED or RAGGED), and one install (the salvaged `mod_heater` into the vacant `hp_cargo_heater` that arms the staged PWR-OVL fault). The welded body holds the home-ship's 5 compartments plus derelict B's 5, which is 10 and within the Graph's "<= 12 compartments at the weld" (the loaded set `{home+B, A}` holds 14; whether LS-26's 12 bounds that set is the Graph's call, routed as an ASK, advisor R26). Both sockets go terminal after use, so MVP offers exactly one weld-then-cut cycle (MSA-25). *Scope note for the producer:* the concept's MVP item reads "One weld"; the neighbouring GDDs (Layout's slice, Body's detach, Derelict Authoring's staged faults) already assume the weld plus one cut plus one install, so the spike plan covers all three (see Open Questions).

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct, with the ship's answer as its consequence layer.** *(creative-director consulted; Framing A adopted, B kept as the second beat of the anchor moment, C rejected.)* The category is Gameplay, so the player *does* the fantasy with their hands. The Graph's indirect framing (the player feels the ship through its consequences) is the second beat here, not the first: the player performs the graft, and the ship answers in its own body (a draught, a weep, a creak). The grafter never gets a "success" from the UI; they get it from the room.

> *"I join a dead ship to ours with my own hands, and the seam I leave is the only thing on board that is ours."*

The home-ship is "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" (concept, Core Fantasy), and this system is the act of assembling it. A derelict section keeps everything it was: "A graft keeps its donor dialect in full; **the seam is the only thing that is the crew's**" (art bible 6.1). The player stands at a dead ship's airlock with a welder, lit and still and loud, and leaves a Hull Grey bead across the frame. That bead is the crew's signature on the ship. It is drawn wherever two sections meet, it ages from fresh to old, and it never goes away. If the section is later cut free, the crew's mark becomes a cap plate or a ragged scar, and that also never goes away.

Welding does not make the derelict *work*; it makes it *joinable*. When the bead closes, the hatch is still shut, the valves are still closed and the breaker is still open (Graph LS-15: "a weld moves nothing"). Making the graft live is a second, deliberate act, one lit verb at a time, and the ship answers each with something physical. The power fantasy is not "I built this". It is **"I chose what this ship is made of, and I chose when to let it in."**

**Aesthetics served** (concept ranking). *Fantasy* (rank 4, primary): "We are the only ones awake"; "a ship that is ours because we welded it". *Discovery* (rank 1): the weld is the payoff of the derelict's one visible unknown, and the player finds out what it does to the air. *Challenge* (rank 2): preparing a clean cut, welding under rising Attention, reading the witnesses before opening a seam. *Fellowship* (rank 3): the crew roles of boarder and ship-keeper meet at the seam, one welding lit and still while the other watches the dark or holds the far side, and the crew both witnesses and can veto a cut (the co-op beat, below). *Expression* (rank 7) is a Vertical Slice aspiration: MVP has exactly one socket pair, so "where" is not yet a choice, and when it becomes one it must stay functional, never cosmetic. *Submission* is deliberately absent: the weld is never a relaxing build loop.

**Autonomy, stated honestly.** The MVP weld is a **choiceless sequence by construction** (one socket pair, a fixed `q`, no rotation or mirror or free placement), and this GDD says so rather than inventing options; a quality slider or weld pattern would be a false choice and progression creep (MSA-42). The player's autonomy lives in three places: **whether** to weld at all (derelict B can be stripped by V8 salvage with no weld, and neither line dominates: welding brings power, O₂, fuel and five rooms plus an attack surface and a leak; stripping brings portable modules and costs salvage noise and open ends to cap), **when** (relative to Attention, because the channel is 360 ticks of tool noise while standing still), and **how clean a later cut will be** (preparing the seam with V4, V5 and V3 is lit physical work that earns a SEALED cut). Competence comes from reading the consequence channel; relatedness, in solo, from the seam as the previous inhabitants' hand and the player's own earlier self, and, in co-op, from the crew beat below.

**The co-op beat: one welds, one watches (Fellowship, CD-GDD-ALIGN).** *This is a fantasy statement and a validation target, not a mechanic: Assembly adds no co-op rule, role, bonus or shared channel.* The concept's two crew roles meet at a socket. The **boarder**, who went out to the derelict, stands lit and still at the airlock with the welder for six loud seconds, unable to see past their own beam; the **ship-keeper** stands a few tiles off at the edge of that beam, watching the dark behind the welder or holding the far side of the seam, with their own chest lamp and ears for whatever the weld is calling. The one who welds trusts the one who watches, and the watcher's job is to be the half of the crew that is not looking at the bead. The same trust runs through the cut: the crew **witnesses** a cut (the burn is loud, the hatch is a place two people can stand on either side of) and the crew **vetoes** it, because a crewmate who steps into the open hatch passage stops the cut (`CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-18, MSA-21). The seam is where the crew decides, in the room and without a menu, what the ship is made of. Under hallucination the beat deepens: the watcher's chest beam and the welder's sparks are the human channel (P1) and stay true, and a hallucinating crew learns to say "put your hand on the seam" before they trust a lamp.

### Anchor moments

**The weld (primary anchor), in two beats.**

*Beat 1, the join: domestic, not triumphant.* Once Web Map & Transit has established docking (no prompt appears until `docked` holds), the player stands at the airlock; their chest lamp is on the frame and the prompt reads `E · Weld`. They hold still. For `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (6 s) the welder spits non-emissive sparks at the hand point (never the chest point; the chest beam stays where they are looking), the linear bar with its needle-mark fills, and every accrued tick is tool noise the web hears. Moving stops it at any time; the partial-progress ledger keeps the work for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600. When the bar completes, a Hull Grey bead lies across the frame and two hulls are one body. **The bead is the spectacle**: there is no reward pop, no fanfare and no weld-completion `LOUD`. Nothing else changes: no air moves and no lights come on. The Pipeline's solver reset (`BODY_TRANSFER`) happens only for an actor standing on the absorbed (derelict) side and, with `docked` holding, moves nothing on screen.

*Beat 2, letting it in: the ship answers.* The closed hatch is now a door with two jambs and a leaf. Before touching it, the careful player listens and looks. If there is a pressure gradient across the closed leaf it creaks (the Graph's `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor, emitted by any closed opening under gradient; `WELD_DP_WARN` 300 dkPa is only what Assembly's cue may read as a boolean, not the creak's threshold); if the derelict is frozen, frost rims the jamb. Then they open the hatch and feel **the draught** cross the beam as the pressures meet; they open the O₂ valve and close the breaker, and B's dead lamps either flicker on or do not. From the step the seam exists it weeps, faintly, through `A_seam`; it weeps steadily for about 16.7 minutes of wear-in and then stops (LSG D.14: flat at 1 per tile until `leak_bp` reaches 0 at period 100), and until then it is the graft's first failure point and the art bible's hero shape #3. Beat 1 is *held breath* (exposed, still, loud on purpose, P3). Beat 2 is *earned calm* (art bible 2.3, "the only state the player should want to linger in"), reached only if the player chose right and was patient. *Honest risk (CD-GDD-ALIGN):* the calm leans on the weep wearing in, but LSG D.14 makes the weep flat and then stop abruptly, so the seam gives no gradual "settling" read; the promise that the weld proves itself in the room therefore needs a **playtest validation target**, not an assumption: players can tell an old, settled seam from a fresh one by the weep or the bead age alone (AC-MSA-127, Validation targets). The wear-in shape stays routed to the Graph and Failure & Trail (Open Question 11).

**The cut (secondary anchor): amputation is a choice you prepare.** At a welded seam with the cutter, a player who has *prepared* the seam (hatch shut, valves shut, breaker open, which is exactly how the weld left it) ends the `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (4 s) cut with one `LOUD` burn-through, a cap plate and "nothing beyond it". If they did not prepare, or a hallucination told them a valve was shut when it was open, the cut goes **RAGGED**: a breach on both sides, hull stubs, open ends hissing, and about 38 s to decompression in a 20-tile room (Graph LS-16, cited). The emotion is *resolve with a price*: the player decides what the ship loses and the ship keeps the scar. Socket states `CAPPED` and `CUT_OPEN` never return to `FREE` in MVP (Layout LY-13).

**The install (payoff anchor): the clunk, then waiting for the rotor.** The player has carried a salvaged module home at carry gait with rising Attention behind them. At the vacant hardpoint the prompt appears only because what they carry fits. One press, a heavy clunk, and then the real payoff, which Assembly does not own: "a running machine begins its loop only if the Graph runs it" (Item DB). The rotor turns or does not. The player learns the truth from the machine's moving part, not from a confirmation.

### Pillar alignment

- **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** (primary). *"Risk and reward map to depth in the web ... there is no safe place to farm forever."* The weld is the ship's history: a record, not an undo. Scars are terminal, a weld adds edges and mints nothing (Graph conservation), and a reinstalled store starts at stock 0 (Item DB IDB-18).
- **P1 The World Never Lies** (primary). *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth."* The weld's quality is told by the seam's weep, never by a number; the surge is told by the hatch's creak, never by a preview value.
- **P3 The Web Watches** (serves). *"The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention."* Six seconds lit and loud at a socket is the price of every graft.
- **P2 Dread Before Chaos** (serves). *"Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived."* A ragged cut is the player's own breach and its witnesses come before the cut; a system cut (VS) waits behind the 10-30 s tell.
- **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** (serves, as a constraint). *"No lie is unfalsifiable."* Assembly is a truth anchor, below.

**Tone and vocabulary.** Workmanlike, intimate, unglamorous: the register of a field technician (art bible 5.1), not an architect; the weld is surgery done in the dark (a metaphor for tone, not a pose requirement). Body and graft metaphors are welcome in language only: flesh and violet stay out of every Assembly visual (P1 sane palette). Player-facing words: *weld, seam, bead, graft; cut away, cap plate, scar, ragged; install, seat; the derelict's side; "the seam weeps / holds / creaks"*. Never: *build, construct, place, snap; demolish, refund, undo; upgrade; blueprint, layout, module grid; "seam integrity 92 %", "weld quality", "connected!"*. Prompt labels carry a verb only and never a state word (a label is a function of the static offer, never of module-body state).

### Design test

> **When a choice makes assembly more flexible vs. makes the seam more consequential → the seam. When a choice tells the player the weld is good vs. lets the weld prove itself in the room → let it prove itself.**

Corollaries the Detailed Design must satisfy (each already decided and cited, not re-ruled here): (1) `q` is never displayed anywhere a player can see it; its consequence is the weep and the strength of the bus. (2) A weld that fails to take after its channel *sputters and does not hold*, with no reason text and the same cue for every non-DONE outcome. (3) The cut mode is *earned* by lit preparation and never picked from a menu or a payload. (4) The preview shows only where the graft will go: no number, ring, percentage or "can't weld" icon, and an invalid candidate is simply not offered. (5) The weld finishes the join and hands the connection back to the player; it never opens a hatch, valve or breaker for them. (6) Every scar stays.

### Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks

MVP ships one hallucination class, (b) *false system states*; classes (a) phantom entities, (c) crewmates as horrors and (d) phantom salvage are Vertical Slice and recorded here so the rules do not change when they arrive. The seam is the place the crew trusts most. The bead geometry, the cap plate, the ragged edge, socket state and Layout's `welds[]` are **truth channel** and the Perception Filter never wraps them; the draught, weep, creak, frost and hiss are the Graph's **consequence channel** (closed list). What *can* lie is the **module-body channel**: a valve sprite, a breaker lever, a hardpoint's "empty" look, a gauge on B's wall. The experience target: *when my instruments disagree, I go back to the seam and put my hand on it.*

| Class | What the lie can do to Assembly's moments | What stays true | Cost of checking (P4: time, risk or trust) |
|---|---|---|---|
| (b) False system state (MVP) | A valve elsewhere on the ship renders **closed** when it is open; for **the seam's own valves and breaker** the lie is not drawn at all while the zero-gradient witness is unconfirmed (MSA-23 fallback: they are TRUTH at the seam), so the player expecting a clean cut is never misled by that sprite | The hiss at the seam, rim frost and `SEAL_STRAIN` are consequence channel; the seam-lane valve and breaker sprites are truth under the fallback; the cut mode is derived from true positions at apply (MSA-19) | **Time:** stand at the seam and listen or look before cutting. **Risk:** cut anyway and take a RAGGED breach that had a witness. The lie can cost the clean cut elsewhere; at the seam it can neither take it nor leave it without a witness. Once the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a zero-gradient lane witness the seam valve sprite may revert to corruptible and the hiss or lane state becomes the check |
| (b) False system state (MVP) | The hardpoint renders **empty** when a module is seated | The install prompt depends only on what the player carries, so the lie gains nothing from it; install resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` after the press | **Time:** a wasted walk at carry gait, not a free oracle |
| (b) False system state (MVP) | B's dead lamps render lit after the breaker closes | Lamp brightness is consequence channel; the beam reveals the real light radius | **Time:** step out of the own beam and look |
| (a) Phantom entity (VS-A) | Something "moves" on B's side while the player welds | Its footprint (sound, frost, hull stress) is P1/P2 truth; weld progress is never paused or faked by perception | **Risk:** finish the 6 s exposed, or step away and keep the ledger for 600 ticks |
| (c) Crewmate as horror (VS-B) | A crewmate welding beside the player renders as a horror | Their own beam and voice are the human channel (P1, inviolable); the sparks and the appearing bead are world truth | **Trust.** A cut is never a weapon; `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence in the hatch rectangles, not perceived identity, so a hallucinating player cannot seal a "monster" into a cap |
| (d) Phantom salvage (VS-A) | The "scrubber" carried home is garbage | The install offer is computed from the true carried def, so no prompt appears at the hardpoint | The trip home is a fallback reveal, not the only one (cheaper field checks are other owners'). ASK to the Filter: it may not fake or suppress an install prompt |

**No lie is unfalsifiable at the seam.** Every Assembly moment has at least one consequence-channel witness the player can reach before committing: before opening the hatch (creak, frost), before cutting (hiss, frost, creak) and after installing (the rotor).

### Reference techniques

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| *Hardspace: Shipbreaker* | Cuts only along pre-declared lines (our seams); cutting into a pressurised volume vents and that is the consequence (our RAGGED cut); depressurising first is the skill | Salvage grading, quotas and profit screens; free cutting anywhere; physics-toy fragmentation |
| *Alien: Isolation* (ion torch on doors) | A long, stationary, noisy tool hold that leaves you exposed while something may be listening | Its circular progress dial (art bible 7.3: a linear bar with a needle-mark, never a radial wipe) |
| *Project Zomboid* | Timed actions in a world that keeps running; movement cancels the action; "Death is a bitch. It happens." No invulnerability while channelling | Skill levels and build-quality numbers (`q` is data, never a skill); the right-click construction menu |
| *Barotrauma* | The welder as a tool you stand still with at exactly the place that matters; docking as a physical act whose hatch you open afterwards | Repair-progress percentages; the status-monitor overview |
| *Satisfactory* | Pre-shaped pieces that mate at defined points | The build menu, hologram ghost placement, rotation and blueprints |
| *FTL* | A module slot is a fixed place on a fixed ship; install is one decisive act | Abstract power bars and numeric stat upgrades; installing from a menu anywhere |
| *Oxygen Not Included* | Opening a door between two atmospheres is a visible event in the medium (our draught) | Overlay views of gas or pressure |
| *Pandorum* (film) | The grafted, mismatched ship as wrongness you live inside; working by chest lamp in the dark | n/a |
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The kindness of the lie, which applies to the seam only through the module-body channel; the seam itself never lies | Any lie touching the bead or the cap plate |
| *Space Engineers / Starbase* (counter-reference) | n/a | Merge blocks, free-angle ship design, voxel hulls: everything this game is not |

### What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails)

1. **Not a freeform builder.** No rotation, mirror, free placement, resizing, runtime sockets, blueprint export or import, or build mode (Layout LY-34). Welds occur only at pre-declared socket pairs; installs only at pre-declared hardpoints.
2. **No relocation verb.** No `remove_module` in MVP; V8 salvage is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED`. Moving a working machine always costs a salvage cut.
3. **No meter, readout or overlay.** No weld-quality bar, seam-health number, surge number in the preview, "connection established" panel or ship schematic. The only UI is the prompt plate, the linear bar and the in-world cursor.
4. **No progression from welding.** No `q` upgrades, tool tiers, weld skill, or `Σ q_mods` slot. Better ships come from what the player salvages.
5. **Not a combat game.** A cut is a ship operation, never a weapon or trap; no force, push or knockback at weld or cut (Body BDY-28). A cut never closes a seam on anyone: a system cut that cannot clear the hatch is abandoned, never forced.
6. **Not intentional PvP.** Assembly offers no verb whose only use is to harm a crewmate, no force, push or pin (rail 5), and the one way a cut can leave a crewmate on the leaving piece (a keep-side seam cut, MSA-18) is **counterable by the victim, never silent**: the channel is lit, still and noisy for 4 s (the tell), and a far-side crewmate who steps into the open hatch passage before completion blocks the cut outright (`MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-21, AC-MSA-123). This is stated honestly as a counterplay, not as the absence of a harm path: until Web Map & Transit ships a recovery path for stranded crew the stranding exists in MVP and Vertical Slice A under the user's "death is a bitch. It happens." ruling, and from Vertical Slice B it is **conditional**: if Transit ships no recovery path (and an audible far-side tell), `CUT_STRANDS` becomes a sanity-gated rule like P4(c) lethal friendly harm (MSA-18, Open Question 2, AC-MSA-124).
7. **No free reversal.** Scars are terminal; there is no re-weld of a used socket and no seam repair in MVP. The language says "history", never "undo".
8. **No celebration.** No fanfare, no weld-completion `LOUD` added for "feel", no screen shake beyond Body's `JOLT_PX` (default 0), no reward pop. The bead is the reward.
9. **No guarantee the graft is good.** A weld can bring a frozen, airless or dead section home, and opening it can hurt. The failure contract (P5) is the safety net, not Assembly.

### Validation targets (handed to the playtest gate)

At the MVP playtest gate (three or more outside players): at least two of three describe the weld in ownership language ("our ship", "I welded that") and none describe it as "building"; players pause at the closed seam hatch before opening it by their second session; no playtester asks "did the weld work?" after Beat 2 (after Beat 1 is acceptable and intended); a RAGGED cut is reported as "my fault, I should have closed the valve", not "the game killed me".

Three further targets added by CD-GDD-ALIGN (2026-10-26), all manual and all `[PLACEHOLDER]` thresholds: (1) **Fellowship (AC-MSA-128):** in co-op playtests (two seats), when one player welds the other is observed standing off the beam or holding the far side for the channel in at least 3 of 4 first welds without being told to, and at least half of the pairs describe the weld or the cut in "we" language ("we welded it", "I held the door"); a cut is described as "we decided", not as one player's act. (2) **Settled versus fresh (AC-MSA-127):** players can tell an old, settled seam from a fresh one by the weep or the bead age alone, with no HUD; the weep's flat-then-abrupt-stop wear-in (LSG D.14) is a P1 promise only if it reads, so a failure is routed to the Graph and Failure & Trail (Open Question 11), never answered with a number or an indicator. (3) **The veto is usable (AC-MSA-123, manual companion):** a far-side playtester told only that "someone is cutting the seam" opens the hatch and stands in the passage in time in at least 3 of 5 trials from a start within 8 tiles; fewer is a tuning input on `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and the far-side audibility ask (ASK AU1), and it feeds the Vertical Slice B `CUT_STRANDS` decision (MSA-18).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rule ids are `MSA-n`, collision-free across `design/gdd/`. Formulas are `D.n` in this document; a formula owned elsewhere is always prefixed with its owner (`LSG D.14` is the Graph's seam formula, `LY D.6` Layout's weld offset). Acceptance criteria are `AC-MSA-nn` and are pre-assigned below (the Acceptance Criteria section expands each to Given/When/Then and keeps these ids). Diagnostics are `MSA_*`. Assembly's reason codes are namespaced `MSA.<CODE>` under the kernel's `VALIDATION_FAILED(domain_code)`. Every new number carries `[PLACEHOLDER]` and a registry-candidate line (see *Names this GDD introduces*, end of this section). Neighbour rules are cited by rule id (`LS-15`, `LY-16`, `BDY-24`, `IA-14`), never by line number, because sibling GDDs are still being edited. "Advisor R<n>" names an orchestrator ruling that decided the point; "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" names a reconciliation made while authoring. Each rule gives **Rule**, **Criterion** (AC ids), **Pillar**, an *Alternative considered* line where a choice was made, and *Decides*.

#### A. Shape of the system

**MSA-1 — Five commands, three families.** *Rule:* Assembly registers exactly five command types (table T1). **Family W (weld):** `begin_weld` (PLAYER, CHANNEL) and `weld` (SYSTEM, the atomic completion). **Family C (seam cut):** `begin_seam_cut` (PLAYER, CHANNEL) and `seam_cut` (SYSTEM, the atomic completion). **Family I (install):** `install_module` (PLAYER, TAP). The `begin_*` commands open a channel and mutate no ship state; the completions are the atomic cross-block applies; install is a one-shot atomic apply. No other type is registered. `remove_module` is reserved and unregistered (MSA-28). The name `begin_cut` is Interaction's V8 module salvage and is never used here; `CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and `SALVAGE_*` are likewise never reused. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-01. *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar "not a freeform builder" (three pre-shaped verbs). *Decides:* advisor R1, R4, R5, R10, R27.

**T1 — Command types** (registry: `assembly_commands`).

| Command | Origin | Mode | Payload (primitives only) | Actor binding | Coalesce | `max_age_ticks` | Declared / registered | Reason set |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `begin_weld` | PLAYER | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (a `WELD_SOCKET` subject in `FREE`) | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` | `NONE` | `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` 120 (kernel, cited) | Interaction declares the row (IA-23); Assembly registers | `weld_ready`, IMMEDIATE half (T2); tool `tool_welder` |
| `begin_seam_cut` | PLAYER | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (a `WELD_SOCKET` subject in `WELDED`) | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` | `NONE` | 120 | declaration row requested of Interaction (ASK I1); Assembly registers | `seam_cut_ready`, IMMEDIATE half; tool `tool_cutter`; `CUT_BLOCKED` |
| `weld` | SYSTEM | atomic completion | `{actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause}`, `cause` = `CHANNEL` | explicit `actor` field (SYSTEM origin has no issuer binding) | `NONE` | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (MSA-6) | Assembly | `weld_ready` full; DEFERRED branch resolves to `BLOCKED` |
| `seam_cut` | SYSTEM | atomic completion | `{actor, seam_id, cause}`, `cause` in {`CHANNEL`, `CASCADE`} | explicit `actor` field | `NONE` | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 | Assembly | `can_detach` set, `CUT_BLOCKED` |
| `install_module` | PLAYER | TAP | `{hardpoint_ref}` | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` | `NONE` | 120 | declaration row requested of Interaction (ASK I1); Assembly registers | IMMEDIATE set only; DEFERRED branch resolves to `BLOCKED` |

All reasons are computed from true state and the kernel never filters them (CR-5). `allowed_origins` of `weld` and `seam_cut` is SYSTEM only (a channel completion or a cascade); tests submit them through a SYSTEM-origin double.

**MSA-2 — Assembly owns decisions, not mutations.** *Rule:* Assembly owns the five commands, the weld quality `q`, the channel durations, the offer predicates, the cut-mode derivation, the order of mutator calls inside each apply, `assembly.state`, `assembly.step`, and its reason codes. It owns no geometry (Layout), no seam edges, atmosphere or the formulas `cap_seam`, `hp_seam`, `A_seam` (Graph), no pose, velocity or actors (Body), no item vocabulary (Item DB), and no channel clock, abort set, partial-progress ledger or noise (Interaction). Every state change goes through a mutator the owning system publishes (kernel CR-9; H1, H3). Assembly writes exactly one block, its own. It adds no arithmetic of its own to the Graph's seam formulas: it supplies `q` and `L_seam` and cites `LSG D.14` and `LSG D.2` (D.4 below works them at MVP values). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-02 (review: no restated foreign arithmetic or value), AC-MSA-03 (lint: the only block Assembly writes is `assembly.state`; every other write is a published mutator). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R2; KRN H1, H3.

**MSA-3 — Weld and seam cut are two-phase: begin, accrue, handoff, completion.** *Rule:*

1. **Begin (P2 of tick `Tb`, PLAYER).** `begin_weld {target_ref}` or `begin_seam_cut {target_ref}`. `validate` runs the IMMEDIATE half of the predicate only (MSA-7, MSA-8). `apply` calls Interaction's proposed Class B `open_channel(seat, ASSEMBLY, verb_id, target_ref, duration_ticks)` and the Controller's proposed `set_channel_ref(actor, ASSEMBLY, channel_id)`, and mutates no ship state. Coalesce `NONE`.
2. **Accrue (P3, Interaction).** `interaction.step` runs the clock, the shared abort set, the lit recheck, the partial-progress ledger and the noise exactly as for its own verbs (MSA-30). Per IA-14, elapsed `e = T - Tb` and accrual happens at `e = 1..D`, so a fresh channel is DONE on the P3 of tick `Tb + D` (a channel resumed from the ledger is DONE `resume_offset` ticks sooner; the arithmetic below is for a fresh one).
3. **Handoff (P3 of tick `Tb + D`, `assembly.step`, ordered after `interaction.step`).** It reads the DONE handoff Interaction exposes in `channel_view` and re-runs the IMMEDIATE predicate. On a fail it writes `last_result = REJECTED(reason)` and submits nothing (the channel's cost is spent; the cause is world-visible). On a pass it writes `pending {kind, target_ref, submit_T}` and submits the SYSTEM-origin completion `weld {actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause CHANNEL}` or `seam_cut {actor, seam_id, cause CHANNEL}`.
4. **Completion (P2 of tick `Tb + D + 1`).** Authority, then `validate` proves everything by pure reads (MSA-7, MSA-14, MSA-20), then `apply` is total.

*Worked example (cited convention, weld at the Save/Load registry's tick):* a weld begins in P2 of `Tb` = 437039; accrual happens on ticks 437040..437399 (`D` = 360); the DONE handoff is in P3 of 437399; the completion applies in P2 of 437400. Because both channel durations are multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 (IA-14 lint), the channel spans a whole number of 6-tick blocks, but DONE need not fall on a Graph hook tick. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-04. *Pillar:* P1 (a state change during a six-second channel is re-validated, never assumed), P3. *Alternative considered:* a one-phase shape in which the hook calls M2/LM2/BM3 directly at channel end — rejected: the hook would skip `validate`, and "both verdicts must pass before apply" (Layout's `socket_plan` contract and its Interactions row) would have no home, so a defect would become FAULTED instead of a clean reject; a `begin_*` that is itself the command applied at press with the channel as a wind-up — rejected: the world would change before the work. *Decides:* advisor R10; KRN CR-9; LS-15 ("`can_join` is run at the command's `validate`, the same tick as apply").

*Origin caveat (load-bearing).* Hooks may submit SYSTEM-origin commands only (KRN CR-3), so every channel-completed `weld` and `seam_cut` reaches P2 as SYSTEM. Body's BDY-24 keys the hatch-cap rule on origin ("PLAYER-origin cut rejects, SYSTEM-origin cut defers"). This GDD reads that split as **`cause`**: a `CHANNEL` completion **rejects** with `CUT_BLOCKED` (at begin, at handoff and at completion); a `CASCADE` completion **defers** (MSA-22). The wording refresh is routed to the Body owner (CONFLICT C7).

**MSA-4 — `assembly.state` holds outcomes only: no progress, no history.** *Rule:* block id `assembly.state`; class `SIM`; `persist true`; `persist_scope WORLD`; scope `SHARED` with per-seat records keyed by **PlayerId** (never the transport peer id); `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`; `schema_version 1`; `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]`. Per seat it holds `pending {kind NONE|WELD|SEAM_CUT, target_ref, submit_T}` and `last_result {verb_id, target_ref, outcome, reason, T}`; the `[VS]`-reserved CASCADE deferral record (MSA-22) is the only other field. Schema in table T6. It stores **no channel progress** (that is Interaction's record, MSA-30) and **no world history** (that is Layout's `welds[]`: `welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T`, state). `post_restore` first lets the verifier read the as-restored block (INV-10 sees `pending` as saved, MSA-32) and then clears every seat's `pending` and `last_result`, mirroring IA-36 *(orchestrator ruling, QA-lead flag F-10: a tick-boundary snapshot in the handoff window still has the weld target `FREE` or the cut seam `WELDED`, because the apply is the P2 of the next tick, so verifier-first keeps INV-10 meaningful and never fails a legitimate save)*. 64-bit `T` values are held as two 32-bit integers or strings and never travel as JSON numbers (Save/Load SL-2). The block holds **no packed array** (at most four seats, plain keyed records), which removes the Godot 4.7 hazard that setting one element of a packed array no longer calls the property setter (breaking-changes.md) from Assembly altogether; dirty keys are still marked explicitly inside every Assembly mutator (the kernel rule). *Why `persist true` for a block cleared on restore:* `pending` is live across exactly one tick boundary (set in P3 of `T`, consumed in P2 of `T+1`), so a snapshot taken at that boundary contains it and the canonical state hash must cover it -> ADR candidate (could be `persist false` if the snapshot hash covers it otherwise). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-05 (schema, restore clears), AC-MSA-06 (lint: no packed-array typed field). *Pillar:* P1. *Alternative considered:* (B) no Assembly block, the channel record in `interaction.state` and Interaction's hook calling back into Assembly's cross-block mutators — rejected: it makes Interaction a second handler for Assembly's atomic apply (IA-23's own rejection); (C) the channel as Adapter-side only — rejected: PC-7 says progress is sim state, and MIRROR identity and abort-before-accrual would break. Assembly therefore owns a **minimal** block and the channel lives in Interaction's record (MSA-30). *Decides:* advisor R2, R12; TD-9. CONFLICT C2 (Save/Load row "Owns no block"), C3 (PCI "accrue in Assembly's block").

**MSA-5 — One hook, `assembly.step`, with two jobs.** *Rule:* `(hook_id assembly.step, owner Assembly, divisor 1, offset 0, after [interaction.step])`. It does exactly two things: (a) turn a DONE ASSEMBLY handoff into the completion command (MSA-3.3); (b) resolve a completion that was Rejected or Dropped by the TTL rule (MSA-6). It submits SYSTEM-origin commands only and writes only `assembly.state`. It iterates seats in ascending PlayerId, reads only `channel_view` and its own block, submits at most one SYSTEM command per seat per tick, and uses no wall-clock, no RNG and no dictionary-order dependence, so the same state always submits the same commands (what makes replay and MIRROR identity possible). It is O(seats <= 4) reads when idle. Divisor 1 because the handoff must follow DONE on the same tick. `interaction.step` must never name `assembly.step` in its own `after` (no cycle); an unknown `interaction.step` id fails boot (CR-3). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-07 (auto: deterministic output, ascending PlayerId, hook-order registered), AC-MSA-62 (lint/budget, MSA-39). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R2, R10, R12; TD-11.

**MSA-6 — The completion carries nothing derived; its life is bounded so `DONE` and `NOT_TAKEN` are mutually exclusive.** *Rule:* the completion payload is exactly `{actor, target, cause}` (target = the socket pair or `seam_id`): no `q`, no plan, no mode. `q` is read from the knob at apply, the plan is re-derived from Layout's `socket_plan`, the mode is derived (MSA-19). A payload that carries nothing derived stays replayable and gives a hallucination nothing to influence. `assembly.step` stores `submit_T` in `pending` when it submits. The completion's `apply` clears `pending` and writes `DONE` or `BLOCKED`. If `pending` is still set when `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS`, `assembly.step` writes the neutral outcome `NOT_TAKEN` and clears it (the true reason goes to the result event for DIAGNOSTIC and replay only; there is no reason text for the player). The two registered completion types set `max_age_ticks = PENDING_TTL_TICKS`.

*Why that makes the two outcomes exclusive by construction.* A hook-submitted command is stamped `queued_tick = submit_T`. At P2 of tick `submit_T + k` its age is `k`, and the kernel rejects `STALE` when age exceeds `max_age_ticks`. So the command can apply only while `k <= PENDING_TTL_TICKS`; at `k = PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` P2 rejects it `STALE` and the P3 of that same tick (after P2) is the first tick on which `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS` and `assembly.step` may write `NOT_TAKEN`. No late `Applied` can follow a `NOT_TAKEN`, and no `NOT_TAKEN` precedes an apply. Constraint (formula D.11): `PENDING_TTL_TICKS >= ceil(QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK)` = 8, which is the kernel's own never-stale invariant (`KRN D.7`: `max_age_ticks(any type) >= 8`), so a local command is never spuriously stale from queue depth; default 12, range 8-119 `[PLACEHOLDER]`. **Channel-floor constraint (formula D.11):** `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1 < min(WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS, SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS)` (13 < 240 at the defaults; this is why the range tops out at 119, well below the 240-tick channel floor). Its job is to make it impossible for a new handoff to meet an unresolved `pending`: a seat cannot reach the next DONE (at least 240 ticks after a new `begin_*`) before the previous completion has either applied or been resolved to `NOT_TAKEN` (at most `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` ticks after `submit_T`). As a second, independent guard, `pending.kind != NONE` is a `BUSY` term at site 1 only (MSA-7), so a `begin_*` pressed while a completion is unresolved is refused at the prompt rather than queued behind it. A rejection at admission (`QUEUE_FULL`) or in P2 leaves `pending` set, so the same TTL covers it; the cue arrives `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` = 13 ticks (about 0.22 s) after submission, which is acceptable for a neutral cue. *(orchestrator ruling: supersedes the wording of advisor R12, which wrote `NOT_TAKEN` when `pending` was "uncleared at T+1". Under the kernel's `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` truncation a legitimate completion can apply at T+2..T+8, so R12's wording could write `NOT_TAKEN` and then `DONE` for the same weld. The outcome name, neutrality and clearing behaviour of R12 are kept; only the trigger is TTL-based. The specialist proposal `MSA_COMPLETION_MAX_AGE_TICKS` 2 is rejected: it violates the kernel's `max_age_ticks >= 8` boot invariant.)* A sim hook has no confirmed path to command result events (CR-10: simulation systems never subscribe), so the rule reads only `assembly.state`, as Interaction's own door retry reads its `attempts[]`; whether a hook can read a result event at all, and the `issuer_peer` of hook-submitted commands, are ASKs to the kernel owner (ASK K1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-08 (auto: under an injected backlog of 1..8 ticks the result is `DONE` exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` never; a completion rejected at admission yields `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`), AC-MSA-09 (lint: payload fields; `max_age_ticks == PENDING_TTL_TICKS`; value within 8-119). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-6, TD-7, F-TD-1; advisor R10, R12; KRN CR-5, CR-13, CR-17, `KRN D.7`.

#### B. Predicates, reasons and the P4 boundary

**MSA-7 — One predicate per verb, three call sites, one reason order; the completion `validate` is the only safety.** *Rule:* `weld_ready(seat, socket)` and `seam_cut_ready(seat, socket)` are pure. Each is called at three sites: **site 1** the `begin_*` `validate` (PLAYER origin), **site 2** the handoff pre-check in `assembly.step`, **site 3** the completion `validate` (SYSTEM origin). The three sites share one body and one reason order (a lint); the only differences are the declared skips in T2a. Between sites arbitrary other commands can apply (a crewmate opens the hatch, salvages a lane node, welds the same socket), so sites 1 and 2 exist to avoid wasting a channel and to produce the world cue; **site 3 is authoritative** and the only site correctness relies on. Reason order, first failure wins: kernel authority -> `NO_TOOL` -> `OUT_OF_RANGE` -> `NOT_LIT` -> `BUSY` (at site 1 only: the seat already has a channel, **or `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for the seat**; MSA-6) -> `INVALID_TARGET` -> `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (Interaction's order, IA-10) -> Layout's `can_attach` reasons in Layout's order (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`) -> `NOT_DOCKED` (weld only) -> `can_join_static`'s nine structural reasons in the Graph's order (MSA-10). For the cut: kernel authority and the actor terms -> `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (wrong side, MSA-18) -> Layout's `can_detach` (`SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE`) -> `CUT_BLOCKED` (MSA-21). The DEFERRED slot is not part of any site (MSA-8).

**T2a — Predicate terms by site.**

| Term | Site 1 begin | Site 2 handoff | Site 3 completion `validate` | Why the skip |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Actor terms: `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE` | yes | yes | yes | cheap; the tool or light may have changed |
| `BUSY` (the seat has a channel, **or `pending.kind != NONE`**) | yes | no | no | after begin the seat's own channel is the one finishing, and at sites 2 and 3 `pending` is the very completion in flight, so the term would be self-blocking; it guards only a *new* begin (MSA-6 belt and braces) |
| Layout `can_attach` / `can_detach` | yes | yes | yes | |
| `NOT_DOCKED` (weld) | yes | yes | **no** | Body's BDY-27 (locked): weld legality takes no pose input and the *offer* is gated, so a kernel `validate` must not add a pose term; the handoff pre-check is not a kernel `validate`, so it re-checks and shrinks the loss window to one tick *(orchestrator ruling: reconciles advisor R12 "same body at three sites" with R17 "`NOT_DOCKED` is a backstop in begin" and BDY-27; the declared skip is the only exception)* |
| `can_join_static` | yes | yes | yes | |
| `CUT_BLOCKED` (cut) | yes | yes | yes | |
| full `can_join` (reads `PORT_ABSENT`, `surge[]`) | no | no | no | module-body state; evaluated only in the completion `apply` (MSA-8) |

*Criterion:* AC-MSA-10 (lint: identical bodies and reason order modulo T2a; site 3 marked authoritative). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R12; TD-3; Layout's `socket_plan` contract ("both verdicts must pass before apply").

**MSA-8 — IMMEDIATE reasons reject; DEFERRED reasons are outcomes after cost.** *Rule:* a reason is **IMMEDIATE** iff it depends only on geometry, light, the actor's own state, tool or stock, registry structure, pose or the consequence channel. IMMEDIATE reasons reject at the `begin_*` commands and are shown in the world (a missing prompt or the standard rejection cue). A reason is **DEFERRED** iff it depends on module-body state (`hp`, `state`, `forced_mode`, `served_pm`, a node being ABSENT, DESTROYED or SALVAGED). Two exist: `PORT_ABSENT` (weld) and `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (install). A DEFERRED reason is **never read by a PLAYER-origin `validate`** (MSA-9). In a completion `apply`, step A0 re-runs the full Graph `can_join` (weld) or `check_install_module` (install), SYSTEM-side. If the only failing verdict is DEFERRED, `apply` writes `last_result = BLOCKED(reason)`, clears `pending` and **mutates nothing else** (M2 or M5 is never called); both branches of `apply` are total, an Applied command with an outcome, not a fault, following the IA-12 / IA-24 precedent. The structural half (`can_join_static`) runs first in `validate`, so a DEFERRED verdict can never mask a structural failure (`PORT_ABSENT` precedes later structural reasons in the Graph's own order; a masked read would be lossy). Because `validate` and `apply` run contiguously in one P2 slot with no other command, hook or snapshot between them (CR-2, CR-9), the structural verdict at A0 equals the one `validate` computed; a difference is a defect (`MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`, debug assertion). `PORT_ABSENT` is reachable in MVP only through a salvaged def-backed lane node (`j_main` or `j_b_main`, both CABLE_JUNCTION); the bare duct junctions `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` are not salvageable (DA-19). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-11 (auto: `PORT_ABSENT` -> `BLOCKED`, nothing mutated, no `weld_join` call), AC-MSA-12 (auto: `SLOT_OCCUPIED` -> `BLOCKED`, instance stays carried). *Pillar:* P4. *Alternative considered:* treat every `can_join` reason as immediate — rejected: `PORT_ABSENT` would be a free oracle for a hallucinated "fixed/absent" module. *Decides:* advisor R12, R18; IA-10, IA-12; TD-1, TD-2.

**MSA-9 — The P4 boundary: IA-11 is adopted as Assembly's own numbered lint.** *Rule:* no `validate` of a PLAYER-origin Assembly command (`begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `install_module`) reads `node()` or `edge()` `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm`, or calls the full `can_join` or `check_install_module`. The existence of a prompt is a function of (object classes, tool in hand, `lit`, `in_reach`, `eligible`, registry and static data) only. Channel duration and bar length are identical regardless of state. No payload carries a believed state, direction or kind. Design test, quoted from IA-11: *"could a player learn a module-body truth from this at zero time, risk or trust?"* **One admitted exception, install's apply (MSA-27):** reading a node's `state` is allowed inside the TAP's `apply` because the prompt itself is state-independent and the attempt has already cost the carry (time, carry gait, carry noise); this is routed to Interaction as an extension of IA-11(a) for install only. **One kept oracle, `CUT_BLOCKED` (MSA-21):** it reads true actor presence, not module-body state, and is deliberately a truth channel. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-13 (lint: PLAYER-origin validate sources reference no full `can_join`, no `check_install_module`, no node-state accessor; only `can_join_static`). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R12, R18, R29.

**MSA-10 — The structural join query: `can_join_static` (Graph ASK, provisional).** *Rule:* the Graph publishes a second pure query `can_join_static(plan)` returning exactly the nine structural reasons of LS-15 (`SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY`) in the Graph's order, reading no node `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm` and computing no `surge[]`; the existing `can_join` is `can_join_static` plus `PORT_ABSENT` plus `surge[]`. `STABILITY` sums static template areas and `POWER_CYCLE` is wiring topology, so both are structural. Assembly calls `can_join_static` at all three sites; only the completion `apply` calls the full `can_join`, and the Graph's M2 `weld_join` has the full `can_join OK` as its own Class A precondition. Cost: `STABILITY` recomputes the Graph's D.12 sum for the two seam compartments; that is acceptable once per `begin_*` press and once per completion, and it must **not** run inside `weld_offer`, which Interaction evaluates every tick for a focused socket (MSA-12). *(provisional — Graph ASK open; this is the one name used for the ask that the systems-designer wrote as a "masked `can_join`" and the technical-director as `can_join_static`.)* *Fallback if the Graph refuses:* begin checks only Layout's verdicts and the Graph's structural reasons surface at the handoff as `REJECTED`, which costs the player the channel (a P2-fairness degradation, flagged). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-14 (auto: structural order; `PORT_ABSENT` never masks a structural reason). *Pillar:* P4, P2. *Decides:* advisor R12 (resolves its internal tension); TD X-2.

Reason table (T2; registry: `assembly_reasons`). "Sites" are 1 begin, 2 handoff, 3 completion `validate`, A the completion `apply`.

| Code (`MSA.` namespace for Assembly's own) | Class | Source | Sites | Cue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| kernel auth; `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `BUSY`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE` | IMMEDIATE | Kernel / Interaction (shared vocabulary); `BUSY` also fires when `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for the seat (MSA-6) | 1, 2, 3 (`BUSY` site 1 only, including the `pending` term) | shared prompt grammar; most appear as an absent prompt |
| `SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP` | IMMEDIATE | Layout `can_attach` | 1, 2, 3 | no prompt: an invalid candidate is simply not offered |
| `MSA.NOT_DOCKED` (new) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly, from Body `docked` | 1, 2 | no prompt (the offer is gated, MSA-12); backstop uses the standard rejection cue |
| the nine structural reasons, in LS-15 order: `SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY` | IMMEDIATE | Graph `can_join_static` (surfaced in the Graph's namespace, not `MSA.`) | 1, 2, 3 | standard rejection cue (structural, falsifiable in the world; in MVP these are authoring defects) |
| `SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` | IMMEDIATE | Layout `can_detach` | 1, 2, 3 | no prompt |
| `NOT_ELIGIBLE` for a cut from the wrong component (MSA-18) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly `seam_cut_offer` | offer; 1 | no prompt |
| `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (new, suggested by Body BDY-24) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly, from Body `actors_overlapping` | 1, 2, 3 | at begin the standard rejection cue; at handoff and completion the cut family's cue, the flame gutters and the seam holds; the crewmate in the hatch is the witness |
| `PORT_ABSENT` | **DEFERRED** | Graph full `can_join` | A only | "the bead sputters and does not hold"; no reason text |
| `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (install) | **DEFERRED** | Assembly: maps the Graph's unnamed occupancy failure of `check_install_module` (slot node not ABSENT or SALVAGED, LS-18 M5) to the outcome `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`; provisional until ASK G9 | A only | "the module bumps against the socket and stays in your hands" |
| `PORT_INCOMPAT` (install, **defect-class backstop**) | static, not DEFERRED: it fires only if `compatible_defs(h)` and the Graph disagree, which is a content or code defect | Graph `check_install_module` | A only (install `apply` step A1) | the same install bump; a debug build also raises a diagnostic |
| `NOT_TAKEN` | outcome | Assembly (TTL rule, MSA-6) | n/a | the verb family's one cue: weld sputter, cut "flame gutters and the seam holds", or the install bump (MSA-34) |

*Namespaces.* Layout's reasons surface as `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` (LY-29), the Graph's as its own, Body's as its own; owner reasons keep their owner's namespace. Only Assembly's own codes, `CUT_BLOCKED`, `NOT_DOCKED` and the install outcome `SLOT_OCCUPIED`, are `MSA.<CODE>`.

#### C. Weld

**MSA-11 — Host and mate selection: the home-ship side goes first.** *Rule:* the Graph's section `kind` (LS-17: HOME or DERELICT) decides the order. `socket_a` is the socket whose section has `kind` HOME; the other is `socket_b`. The seat may stand on either side and may focus either socket; Assembly derives the ordered pair, so the surviving body is always the home-ship's and the absorbed derelict body is re-based (this answers Layout's LY-16 host-rule clarification and Open Question 21: confirmed). The **mate** is the unique `FREE` socket `b` for which `can_attach(a, b)` passes **and** `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds. Two non-home bodies `[VS]` tie-break on the lower `body_id`; several mates `[VS]` tie-break on the lowest `(socket_key_a, socket_key_b)` (formula D.3). MVP has exactly one pair: `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W), `class_id` 1 (`SOCKET_CLASS` = DOCK_STD), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER lane 0 and O₂ lane 0, no COOLANT lane, one hatch each, weld offset `(696, 384)`. Assembly repeats the lane set and the offset as Derelict Authoring's must-repeat asks (DA-10). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-15. *Pillar:* P5 (the home-ship body survives). *Decides:* advisor R14; LY-16.

**MSA-12 — The player weld is offered only once the pair is docked.** *Rule:* `weld_offer(seat, socket)` is a pure read published by Assembly (formula D.2) and is the `eligible` predicate of Interaction's `begin_weld` declaration row: it is true iff the welder is the equipped tool, the seat is `lit` and `in_reach` of the socket, the socket is `FREE`, a unique mate passes `can_attach`, and `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds. No prompt exists unless the offer holds. `NOT_DOCKED` exists only as a backstop (T2). Web Map & Transit establishes `docked` (provisional contract); Body tests it; Assembly gates the *offer*, as BDY-27 asks ("the offer, not Body's `validate`, is gated"), so the player never sees "the ship jerking". Four consequences. (1) `weld_offer` reads only tool, light, reach, Layout socket state and Body `docked`; it never calls `can_join_static` or the full `can_join`, never reads a node or edge accessor and never reads `surge[]`, and it is a hot-path read (cached static half, O(1) `docked`; its per-call budget is `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US`, a placeholder in T9 whose value the spike sets). (2) `eligible` currently has one member, `actor_sane_gate` (IA-2), so adding `weld_offer` is an ASK to Interaction; the dependency runs Interaction -> Assembly read view while Assembly calls Interaction's mutators, so it is an injected read-view lookup, not a code import -> ADR candidate. (3) The channel lasts 360 ticks, so Transit could separate a pair during it: the mitigation order is (i) ASK to Transit — do not break `docked` for a pair while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open on it (readable from `channel_view`); (ii) the handoff pre-check re-tests `docked` (T2a), which closes all but one tick; (iii) the fallback BDY-27 itself names — the Pipeline's `BODY_TRANSFER` camera cut hides every non-zero `delta_abs`, in which case the offer drops the `docked` term. *(orchestrator ruling: the technical-director's option B, re-checking `docked` in the completion `validate`, is rejected because Body's locked text says weld legality takes no pose input; the handoff re-check is not a kernel `validate`, so it does not contradict it.)* (4) The visual cursor is Layout-supplied data drawn in world space (UI Requirements). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-16 (auto: offer false when not docked, no prompt), AC-MSA-17 (lint + spike: `weld_offer` source calls no `can_join*` or node accessor and meets its budget). *Pillar:* P1. *Alternative considered:* not gating and relying on the camera cut — kept as the named fallback only. *Decides:* advisor R17; BDY-27; TD-8, TD-16.

**MSA-13 — `q` is a data constant: never an input, never displayed.** *Rule:* `q = WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 850 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, loaded from the data file, range **400-990**, default 850 *(refines advisor R11, which ruled 400-1000: LSG D.2's Output Range says `A_seam` is 0 only when `q` = 1000 or `leak_bp` = 0, and the Graph's own `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` row calls "welds never weep" an R9 violation, so q 1000 would delete the weld's P1 tell)*. There is no player input, no modifier slot and no `Σ q_mods` term anywhere in the rules; the Vertical Slice options (a hold-longer trade, a weld-rod part) appear in Open Questions only, because a modifier slot in the rules invites progression creep. `q` goes into the plan handed to `weld_join` and the Graph stores it on the SeamRecord. `q` is never shown in any prompt, preview, tooltip or player-visible debug HUD; the Debug Overlay reads the true value. Its consequences are physical and come from the Graph, cited and never restated as Assembly's: `cap_seam` is 925 permille of the lesser port at q 850, `hp_seam` is 8500 (above `HP_OK` 7000), `leak_bp` starts at `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` 10000 unscaled, and the D.2 seam area `A_seam` gives the weep (`LSG D.14`, `LSG D.2`; worked in D.4). **Named cliffs for the knob.** Below 400, `hp_seam = q * 10` falls below `LEAK_HP` 4000 and every MVP weld would start leaking at birth, which breaks P2 fairness (a leak with no prior tell); data outside the range is clamped at **both** ends, to 400 or to 990, and logged `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`. **The upper cliff is q = 1000** (LSG D.2 Output Range): `A_seam` is 0, the seam has no weep, and the weld's own P1 tell vanishes; at q 990 the weep is still `ceil(16 x 10000 x 100 / 10^10)` = 1 per tile (worked in D.1). **The lower cliff is q < 400**, named above. Default 850 keeps the Graph's q-dependent paths exercised only at one value in play; the Graph side tests q 400 and 1000. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-18 (lint: clamp and diagnostic), AC-MSA-19 (lint: `q` reaches no player-visible string or UI path), AC-MSA-20 (review: no modifier term). *Pillar:* P1, anti-progression. *Alternative considered:* `q` from hold time, a consumed weld rod or tool condition — rejected for MVP (channel length would become player-variable, no such Item DB def exists, tools have no durability). *Decides:* advisor R11; `LSG D.14`.

**MSA-14 — The weld `apply` runs in one fixed order; every call has a named proof.** *Rule:* after the completion's `validate` passes and the A0 deferred check is clear, `apply` runs in this order (table T3): Graph M2 `weld_join(plan)` -> Layout LM2 `attach_section(socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T)` -> Body BM3 `rebase_on_attach(surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, frame_shift_px)` -> Interaction `sync_section(section_a)` and `sync_section(section_b)` -> `assembly.state` (clear `pending`, write `last_result = DONE`). The order Graph -> Layout -> Body is Body's (BDY-21): BM3 or BM4 before its Layout mutator is `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, a defect. Assembly allocates no id; the `seam_id` that LM2 needs is returned by M2 *(provisional — Graph ASK open, Layout Open Question 4: `weld_join` returns `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}`)*, and without that return the chain cannot be written (fallback: Assembly allocates, which advisor R6 rejects on H3 grounds). The apply is infallible *by proof*, not by hope: each call's precondition is a pure read already run in `validate`, and a call with no proving read is a FAULTED path and a design defect (TD-1). One P5 notification batch results, with the Graph's keys `T`, `S/<seam_id>`, `SEC/<a>`, `SEC/<b>` (and nothing else from the Graph) plus Layout's `BODY/` and `SEC/` keys for moved sections and Body's keys. Weld `apply` is integer arithmetic only: no RNG, no wall-clock (H9). The Graph's keyed draws (`rng_derive(world_seed, "graph.fail", (step_no << 20) | subject_id)`) are unchanged by a weld or a cut because a weld only appends ids and never renumbers (LS-9, LS-15), so replay identity across a weld needs no Assembly term. Budget: M2 alone <= 0.2 ms Release (locked, Graph); the whole weld `apply` is a placeholder budget measured in the spike (Tuning Knobs). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-21 (auto: recorded call trace equals the T3 order; a BM call before its Layout call raises `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`), AC-MSA-22 (auto: exact P5 batch keys), AC-MSA-23 (spike: budget), AC-MSA-24 (auto: a table-driven test enumerates T3 and asserts every apply call has a proof entry). *Pillar:* P1 (the world is never half-welded). *Decides:* advisor R6, R10; KRN CR-9; BDY-21.

**T3 — Weld apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof (pure read in `validate`, or total by contract) | Data in -> out | If the proof were wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority (SYSTEM allowed; explicit `actor`) | Kernel | CR-5 | -> | `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, Rejected |
| V2 | validate | `can_attach(socket_a, socket_b)` | Layout, pure | Layout's eight reasons | sockets -> verdict | Rejected `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` (LY-29); owner reasons keep their owner's namespace |
| V3 | validate | `socket_plan(a, b)`; add `q := WELD_Q_DEFAULT` | Layout, pure | returns `{pairs[], openings[], L_seam}` | -> plan | Rejected |
| V4 | validate | `can_join_static(plan)` | Graph, pure | nine structural reasons | plan -> verdict | Rejected |
| V5 | validate | `can_rebase_attach(...)` with its arguments **as Body publishes them** (Body's table states no arity; the surviving and absorbed body ids and the `frame_shift_px` of V3 are what Assembly holds to pass) | Body, pure | `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, and Body's optional `BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID` | -> OK | Rejected with Body's reason in Body's namespace |
| A0 | apply | full `can_join(plan)`: compare structural verdict with V4 (`MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`); read `PORT_ABSENT` | Graph, pure | same-slot guarantee (CR-2, CR-9) | -> deferred? | defect (debug) |
| A1 | apply | **if `PORT_ABSENT`**: `last_result = BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, clear `pending`, return | own block | total | -> | none; the one "applied but did nothing" branch |
| A2 | apply | M2 `weld_join(plan)` | Graph, Class A | V4 + A0 (full `can_join OK` is M2's own precondition) | plan -> `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}` *(provisional)* | FAULTED |
| A3 | apply | LM2 `attach_section(socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T)` | Layout, Class A | V2 plus the `seam_id` from A2 | -> `{surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` | FAULTED |
| A4 | apply | BM3 `rebase_on_attach(surviving, absorbed, frame_shift_px)` | Body, Class A | V5; the `frame_shift_px` of A3 equals Layout's pure prediction (`MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`, formula D.5) | -> actors on the absorbed body moved | FAULTED (`BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` if before A3) |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section(section_a)`, `sync_section(section_b)` | Interaction, Class B | total; fails dark on subject overflow, counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW` (MSA-16) | -> subjects re-registered | none |
| A6 | apply | `assembly.state`: clear `pending`, `last_result = DONE(WELD, seam_id, T)` | own block | total | -> | none |

**MSA-15 — A weld moves nothing; connecting is the player's later lit work.** *Rule:* the Graph creates the seam with **all** sub-edges disconnected: opening `area` 0, PIPE and DUCT valves CLOSED, CABLE breaker OPEN (LS-15). No pressure, temperature, charge or flow moves at the weld, onto a vacuum, frozen or dead section included. The weld's own P1 tell is the weep through `A_seam` from the step the seam exists. `can_join` warns and never rejects: it flags an opening whose `|dP|` exceeds `WELD_DP_WARN` 300 dkPa in `surge[]`. **no Assembly presenter reads `surge[]`**; the creak and rim frost are the Graph's own `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor (VA-16), and an optional audio sting may read `surge[].warn` as a boolean only, never formatting a magnitude; the Graph's lint (LSG AC) is repeated here as an Assembly lint over the weld-cue source. Assembly never opens a hatch, valve or breaker for the player at the weld (MSA-17). Connecting afterwards is Interaction's V4 (`set_opening_area`), V5 (`set_valve`) and V3 (`set_breaker`), each with its own surge read-out. Breach during the channel: the Graph does nothing special; when `weld_join` applies the seam opening starts at 0, so the breach cannot cross into the other section until the player opens the hatch, and the full `can_join` at apply step A0 reports the now-larger `surge` (LS-15 and the Graph's edge cases). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-25 (auto: a fresh seam is fully disconnected on the apply tick), AC-MSA-26 (auto: weld onto a vacuum, frozen and unpowered section moves nothing), AC-MSA-27 (lint: the weld-cue source reads `surge[].warn` only). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R23, R24; LS-15.

**MSA-16 — Weld and cut re-register Interaction's subjects.** *Rule:* both completion applies end with `sync_section` on both sections. A weld turns the HULL plate into a door with two jambs and a leaf (LY-15); without re-registration the seam door has no DOOR_FACE subject, the seam is unopenable and the Graph's "connecting is then three lit verbs" would be false. A cut turns the door back into a HULL plate and the subjects must be removed (and, for a RAGGED cut, `stubs[]` entries created for every Graph `open_end` not already recorded; MSA-20). `sync_section` is Class B, idempotent and total: it never refuses and never faults, so it is legal as a trailing call in an infallible apply; but it is **not safe**: at the subject cap an object fails dark (counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`), which for a seam door reads as a hallucinated dead door (a P4 breach by accident). The defence is a content lint, not a runtime reject: the post-weld loaded set must satisfy `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 plus `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 <= 144 with the seam's door faces counted (at most four subjects for the MVP pair; Interaction's worked example puts `{home+B}` at about 156 before player drops against a hard cap of 192, and the loaded set `{home+B, A}` at about 190, so the breach is "A also loaded", a Web Map and Authoring loading decision, not the weld). Asks to Interaction: `sync_section` marks only the subjects whose registered state changed (otherwise a weld could approach the `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 collapse in `interaction.state`); and IA-37's caller list gains weld and cut. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-28 (auto: after a weld the seam door has DOOR_FACE subjects; after a cut it has none), AC-MSA-29 (auto: subject count including the seam's door faces satisfies the lint, no `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`). *Pillar:* P1, P4. *Decides:* advisor R8; IA-37.

**MSA-17 — Calls Assembly does not make.** *Rule:* weld and cut call **no Emitter mutator**: both sections are already instantiated, E1 and E2 run only in instantiate and discard applies (Web Map's in MVP), and the Emitter rebuilds from Layout's `SEC/` and `BODY/` dirty keys through its `body_id` partition (EM-30). Assembly calls neither M13 `set_opening_area` (opening the seam hatch is Interaction's V4; a cut opens and closes nothing by itself, the Graph tombstones or holes the openings) nor M18 `apply_damage` (no impact side effect exists in MVP). It never calls `push_stimulus` (MSA-29), never calls Save/Load (MSA-33), and never calls the Graph's M4 `discard_section` or Layout's LM4 (MSA-24). **It calls none of Body's BM1 `place_actor`, BM2 `remove_actor`, BM5 `transfer_actor` or BM6 `set_body_velocity`:** actor placement, removal (despawn) and pod transfer belong to Web Map & Transit and respawn, and the detach default velocity 0 is BM4's own business (BDY-26, BDY-28). Assembly calls exactly two Body mutators, BM3 and BM4. Body's BM2 caller column currently reads "Transit, Assembly (despawn)": refresh owed under C7. It writes no perception state and reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (MSA-35). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-30 (lint: none of these calls appears in Assembly's source). *Pillar:* P1, P3. *Decides:* advisor R3, R8, R23. CONFLICTS C6, C8.

#### D. Seam cut

**MSA-18 — The seam cut is offered only from the component that keeps its body.** *Rule:* `seam_cut_offer(seat, socket)` (formula D.2) is true iff the cutter is the equipped tool, the seat is `lit` and `in_reach` of a `WELD_SOCKET` subject whose socket is `WELDED` on an `ACTIVE` seam, **and the seat's foot-origin section lies in the component that keeps its `body_id`** (the component containing the body's `origin_section_id`, Layout LY-17; in MVP the home-ship side). On the derelict side of a welded seam no cut prompt exists. This is a geometry-plus-own-position read, so it is IMMEDIATE and P4-safe; the begin-time backstop is the existing reason `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, deliberately **not** `CUT_BLOCKED`, which stays the clean "a human is in the passage" oracle (MSA-21). It needs a Layout read: ASK L1, a pure `detach_preview(seam_id) -> {keep_sections[], leave_sections[], moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` computing the same components as LM3 and `LY D.12` without mutating (if refused, Assembly would derive components itself from `welds[]`, a duplicate of Layout logic that is not recommended). *Why:* body transfer follows the actor's foot origin (BDY-23). A player who cuts from the derelict side ends alone on a new, sealed, drifting body with no free socket (both are terminal) and no pod: an unrecoverable, untelegraphed trap whose only recovery path (Web Map & Transit) is out of MVP scope (WM-23: stranded-crew recovery deferred to Vertical Slice A / B). A trap fails "no lie is unfalsifiable" and the flow principle that the cost of a failure be proportional to its frequency. *Cost:* the player cannot abandon the home-ship and keep the derelict; that play is a Vertical Slice decision with a Web Map recovery path (Open Questions). *(orchestrator ruling: adopts the systems-designer's keep-side rule and the game-designer's G6 option A; **rejects** the systems-designer's second clause, "no cut while anyone is on the leaving piece", as a **blanket gate** for MVP and Vertical Slice A. It is a far-side crewmate gate, which the game-designer's G8 and the binding decision "death is a bitch; it happens" exclude for those builds, and in solo play it is vacuous. The creative-director's `CUT_STRANDS` proposal is recorded as the Vertical Slice B question, now **conditional** (below). CD-GDD-ALIGN revision 2026-10-26: the ruling stands, but it is no longer written as an unqualified "there is no gate"; the victim's counterplay is a rule of this section and the Vertical Slice B outcome is tied to a recovery path.)*

**The victim's counterplay (rule, CD-GDD-ALIGN).** A keep-side cut can leave a crewmate on the leaving piece, so the victim is given a way to refuse it, using only rules this GDD already owns. (1) *The tell.* The cut channel is lit, still and loud: `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (4 s) of TOOL noise on every accrued tick and one `LOUD` at DONE (MSA-29). The closed leaf is solid and occluding (Layout's class table), so on the far side the flame is not seen; **the tell the victim gets is the sound of the burn through the hull** (ASK AU1 to Adaptive Audio and Failure & Trail: the seam-cut TOOL loop is a positional sound audible on both sides of a closed seam). (2) *The veto.* A closed leaf cannot be entered, so the far-side crewmate opens the hatch from their own side with the ordinary DOOR_FACE verb (V4) and steps into the passage. `actors_overlapping` over the seam's two hatch rectangles then lists them and the cut is rejected `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at completion (MSA-21, D.13): the crewmate is never sealed out, stranded or crushed, and the cutter has paid the 4 s and the noise. The reach is in budget: `V_WALK` 2 px per tick over 240 ticks is 480 px, 10 tiles of walking before the door-open tap, so a far-side seat within about 8 tiles of the hatch can arrive in time; a seat further away, downed, asleep or unaware cannot, and that residue is the honest remainder of the harm path. (3) *The cost of the veto.* Opening the hatch makes the seam unprepared, so **a victim who opens the hatch and does not stand in the passage turns the cut RAGGED** (the mode is re-derived at completion, MSA-19, E-20); the veto is stepping in, not merely opening. (4) *What it is not.* No sanity read, ownership check or pose oracle is added: `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence only (MSA-21). It is a veto available to the victim, not a gate on the cutter. Criteria: AC-MSA-123 (a far-side crewmate who reaches the hatch passage before completion always blocks the cut), AC-MSA-75 (the cut proceeds, with `abs` conserved, when no one is in the passage).

**The Vertical Slice B outcome is conditional on recovery (rule, CD-GDD-ALIGN).** "No gate" holds **only if** Web Map & Transit ships (i) a recovery path for crew stranded on a detached body, one that does not depend on the cutter's action (ASK T2), **and** (ii) the audible far-side tell of (1) is confirmed (ASK AU1). If either is missing when Transit's GDD is approved, `CUT_STRANDS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` is adopted as a **sanity-gated** rule of the same kind as P4(c) lethal friendly harm: a `CHANNEL` seam cut is rejected `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` at the **handoff and completion sites only** (never at begin, so probing costs the channel's time and noise and is not a free occupancy oracle) while another seat's foot origin lies in the leaving component, **unless the cutter's Sanity `harm_eligible` bit is set** (Sanity SN-10, SN-11, SN-13: system-gated by the actor's authoritative sanity, preceded by the victim tell, recorded in `incidents` and attributed to the web; ASK F2). The check reads true positions (`detach_preview.leave_sections[]` and Body's actor poses), never perceived identity, so a hallucinated crewmate does not change it (MSA-21's truth-oracle line applies). The trigger is Transit's design, not the playtest: Transit is designed after this GDD and its approval is the moment the condition is evaluated. Until then MVP and Vertical Slice A keep the ruling above, and the Vertical Slice exit checklist (Open Question 2) carries the open decision. Criterion: AC-MSA-124 (a design-gate check, `[VS]`).

*Criterion:* AC-MSA-31 (auto: no prompt on the derelict side, prompt on the home side). *Pillar:* P5, flow. *Alternative considered:* (B) allow either side with a diegetic tell — rejected: a new presentation system and the unrecoverable state remains; (C) allow either side and rely on the autosave — rejected: an unfair failure that the autosave hides and that teaches players cutting is dangerous. *Decides:* game-designer G6; advisor R4 (the cut is a derived, state-independent offer).

**MSA-19 — The cut mode is derived from the seam's true prepared state at apply, never chosen.** *Rule:* `cut_mode(seam)` (formula D.6) is **SEALED** iff, for this seam, every OPENING has `area == 0`, every PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valve is CLOSED and every CABLE breaker is OPEN (exactly as `weld_join` left it); **RAGGED** otherwise.
