The reads are positions, never health fields: completion is SYSTEM-origin and the channel cost has been paid, so the read is admitted (MSA-9). It uses a Graph read `seam_view(seam_id) -> {state, sub_edges[{kind, id}], leak_bp}` (the Graph's read set has no seam query today; ASK G3, provisional). No payload carries a mode and the prompt is the same in both cases (no "clean cut / burn through" toggle). The derived mode is passed identically to M3 and LM3. A RAGGED cut is EXOGENOUS and player-caused, so it is exempt from the fairness gate (LS-16): per former opening a virtual hole `A_CUT` 24 on each side as a hull stub of `hp` 2500 (severity 3 BREACH), fluid ports `open_end` leaking at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` until `cap_port` (M12, Interaction's `begin_cap`); decompression of a 20-tile room takes 382 steps (38 s), all cited from the Graph. A clean cut is earned by lit physical work (V4 close, V5, V3). The mode is re-derived at completion, so a hatch opened during a cancelled-and-resumed channel makes the cut RAGGED; the witnesses apply (MSA-23). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-32 (auto: the SEALED/RAGGED matrix over hatch, valve and breaker positions), AC-MSA-33 (lint: the derivation reads positions only). *Pillar:* P1, P4. *Alternative considered:* two commands `..._clean` / `..._ragged` with two prompts — rejected: a player-chosen "kind" in the command type; always SEALED in MVP — rejected: it removes the Graph's `A_CUT` and `open_end` consequences from the weld-and-cut story. *Decides:* advisor R15; IA-11(d).

**MSA-20 — The cut `apply` runs in one fixed order.** *Rule:* (table T4) Graph M3 `cut_detach(seam_id, boundary_mode)` -> Layout LM3 `detach_section(seam_id, boundary_mode)` -> Body BM4 `rebase_on_detach(old_body_id, new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px)` -> Interaction `sync_section` x2 -> `assembly.state`. One P5 batch with the Graph's `T`, `S/<seam_id>` and affected `C/<id>` keys (LS-16), plus Layout's and Body's keys. The detached body translates **without rotation** (`heading_q` 0, BDY-26 and the Pipeline's routed constraint) and its drift default is 0 (`JOLT_PX` 0, BDY-28). Detach follows BDY-23 (the foot-origin section picks the side; actors on the new component get `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px` and `sub := (0,0)`; `abs(actor) = world(body) + pos_curr` is conserved). **A weld followed by a cut of the same seam is not in general an identity on `pos_curr`**, so tests assert `abs` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`. For a RAGGED cut, `sync_section` must create `stubs[]` entries for every Graph `open_end` left and not already recorded (today only V8's apply creates stubs): without them a RAGGED cut's O₂-lane open ends could never be capped, a breach the player can never stop, which breaks P2 fairness (ASK I1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-34 (auto: call-order trace), AC-MSA-35 (auto: `heading_q` 0, no rotation; `abs` conserved). *Pillar:* P1, P2. *Decides:* advisor R8, R10, R21; BDY-23, BDY-26.

**T4 — Seam-cut apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof | Data | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority | Kernel | CR-5 | | Rejected |
| V2 | validate | `can_detach(seam_id)` | Layout, pure | `SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` | | Rejected |
| V3 | validate | `detach_preview(seam_id)` prediction of `moved_sections[]`, `frame_shift_px` (`LY D.12`) | Layout, pure *(ASK L1)* | pure formula | -> prediction | |
| V4 | validate | `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id)` | Body, pure | proves only that the old body is live in Layout (`BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`; BDY-21, Body's 3.5 table); the `moved_sections[]` / `frame_shift_px` proof is **not** Body's: it stays in V3 (`detach_preview`, ASK L1) and in the A4 `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT` assertion | -> OK | Rejected |
| V5 | validate | `actors_overlapping(body_id, [hatch_rect_a, hatch_rect_b])` empty | Body, pure | `LY-15` rectangles `width_tiles * 48` by 24 px | -> list | `CUT_BLOCKED` (CHANNEL); deferral (CASCADE) |
| V6 | validate (assert) | `body_count + 1 <= min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)` | Body, pure | MSA-41 | | debug assertion only |
| A1 | apply | derive `boundary_mode` (D.6) | Graph read (`seam_view`) | positions only | -> mode | |
| A2 | apply | M3 `cut_detach(seam_id, boundary_mode)` | Graph, Class A | V2; "cutting through a compartment" is unrepresentable (LS-16) | -> tombstones or holes | FAULTED |
| A3 | apply | LM3 `detach_section(seam_id, boundary_mode)` | Layout, Class A | V2, V3 | -> `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` | FAULTED |
| A4 | apply | BM4 `rebase_on_detach(old, new, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px)` | Body, Class A | V4; A3's `frame_shift_px` equals V3's (`MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`) | -> actors on `C_new` shifted | FAULTED (`BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` if before A3) |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section` x2 (door faces back to HULL; `stubs[]` for RAGGED open ends) | Interaction, Class B | total | | none |
| A6 | apply | `assembly.state`: clear `pending`, `last_result = DONE(SEAM_CUT, seam_id, mode, T)` | own block | total | | none |

**MSA-21 — `CUT_BLOCKED`: a CHANNEL cut rejects, a CASCADE cut defers; it is a kept oracle.** *Rule:* this is Body's named must-repeat 1 (the hatch-cap hazard, BDY-24). If `actors_overlapping(body_id, [the seam's two hatch rectangles])` is non-empty, a `CHANNEL` cut **rejects** with `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at begin, at handoff and at completion (MSA-7), and a `CASCADE` cut defers (MSA-22). The ship never closes a seam on a crewmate. Body adds no push-out (BDY-24 struck it). The handoff and completion cue (the seam-cut family's one post-channel non-DONE cue, MSA-34): *the flame gutters and the seam holds*, and the channel has ended; the crewmate in the hatch is the witness; the `LOUD` was already pushed at DONE by Interaction (MSA-29). **Kept oracle:** `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence in the hatch, not module-body state, so it passes IA-11; it is deliberately a truth channel for hallucination classes (a) and (c) (a phantom entity is not an actor, a real person is), costs one attempt and zero time, and the Perception Filter must never wrap it. It is also a griefing vector in co-op (a crewmate can stand in the hatch to veto a cut), recorded as a Vertical Slice B concern. **The cutter's own body.** `actors_overlapping` lists every actor including the cutter, and each rectangle is `width_tiles * 48` by 24 px (96 x 24 px for the MVP pair), `REACH_PX` is 72 and the foot box is `FOOT_BOX_W` 16 by `FOOT_BOX_H` 8. If the `WELD_SOCKET` subject's anchor sat inside a rectangle, the cutter's own body would block their own cut at all three sites, a defect no unit test with a stub actor would find. Required resolution (a): Layout and Interaction place the subject's anchor and reach so that a legal lit stance exists outside both rectangles with at least one foot-box half-extent of clearance, enforced by an authoring lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (ASK L2; the numeric anchor clearance for `WELD_SOCKET` subjects is not stated anywhere yet `[UNV]`). The alternative, an exclusion parameter on `actors_overlapping` (ASK to Body), is not preferred. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-36 (auto: reject at the three sites with a real second actor), AC-MSA-37 (spike: one actor at maximum legal reach starts a SEALED cut without `CUT_BLOCKED`; one foot-box into a rectangle fires it). *Pillar:* P4, P1, anti-pillars. *Decides:* advisor R10, R16; BDY-24; game-designer G5, G7.

**MSA-22 — The CASCADE (system) cut is specified and submitted by nothing in MVP. `[VS]`** *Rule:* `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` exists in the command table and its rules are specified and tested through a stub submitter; no MVP system submits one (Horror and Attention have no cut verb; the breach rhythm uses STRESS and M17, not detaches). It **defers** while `actors_overlapping` is non-empty for the seam's hatch rectangles; it is preceded by the Pillar 2 tell window that Assembly and Adaptive Audio own (the 10-30 s escalating tell) and by the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s fairness gate (ENDOGENOUS transitions only); it expires after `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` (a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, formula D.10). On expiry with an actor still in the hatch rectangles the cut is **abandoned, never forced** ("the ship will not close a seam on a crewmate", BDY-24). `assembly.state` gains the deferral record `{seam_id, first_deferred_T}` at Vertical Slice; the field is reserved in schema v1. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-38 (auto, stub submitter: defer, apply when clear, abandon on expiry). *Pillar:* P2. *Alternative considered:* a scripted system cut for the breach rhythm in MVP — rejected (scope; the tell is not built). *Decides:* advisor R16.

**MSA-23 — A cut's mode must be predictable from the world alone.** *Rule:* at any moment between "prompt offered" and "channel complete", a player who has looked at the seam within the last `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (a manual-AC parameter for AC-MSA-39, not a sim knob, and unrelated to `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS`) can tell from the world alone whether the cut will be SEALED or RAGGED. The witnesses are the consequence channel, owned elsewhere: a hatch not fully closed is visibly open with a draught across the threshold (Layout hatch presentation, Graph opening area); a closed hatch under a pressure gradient creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`); under a thermal gradient its rim frosts (`HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` 20000 mC); an open seam valve or a closed seam breaker must have a witness at the lane (a hiss from the open valve's descriptor, the junction's own powered state), **and that witness is a BLOCKING dependency, not a provisional ask** (CD-GDD-ALIGN): at zero gradient (equal pressure and temperature across an open seam valve, or a closed seam breaker with nothing to carry) there is no hiss, creak or frost, so a class-(b) lie on the valve or breaker sprite alone would make a RAGGED cut with no witness, failing P1, P2 and P4 together. **Fallback written now:** until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm in their GDDs a consequence-channel lane witness for each of those two states that exists at zero gradient (ASK G8 to the Graph, ASK FT1 to Failure & Trail), the Perception Filter **may not wrap the seam-lane valve and breaker sprites**: the sprites of the seam's own PIPE and DUCT valves and CABLE breaker are classed **TRUTH at the seam** (they render the true position, with no class-(b) lie and no violet gleam), while every other valve, breaker, lamp and gauge stays corruptible. The fallback lifts only when both owners confirm the witnesses and the Graph or Failure & Trail rule it in writing; it is then a content change to the class column of the Truth-channel table, with no change to this rule's shape. AC-MSA-126 covers the zero-gradient case; AC-MSA-39 cannot be signed off before it. An as-welded seam is **quiet** (no creak, frost or hiss). If a fresh weld could emit `SEAL_STRAIN` on a closed hatch with a latent gradient, the "quiet seam" witness would be false, which is why the first-connect beat needs a small or deliberate gradient (a verify handed to the Graph and Derelict Authoring owners). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-39 (manual: at least 4 of 5 first-time players who have closed the seam can say before pressing E whether the cut will be clean; no ragged cut is reported as "random"). *Pillar:* P1, P2. *Decides:* game-designer G9.

**MSA-24 — The detached piece stays as its own Layout body; Assembly never discards.** *Rule:* after a cut the piece keeps a Layout body and a Body pose row (an orphan if empty; Body's BDY-4 and its cut edge case). Unload, `discard_section` (M4 + LM4 + E2 + subject unregister) and the orphan body are Web Map & Transit's. Assembly adds no shake, impulse or push. Items lying in the piece are not Assembly's concern in MVP. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-40 (auto: the body persists after a cut; Assembly source calls neither M4 nor LM4). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R21.

**MSA-25 — Sockets are terminal.** *Rule:* a socket goes `FREE -> WELDED(seam_id) -> CAPPED` (SEALED cut) or `CUT_OPEN` (RAGGED cut) and **never back to `FREE`** in MVP (LY-13). Weld, then cut, then a second weld attempt on the same socket yields `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` in the world with no prompt; the playtest gate must not require a second weld. Illegal transitions: `FREE -> CAPPED`, `WELDED -> FREE`, `CAPPED -> WELDED`, any state to `FREE`. There is no `install_cap_plate` and no `set_socket_state` mutator (LY-27): a socket's state changes only inside LM2 and LM3, atomic with the Graph's weld and cut. The socket economy (one home socket, terminal scars, net cycle minus one) is **not solved here** and is recorded `[VS]` with Layout's three options (a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket keeping history; more home sockets; derelicts that bring three or more sockets) and the lint `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW`. **It is an explicit Vertical Slice GATE criterion, not an open question (CD-GDD-ALIGN):** MVP can play the core P5 loop (salvage integrates into the body that survives; deeper is richer) exactly once, which is acceptable for MVP only; the Vertical Slice may not close, and `/gate-check` may not pass it, until one option is chosen, authored and played through `VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 3 consecutive weld cycles with `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` silent (AC-MSA-125, Open Question 7), so that "net cycle minus one" cannot ship past the Vertical Slice and leave the assembly fantasy unrepeatable. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-41 (auto: weld, cut, second weld returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, no prompt). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R20, R22; LY-13.

#### E. Install

**MSA-26 — Install is Assembly's own TAP, with a carve-out to carrying.** *Rule:* `install_module {hardpoint_ref}` (PLAYER, TAP). The prompt exists iff the seat carries an instance whose def is in `compatible_defs(h)` for the focused HARDPOINT subject; that is static Item DB data, so it is IMMEDIATE, and an incompatible install is simply never offered (Item DB: "refused with `PORT_INCOMPAT`, never offered"). IMMEDIATE `validate`: kernel authority, carrying, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, not staggered or downed, and a `PORT_INCOMPAT` backstop. **CONFLICT against two locked GDDs (C1):** the Controller's PC-8 and Interaction's IA-28 both reject "every `interact` mode except `drop`" while carrying as `BUSY`. Carve-out sentence for the Controller owner (PC-8) and the Interaction owner (IA-28): *"while carrying, `interact` on a HARDPOINT subject whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def is admitted, alongside `drop`."* ASK to Interaction (ASK I1): a declaration row, object class HARDPOINT, `tool_required` NONE, a carry precondition, owner Assembly, `noise_class` QUIET. The `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` CHANNELS stay inadmissible while carrying (PC-7; a torch needs two hands). *Named fallback if the Controller owner refuses:* drop first, then install from LOOSE_WORLD with bare hands, which amends the Item DB's instance row 8 instead (source state LOOSE_WORLD). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-42 (auto: prompt iff the carried def is compatible), AC-MSA-43 (auto: every other `interact` mode is still `BUSY` while carrying). *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar. *Alternative considered:* (a) install as a `drop` onto a hardpoint — rejected: silently overloads Interaction's `drop` and a mis-drop installs; (c) drop-then-install — rejected as primary: the module lies loose between steps and the trophy is on the floor before the payoff. *Decides:* advisor R1.

**MSA-27 — Install `apply`: check first, then the chain (table T5).** *Rule:* `apply` first calls the pure Graph `check_install_module` (Item DB spelling). On a fail it writes `last_result = BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` (Assembly's outcome name for the Graph's unnamed "slot not ABSENT or SALVAGED" failure; ASK G9) (or `BLOCKED(PORT_INCOMPAT)` as a backstop), changes nothing else and the instance stays carried (IA-24's TAP-outcome precedent). On a pass, in order: Graph M5 `install_module(slot_node_id, def_id, hp_start := condition_bp)` -> Suit `release_carried(player_id)` (SE-13; "Suit never self-releases") -> Controller `clear_carry_ref(actor)` *(IA-36 proposed name, cited as proposed)* -> Interaction `sync_section(section_id)` -> `last_result = DONE`. The instance dissolves (Item DB row 8: CARRIED -> INSTALLED). A new store's stock is 0. Cap and decay-clock carry-through follows Item DB IDB-18, **provisional on the Graph's ruling (IDB Open Question 10)**: M5's signature has no cap or clock parameter, and adding a second mutator call in the same scope would add an unproven FAULTED row, so the AC is blocked and the gap is listed (ASK G5). No Emitter call (no `mod_lamp` install exists in MVP; an ASK if one is added). There is no sim seating timer: the seating cue and the install clunk are presentation, Assembly's by the Item DB. A running machine begins its loop only if the Graph runs it. The `BLOCKED` result is a free probe of slot occupancy in one direction (a false-system-state hallucination could show a slot empty), while the opposite direction costs an irreversible install; a short per-seat lockout after `BLOCKED` is proposed by the game-designer but would add state to `assembly.state`, so it is not adopted here (Open Questions). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-44 (auto: chain order), AC-MSA-45 (auto: `BLOCKED` leaves the instance carried and every block unchanged but `assembly.state`). *Pillar:* P5, P4 (state read admitted, MSA-9). *Decides:* advisor R18; IA-24; TD ledger 2.3.

**T5 — Install apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof | Data | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority (`OWNED_BY_ISSUER`) | Kernel | CR-5 | | Rejected |
| V2 | validate | IMMEDIATE only: carrying, `compatible_defs(h)` contains the carried def, reach, lit, not staggered | Suit / Item DB / Controller reads | static data | -> OK | Rejected |
| A0 | apply | read the carried instance's `condition_bp` and `def_id` before release | Suit read | carry proven in V2 | -> `hp_start` | |
| A1 | apply | `check_install_module(slot_node_id, def_id)` | Graph, pure | | -> OK / occupancy fail (mapped to `SLOT_OCCUPIED`) / `PORT_INCOMPAT` | on a fail: BLOCKED, return |
| A2 | apply | M5 `install_module(slot_node_id, def_id, hp_start)` | Graph, Class A | A1 | -> node installed | FAULTED |
| A3 | apply | Suit `release_carried(player_id)` | Suit, Class B | carry proven in V2; same slot | -> instance dissolves | FAULTED (cannot occur) |
| A4 | apply | Controller `clear_carry_ref(actor)` *(proposed name)* | Controller | | | |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section(section_id)` | Interaction, Class B | total | | none |
| A6 | apply | `last_result = DONE(INSTALL, T)` | own block | total | | none |

**MSA-28 — No `remove_module` in MVP.** *Rule:* V8 salvage (Interaction's `begin_cut` -> M6) is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED`. The name `remove_module` is reserved and left unregistered. *Why:* an "unbolt cleanly" verb is the relocation freedom the anti-pillar forbids and would open a second faucet beside salvage-once (IDB-17). Wording refreshes owed: the kernel's command-type example list and the Item DB's Assembly row. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-46 (lint: no `remove_module` registration). *Pillar:* anti-pillar, P5. *Decides:* advisor R5. CONFLICT C9.

#### F. Cross-cutting rules

**MSA-29 — Assembly pushes nothing to the web; Interaction pushes all weld and cut noise.** *Rule:* Assembly never calls `push_stimulus`. Because the channel record lives in Interaction's block (MSA-30), Interaction, as host of the channel, pushes **TOOL once per accrued tick** of `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` (the declaration rows carry `noise_class` TOOL, which IA-33 already lists for the welder and cutter) and **one `LOUD` at the DONE of `begin_seam_cut`**, pushed whether or not the completion later applies (the burn-through is loud either way; this needs the one-shot flag on the `begin_seam_cut` row, ASK I1). There is **no `LOUD` at weld completion**: a weld costs 360 TOOL ticks standing still and lit, and that is its P3 price. The earlier idea that Assembly must push its own TOOL ticks "or welding is free to the web" is withdrawn: under this hosting the noise is never free. Install is a TAP with a one-shot QUIET sound and no stimulus. Units (`U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD`) are Attention's. **Agreed with Attention (C5, closed 2026-10-27):** Attention E-44 and AC-AT-72 now assert zero `LOUD` at weld completion; Interaction's IA-33 declares `LOUD` at cut completion, vent apply and throw landing, not at weld completion. Both sides already agree on the part that binds Assembly (Assembly pushes nothing: Attention AT-3 and AC-AT-95 refuse any `push_stimulus` caller other than the Controller and Interaction; Horror Entities pushes no noise and is not a declared caller, HE-36). Attention's must-repeat ("which events push, without duplicating Interaction's `LOUD` at cut completion") is answered by this rule. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-47 (lint: no `push_stimulus` call in Assembly source, mirroring AC-AT-95), AC-MSA-48 (auto, with an Interaction double: weld = D TOOL pushes and zero `LOUD`; cut = D TOOL pushes and exactly one `LOUD` at DONE). *Pillar:* P3. *Decides:* advisor R3; IA-33; AT-3.

**MSA-30 — Durations, abort and partial progress belong to the channel record in Interaction's block.** *Rule:* the weld and seam-cut channel is an IA-14 record in `interaction.state` with `owner_system = ASSEMBLY`. `interaction.step` runs the clock, the shared abort set (IA-15, closed: `MOVED`, `CANCELLED`, `UNLIT`, `OUT_OF_REACH`, `TARGET_GONE`, `BODY`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `RESTORE`), the lit recheck every `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` 6, the partial-progress ledger (IA-16, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600) and the noise (MSA-29), exactly as for its own verbs; Assembly runs no clock of its own, because a second copy of the IA-14 clock would drift (the "kernel row guessed consumer internals" failure pattern) and the one-channel-per-seat rule (PC-7) is enforceable only inside one block. Durations: `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (6 s, range 240-480) and `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (4 s, range 240-480), both `[PLACEHOLDER]` feel values from the Controller's Open Question 7, both multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 (the IA I-2 lint: 60 and 40 blocks), both in the LONG bar class (`BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240, so the floor is 240, not lower). The ceiling 480 is set by analogy with IA I-9 (which bounds Interaction's own LONG bar at twice `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`), cited as an analogy, not a lock on Assembly. `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and Interaction's `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` happen to share 240 at MVP but are distinct names (the Controller's "cut 240" is ambiguous between them). The cut is deliberately faster than the weld: both verbs are terminal on their socket, so neither is a repeatable exploit, and the cut is the P5 escape verb paid for by a `LOUD` completion and a possible breach; revisit if players use it as a free panic button. Duration never depends on any state (IA-11c); movement cancels (PC-7: "I can leave now"). `abort_channel(actor)` (IA-35's proposed Assembly mutator) is **refused**: `cancel_channel` already ends an ASSEMBLY-owned record held in `interaction.state`, so Assembly publishes no cancel. Asks to Interaction (all under ASK I1; numbering as in T15): I1(i) a third Class B mutator `open_channel(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref, duration_ticks)` called by the two `begin_*` applies (IA-1 says Interaction publishes "exactly two" Class B mutators, so this is a refresh); I1(ii) `channel_view` exposes, for the tick on which it happens, a DONE handoff `(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref)` for records with `owner_system` other than INTERACTION, and at that DONE Interaction applies nothing itself except to clear `channel_ref` through the Controller mutator (IA-14 row 9) and delete any ledger entry; I1(iii) `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` join IA-16's list of non-incremental channels; I1(viii), optional: for ASSEMBLY-owned records the NOT_ELIGIBLE abort re-tests the owner's `eligible` predicate at `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS`, so a lost `docked` aborts the channel (optional; it would complement Transit's hold). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-49 (lint: both durations multiples of 6 within 240-480), AC-MSA-50 (auto: abort resumes from the ledger within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` and not after). *Pillar:* P3, P2. *Decides:* advisor R2, R13; IA-14, IA-15, IA-16, IA-35, PC-7. CONFLICTS C3, C4.

**MSA-31 — Atomicity, the failure model and what a defect costs.** *Rule:* each completion `apply` and the install `apply` is atomic against every other command, hook and snapshot (CR-9), followed by one P5 notification batch per block (CR-10). Dirty keys are marked explicitly in every mutator (Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes call no setter). `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 is not approached if `sync_section` marks only changed subjects (formula D.12). The failure model is **validate-then-apply, no rollback**: `validate` proves everything through read-only queries, `apply` is infallible by contract, and a failure inside `apply` is a defect that moves the kernel `RUNNING -> FAULTED`, drops the pending queue with `Dropped(FAULTED)`, never writes or autosaves over an existing save, and in a development build halts with diagnostics. The only exits are an explicit user `request_restore` or shutdown. **This is not Save/Load's MVP death rule** (SL-7, restart from the last autosave), which is a separate mechanism with the same loading path; the two must not be conflated. The price of "no rollback", stated plainly: the loss window for an Assembly defect (`fault_loss_ticks`, D.16) is up to 18000 ticks (5 minutes of play, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10800 + `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200) worst case, 10800 typical, after the player chooses to load, because weld and cut completions become autosave *events* only once Save/Load enables events (MSA-33). No pre-weld checkpoint is added: autosave is soft-gated while `CHANNELLING` (SL-5) and a checkpoint would be a second transaction. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-51 (auto, fault injection: a stub mutator that throws inside A3 and A4, and a forced verifier failure, each leave the kernel FAULTED with the command `Dropped(FAULTED)`, the existing save untouched, and `request_restore` returning a state-hash-equal world). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* KRN CR-9, CR-10, CR-17, CR-18; advisor R25.

**MSA-32 — A read-only cross-block integrity verifier is the compensating control for "no rollback".** *Rule:* the Graph protects itself (LS-24), Layout and Body each assert their own tables, but nobody owns a check that the three agree, and the three-owner split is exactly what makes each owner's check blind to the others' disagreement. Assembly therefore owns a **read-only verifier** of the **cross-owner** invariants in T7: INV-1 to INV-7 and INV-10, eight in all. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection (T7's former INV-8 and INV-9) are Graph-internal invariants the Graph already asserts itself (LS-24), so the verifier does not re-own them; they appear as AC cross-checks that cite LS-24 (AC-MSA-25 for the fresh seam, AC-MSA-67 for conservation). It reads only `SIM` views of Layout, Graph, Body and Interaction (never a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, CR-12), changes nothing, and reports violation ids. Run policy: in `assembly.state`'s `post_restore` (which runs the verifier first and clears the seat records afterwards, MSA-4, and runs after `layout.state`, `graph.state`, `interaction.state` and `body.state` because of `restore_after`) all eight, **hard**: a violation fails the restore: LOADING -> FAULTED naming `assembly.state` and the INV id (kernel edge case on `post_restore` failure; whole-block replace means the pre-restore state is not intact); the save file is not modified and the player may choose another restore; never silently continued; after every Assembly apply in debug and CI builds, all eight, as an assertion; in release builds after each weld or cut apply, INV-1, INV-2 and INV-7 only (O(seams), at most 48 given `MAX_SECTIONS` 24 and `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` 4), a violation moving the kernel to FAULTED naming the invariant id, which is strictly better than playing on in a corrupt state. -> ADR candidate: where cross-block invariants live (Assembly or a kernel integrity service). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-52 (auto: each INV fixture violation reports exactly its id), AC-MSA-53 (auto: a save with an ACTIVE `welds[]` row and no Graph SeamRecord fails restore: the kernel is FAULTED with the block id and the INV id in the diagnostic, no tick runs, and the save file bytes are unchanged). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-14.

**T7 — Cross-block invariants checked by the verifier** (registry: `assembly_verifier_invariants`).

| Id | Invariant | Reads | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| INV-1 | Each Graph SeamRecord `ACTIVE` <-> exactly one Layout `welds[]` row `ACTIVE` with the same `seam_id`, both sockets `WELDED(seam_id)` | Graph seams, Layout welds and sockets | O(seams) |
| INV-2 | Each `welds[]` row `CUT_SEALED` / `CUT_RAGGED` <-> Graph SeamRecord `CUT` with the same boundary mode; sockets `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` respectively | same | O(seams) |
| INV-3 | Each body's section set equals the connected component over `ACTIVE` welds; the weld relation is a forest (LY-14: `SAME_BODY` is a hard reject, so a weld can never close a ring) | Layout | O(sections) |
| INV-4 | Every Layout body has exactly one Body pose row; a `DISSOLVED` body has none | Layout, Body | O(bodies) |
| INV-5 | Every actor's `body_id` exists in Layout and its `pos_curr` lies in that body's floor area, or `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION` is counted (a defect-only counter, BDY-23) | Body, Layout | O(actors <= `MAX_ACTORS` 8) |
| INV-6 | `body_count <= min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)` (MSA-41) | Body | O(1) |
| INV-7 | Id monotonicity: every `seam_id` and `body_id` in any block is below the owner's `next_*`; none reused | Graph, Layout | O(seams + bodies) |
| *(INV-8)* | *Moved out of the verifier: conservation on the apply tick (weld = node count unchanged, edge count up by exactly the plan's edge count; cut = node count unchanged, tombstones equal the former seam sub-edges) is Graph-internal and already asserted by LS-24. Kept as an AC cross-check citing LS-24 (AC-MSA-67).* | Graph counters | n/a |
| *(INV-9)* | *Moved out of the verifier: a fresh seam's sub-edges are all disconnected on the apply tick (opening area 0, valves closed, breakers open) is asserted by the Graph (LS-24). Kept as an AC cross-check (AC-MSA-25).* | Graph | n/a |
| INV-10 | `assembly.state.pending` target exists and is `FREE` (weld) / `WELDED` (cut); `last_result.outcome` in range | Layout, own block | O(seats <= 4) |

**MSA-33 — The autosave event is a derived read.** *Rule:* Assembly publishes the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` (formula D.9): the maximum over Layout's `welds[]` of `welded_at_T` and `cut_at_T`, or NONE. Save/Load polls it at tick boundaries (SL-13); Layout's `S/<seam_id>` key in the P5 batch is the notification. The event names are fixed as `ASSEMBLY_WELD` (the maximum is a `welded_at_T`) and `ASSEMBLY_CUT` (the maximum is a `cut_at_T`), answering Save/Load Open Question 24 for Assembly *(fixed 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*; enabling them is Save/Load's, and the MVP baseline stays interval plus QUIT plus initial until Save/Load enables events. The 64-bit `T` values never travel as JSON numbers (SL-2). **Assembly never calls Save/Load.** **No Graph gate covers a RAGGED cut:** SL-5 soft row (a) counts only `ENDOGENOUS` NODE/EDGE/SEAM descriptors, and a RAGGED cut's hull stubs are `EXOGENOUS` and player-caused (LS-16, "no gate"), so an autosave **may land during a self-inflicted RAGGED decompression**; the hard DOWNED/DEAD gate (SL-5) still applies. This is accepted: the cut's witnesses came before the player pressed, and a restore there returns the player to the post-cut breach with its stubs to cap, not to a lost run. Optional ASK S1(b) to Save/Load: treat an open `cut_at_T` RAGGED window (a RAGGED seam whose stubs are uncapped) as a soft-gate row, if playtests show players restoring into an unwinnable breach. (This corrects advisor R19, which assumed the gate.) The one-tick window between the handoff (`pending` set in P3 of `T`) and the apply (P2 of `T+1`) is not covered by the channelling gate; a snapshot there contains `pending`, a restore clears it, and the player redoes the six seconds. Closing it is an optional ASK to Save/Load (ASK S1): defer the autosave while `pending_any()` is true (one name: `pending_any()`). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-54 (auto: `last_shape_change_T()` after a weld and a cut; two 32-bit parts or a string at the serialisation boundary). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R19; KRN H3 (one source of truth for history is Layout's `welds[]`).

**MSA-34 — Outcome presentation grammar: one cue, no reason text, the world is the explanation.** *Rule:* REJECTED at begin uses the shared prompt grammar (no prompt, or the standard rejection cue). Every **post-channel non-DONE outcome** (a handoff `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`, `NOT_TAKEN`) shares **one** cue per verb family and carries no reason text (registry: `seam_cue_vocabulary`): weld, *the bead sputters and does not hold*; seam cut, *the flame gutters and the seam holds*; install, *the module bumps against the socket and stays in your hands*. (The cut never takes the weld's sputter: the cut's bead is already laid, and the cut is the verb that fails to *separate* it.) The reason code is in the result event for DIAGNOSTIC and replay only. A distinct cue per reason would be the reason text P4 forbids; the cost is that the player cannot tell `PORT_ABSENT` from `NOT_TAKEN` by the cue alone, which is acceptable because both are rare in MVP and the world answers within one walk. Every non-DONE outcome has a world witness reachable within about 60 s: `PORT_ABSENT` leaves the lane node's hardpoint footprint visible as an empty bay (Layout keeps a MACHINE footprint after salvage, LY-20); `SLOT_OCCUPIED` shows the occupying module at the bay; `NOT_TAKEN` means a world fact changed during the channel. A presenter plays an outcome cue only if `last_result.T` differs from the last value it saw and lies within `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` of the current `T`; `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so a restore never replays a cue and a MIRROR join never replays a stale one. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-55 (review and manual: the cue is identical across the three non-DONE outcomes, none reads as "it worked", the witness is reachable in about 60 s), AC-MSA-56 (auto: `last_result` is consumed once). *Pillar:* P4, P1. *Decides:* advisor R12, R24; TD-13; game-designer G3, G4.

**MSA-35 — The perception boundary.** *Rule:* Assembly reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (CR-12) and writes no perception state; every Assembly read has no perceived variant; Assembly's presentation never consumes `surge[]` magnitudes, `q`, `leak_bp` or `hp`. A phantom seam or a false "welded" state may exist only in the Perception Filter's corruptible presentation layer; it never reaches `welds[]`, the consequence channel or the bead geometry. Seam geometry, bead, cap plate and socket state are truth channel (an ASK to the Filter repeating LY-34), the seam's own lane valve and breaker sprites are truth channel under the MSA-23 fallback until the zero-gradient witness is confirmed (the same ASK to the Filter, F1), and `CUT_BLOCKED` is a kept truth oracle (MSA-21). No flesh or violet appears anywhere in Assembly's presentation. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-57 (lint: Assembly's source references no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block id). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R24, R29; LY-34.

**MSA-36 — Scope guards.** *Rule:* no hull-plate replacement (Interaction's FULL repair once the Graph rules, IDB Open Question 11), no seam repair (M7 on a SEAM is IDB Open Question 12; a seam is restored by a re-weld, never for free), no re-weld of a used socket, no discard, no unload, no rotation, mirror, free placement or resizing, no `remove_module`, no `q` upgrades or tool tiers, no cut as a weapon, no player-placed lamps. The only plate Assembly ever causes is the cap plate, which is Layout's presentation of `CAPPED`. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-58 (review). *Pillar:* anti-pillars. *Decides:* advisor R22.

**MSA-37 — Co-op seats.** *Rule:* up to four seats; each has its own channel record, `pending` and `last_result`. **The same socket gives `BUSY` at begin:** IA-34 makes contention one job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`, and WELD is a verb class, so a second seat's `begin_weld` on a socket already being welded is an immediate `BUSY`; two seats channelling the *same* socket cannot happen. Channels do not stack or shorten. **A double DONE is reachable only with two seats on the two different sockets of one pair** (MSA-11 lets either socket of the pair be focused, and IA-34's key is the focused subject, not the derived pair). Each pays its full `D` and both emit TOOL noise. If both reach DONE on the same tick, `assembly.step` submits two SYSTEM completions in ascending PlayerId order, the first applies, and the second's `validate` fails `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` (the pair's sockets are already `WELDED`), producing `NOT_TAKEN` for that seat (not an error, not a fault). If they reach DONE on consecutive ticks, the second seat's handoff pre-check fails first with `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` and `REJECTED` is written, or, if the first completion has been submitted but not yet applied, the second completion's `validate` fails and it resolves as `NOT_TAKEN`. **Two seats on the same seam cut:** the second `begin_seam_cut` is `BUSY` (IA-34, class WELD, ASK I1(iv)); on the two sockets of one pair the second completion fails `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` and resolves `NOT_TAKEN`. Optional ASK I1(x): key WELD contention on the *host socket of the derived pair*, which would turn the second-socket case into an immediate `BUSY` too and make the double-DONE case unreachable. `assembly.state` is replicated as `SHARED_SUMMARY`; a crewmate's weld in progress is visible through Interaction's replicated channel record (the human channel, P1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-59 (auto: two seats, same tick and consecutive ticks, exactly one `DONE` and a single welded state). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-12; IA-34.

**MSA-38 — Restore and pause.** *Rule:* a restore drops every channel (abort cause `RESTORE`, IA-36) and clears the seat records in `assembly.state`; nothing is half-applied because the apply is atomic and a snapshot is taken only at a tick boundary. Completions are never replayed from a save. While the simulation is paused no tick advances (no P3, so no hook submits), the TTL and the ledger are in ticks and stop, and resume continues as if there had been no pause. For `persist` blocks, restore-then-continue equals uninterrupted play (kernel G4) at every tick where no ASSEMBLY channel record is open and no `pending` is set; the permitted differences are asserted explicitly rather than hidden: while a channel is open the restore drops the channel record and its ledger entry (abort cause `RESTORE`, E-29), and at the handoff-to-apply tick the restore clears `pending` (E-30). *(orchestrator ruling, QA-lead flag F-1: the earlier wording "except the handoff-to-apply tick" was contradicted by E-29.)* *Criterion:* AC-MSA-60 (auto: restore mid-channel leaves no channel, no `pending`, no `last_result`, no orphan, no cue), AC-MSA-61 (auto: snapshot at every tick of a weld-and-cut script, restore, continue; hash-equal except the asserted window). *Pillar:* P1, P5. *Decides:* IA-36; SL-5; KRN G4.

**MSA-39 — No presentation work inside the sim apply; budgets are spike-measured.** *Rule:* a weld or cut apply marks dirty keys and returns. The PROJECTOR (Layout -> scene), the Emitter rebuild (EM-30), the Lighting partition and Failure & Trail rebuilds run as P5 subscribers after the commit with writes disabled; if several ticks run in one render frame (catch-up, `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) they coalesce to the latest committed state and never replay each tick's change. Budget placeholders, all named in T9, measured in the spike and none a measured fact today: whole weld apply <= 0.5 ms Release (`MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`; the Graph's M2 alone <= 0.2 ms is locked and sits inside it), whole cut apply <= 0.5 ms (`MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`), install apply <= 0.2 ms (`MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`), `assembly.step` idle <= 0.02 ms (`MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS`; it runs 60 times a second), P5 subscribers on the weld frame <= 8 ms (`MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS`) with the PROJECTOR <= 4 ms (`PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS`, `[UNV]`) and no frame above 33 ms (`WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS`), and `weld_offer` per call <= `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (value set in the spike), since the Pipeline's camera cut hides a one-to-two-frame hitch. These are co-measured with Body's own weld-projection spike (its S3). `assembly.step` and the completion payloads are pure functions of block state, so replay and MIRROR identity hold. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-62 (lint and auto: determinism of `assembly.step`; no wall-clock or RNG), AC-MSA-63 (spike: P5 subscriber cost and frame hitch). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* KRN CR-10, CR-17, H9, H10; TD-15.

**MSA-40 — Every invariant is stated for every reachable state, not just the default.** *Rule:* conservation (a weld adds edges only; a cut tombstones them; no node is minted), `abs(actor)` of every actor, offsets on the 24-px grid, and the body count hold at **every** reachable state: zoomed in and out on the integer ladder (`k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; nothing in these rules depends on zoom, and the in-world cursor scales with the world viewport while the prompt and bar keep their HUD pixel size), with a carried module, with zero to four seats, with a CASCADE stub submitter, after a restore, and in MIRROR. Tests assert `abs` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr` (a weld followed by a cut is not an identity on `pos_curr`, BDY-23). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-64 (auto: the invariant matrix over those states). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R28.

**MSA-41 — The body count is bounded by construction; Assembly adds no capacity rule.** *Rule:* the number of live bodies satisfies `N(t) = N0 - W(t) + C(t)` with `C(t) <= W(t)` (every cut needs a prior ACTIVE weld), hence `N(t) <= N0` for every reachable sequence (formula D.8). MVP worst case: home + A + B + pod = 4 bodies before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut; the ceiling is one cut because both MVP sockets go terminal (LY-13). The lint `bodies_after_worst_sequence <= min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)` is 4 against `MAX_BODIES` 8 and the 2D Lighting placeholder `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4: **the equality is a risk, not a margin**; adding any body outside Assembly (a second pod, a loose piece Web Map keeps alive) or any Vertical Slice socket-economy change **degrades cross-body lighting: surplus bodies' lights are drawn as `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins (2D Lighting cross-body isolation rule)**. It is a degradation, not a fault: the sim is unaffected, but a body beyond the cap would lose real light cast onto its own geometry. ASK LT1 (T15) asks the 2D Lighting owner to confirm `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4 against this zero-margin worst case or to raise it to 5. `CAPACITY` on `can_detach` and a `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` value are deferred to Vertical Slice (Layout Open Question 8). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-65 (lint and auto: the worst MVP sequence ends at 4 bodies; the equality is reported as zero margin). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R7.

**MSA-42 — Do not fake a choice in the weld; the real decisions are whether, when and how clean.** *Rule:* the MVP weld is deliberately choiceless (MSA-13; Player Fantasy). The design commits to: no quality slider, no weld pattern, no rotation; the real decisions are **whether** to weld versus strip (neither line dominates; the playtest gate checks that both are taken), **when** relative to Attention, and **how clean** a later cut will be. Degenerate-play ledger, resolved: cancel-to-peek costs tool noise per accrued tick and the ledger is time-bounded (Interaction's); weld, strip by V8 salvage, cut away is an intended healthy line (each V8 costs noise and leaves open ends to cap, the cut is `LOUD`); weld-cut-weld to reset or farm is unreachable (terminal sockets, nothing minted); a RAGGED cut breaches both sides including the home side, so it is self-harm, never a weapon ("a cut never removes a threat the player is not leaving behind"); burning an infested section to escape is healthy P5 play, with the rule that **a cut must not launder Attention** (the locus falls back, the level does not reset: a verify handed to the Attention owner, ASK A1); battery-banking through weld and cut is not a faucet (conservation). **First-weld scaffold (authoring sequencing, no change to `weld_offer`):** before derelict B becomes dockable, the player has already exercised V4 and V3 on `home_ship` (clearing the home-ship faults), so the first connect is not a difficulty spike on a leaking seam; `weld_offer` never reads player history (ASK D1 answered by DA OQ-DA-31: no gate needed). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-66 (review: no choice-faking element exists), AC-MSA-69 (manual: both the weld line and the strip line are taken by at least 2 of 5 playtesters), AC-MSA-70 (manual: median time from prompt-first-appears to channel-begin <= 45 s, and from bead to first seam-breaker close 60-180 s; both are `[PLACEHOLDER]` **playtest targets for the playtest gate, not sim knobs**, and live in the playtest script rather than in any data file). *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar, SDT honesty. *Decides:* game-designer G1, G2, G13; advisor R11.

#### G. Tables of record

**T6 — `assembly.state` schema v1.**

| Field | Type | Range / enum | Persisted | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `schema_version` | int | 1 | yes | CR-8; an unsupported version on restore is a reported failure, never a silent default |
| `seats` | map PlayerId -> record | 0-4 entries | yes | keyed by PlayerId, never peer id; no packed arrays (MSA-4) |
| `seats[p].pending.kind` | enum | `NONE`, `WELD`, `SEAM_CUT` | yes, cleared by `post_restore` | |
| `seats[p].pending.target_ref` | primitive pair | socket key or `seam_id` | yes, cleared | stable ids, no object references (H7) |
| `seats[p].pending.submit_T` | int64 | >= 0 | yes, cleared | string in JSON (SL-2); the TTL anchor (MSA-6) |
| `seats[p].last_result.verb_id` | enum | `WELD`, `SEAM_CUT`, `INSTALL` | yes, cleared | |
| `seats[p].last_result.target_ref` | primitive pair | socket key, `seam_id` or hardpoint ref | yes, cleared | stable ids, no object references (H7); presenter anchor for the outcome cue |
| `seats[p].last_result.outcome` | enum | `DONE`, `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED`, `NOT_TAKEN` | yes, cleared | |
| `seats[p].last_result.reason` | int | 0-99 | yes, cleared | `MSA.<CODE>` as a number; diagnostic only |
| `seats[p].last_result.T` | int64 | >= 0 | yes, cleared | string in JSON; the presenter dedupe key (MSA-34) |
| `cascade_deferred` | list | at most 1 entry `{seam_id, first_deferred_T}` | yes | `[VS]`-reserved (MSA-22); empty in MVP |

`default_state()` is empty seats. No derived caches in `snapshot()` (CR-8). v1 is the first version, so no migration is required in MVP.

| AC-MSA-68 | manual | retained screenshots in `production/qa/evidence/` | Visual/Audio, UI Requirements |

| AC-MSA-72 | spike | weld on a moving body (`VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16) with the Graph at 6-tick cadence: zero FAULTED, `abs` conserved when docked | MSA-14 |

**T9 — Names this GDD introduces** (all `[PLACEHOLDER]` values; the registry stage has registered most of them from this text with this GDD as source, formulas from the formula, not only the constant; not yet registered: `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US`, `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S`, `subject_budget_at_seam`, `apply_budget_split`, `fault_loss_window`, `pending_any`). Knobs: `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (240-480), `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (240-480), `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 850 (400-990, refines advisor R11), `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` 600 `[VS]` (60-1800, multiple of 6), `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 (8-119), `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 (12-180), `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.2, `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` 0.02, `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33, `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (value set in the spike), `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` 8 (asked of Layout and Authoring, ASK L2), and the manual-AC parameter `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60 (not a sim knob). Block and hook: `assembly.state`, `assembly.step`. Commands (registry: `assembly_commands`): `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `weld`, `seam_cut`, `install_module`. Reasons and outcomes (registry: `assembly_reasons`): `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, `MSA.NOT_DOCKED`, `NOT_TAKEN`, `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`. Formulas (D.1 to D.18; `seam_at_weld` D.4 is the Graph's and only worked here): `weld_quality` (D.1, with `WELD_Q_DEFAULT`), `weld_offer` and `seam_cut_offer` (D.2), `host_and_mate` (D.3), `frame_shift_prediction_equal` (D.5), `cut_mode` (D.6), `channel_timing` (D.7), `bodies_after_worst_sequence` (D.8), `last_shape_change_T` (D.9), `cascade_defer_deadline` (D.10), `pending_ttl_ok` (D.11), `weld_batch_key_budget` (D.12), `cut_blocked` (D.13), `subject_budget_at_seam` (D.14, Interaction's formula, Assembly supplies one term), `apply_budget_split` (D.15), `fault_loss_window` (D.16), `outcome_cue_gate` (D.17), `welded_body_capacity` (D.18). Read: `last_shape_change_T`, `pending_any`. Diagnostics and lints (registry: `assembly_diagnostics`): `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`, `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`, `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`, `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (a Layout or Authoring lint), `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` (Layout's). Never reused: `begin_cut`, `CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SALVAGE_*`.

### States and Transitions

Three state machines are in play, none of them owned in full by Assembly: the per-seat **channel handoff** (Interaction owns the channel half, Assembly the outcome half), the **install** TAP, and the **socket and seam lifecycle** that Assembly drives but Layout and the Graph own. "Who may cause" names the only system or actor allowed to make the transition.

**S1 — Channel handoff for weld and seam cut, per seat.**

| State | Where it lives | Entry | Exit | Transitions out | Who may cause | Visible to the player |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE | `interaction.state` has no record for the seat; `assembly.state.pending.kind` = `NONE` | start; any terminal | `begin_*` applied | -> CHANNELLING | the seat (PLAYER `begin_weld` / `begin_seam_cut`, offer permitting). A `begin_*` pressed while `pending.kind != NONE` is rejected `BUSY` at site 1 and the seat stays where it is (MSA-6, MSA-7) | the prompt only if the offer holds |
| CHANNELLING | `interaction.state` record `{owner_system ASSEMBLY, begin_tick Tb, duration D, steps_done ...}` | `begin_*.apply` in P2 of `Tb` | DONE (accrued `D - resume_offset` ticks) or ABORTED | -> HANDOFF; -> ABORTED | `interaction.step` (clock, abort set, noise); the seat (cancel, movement) | linear LONG bar with needle-mark, sparks at the hand point, work-loop sound, the chest beam stays on |
| ABORTED | Interaction (an IA-15 cause plus a ledger entry, TTL `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600) | any abort cause | IDLE, or re-entry to CHANNELLING from the ledger within the TTL | -> IDLE; -> CHANNELLING | Interaction (world or body ended it); the seat (let go) | bar drains (cancel) or snaps empty with an "interrupted" cue (abort); the socket and the hull are unchanged (no bead exists before the weld applies; Interaction's abort frames only) |
| HANDOFF | one tick: the DONE handoff in `channel_view` | accrual tick `e = D - resume_offset` (P3 of tick `T`) | `assembly.step` in the same P3 | -> REJECTED; -> PENDING | `interaction.step` creates it; `assembly.step` consumes it | nothing (same tick); for a cut the `LOUD` is pushed now |
| REJECTED | `last_result = REJECTED(reason)`, nothing submitted | the handoff pre-check fails (MSA-7 site 2) | next `begin_*` | -> IDLE | `assembly.step` | the channel has ended, with the verb family's one post-channel cue (weld: the bead sputters and does not hold; cut: the flame gutters and the seam holds); the cause is world-visible (a crewmate in the hatch, a socket already welded, a lost dock). REJECTED at *begin* is a different thing and keeps the prompt grammar |
| PENDING | `assembly.state.pending {kind, target_ref, submit_T}`; the SYSTEM completion is in the kernel queue | `assembly.step` submits the completion | the completion's `apply` clears it; or the TTL fires | -> APPLIED; -> BLOCKED; -> NOT_TAKEN | `assembly.step` (enter); the kernel and the completion `apply` (exit) | nothing (normally one tick, 16.7 ms) |
| APPLIED | Layout `welds[]` row, Graph SeamRecord, Body pose rows changed | completion `validate` OK and no DEFERRED failure | `last_result = DONE`, `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | the completion `apply` only | the world changed: bead or cap plate, hatch leaf, weep, possible camera cut |
| BLOCKED | `last_result = BLOCKED(reason)`; nothing mutated | `validate` OK but the full `can_join` returns `PORT_ABSENT` in `apply` A0 | `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | the completion `apply` only | the weld family's cue, "the bead sputters and does not hold" (`PORT_ABSENT` is reachable only by a weld) |
| NOT_TAKEN | `last_result = NOT_TAKEN` | the completion was Rejected, Dropped or `STALE` and `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS` | `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | `assembly.step` (TTL rule) only | the verb family's cue (weld sputter; cut "flame gutters and the seam holds") |

Transition rules. IDLE -> CHANNELLING -> HANDOFF -> {REJECTED | PENDING} -> {APPLIED | BLOCKED | NOT_TAKEN} -> IDLE; CHANNELLING -> ABORTED -> {IDLE | CHANNELLING}. PENDING resolves to exactly one of its three exits (MSA-6). A restore from any state returns the seat to IDLE with the channel dropped (`RESTORE`) and the seat records cleared. FAULTED is a kernel state, not an Assembly state: a defect inside any `apply` leaves the whole kernel in FAULTED and no Assembly state is meaningful until a player-initiated restore (MSA-31).

**S2 — Install, a TAP, one tick.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions out | Who may cause | Visible |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CARRYING (Suit and Controller own it) | pickup | install or drop | -> APPLIED; -> BLOCKED; -> REJECTED; drop | the seat | carry gait; the install prompt appears on a compatible HARDPOINT |
| APPLIED | `install_module` validate OK and `check_install_module` OK | `last_result = DONE` | -> CARRYING ends (the instance dissolves into an INSTALLED node, Item DB row 8) | the install `apply` only | the module seated, the carried instance gone, the clunk |
| BLOCKED | `check_install_module` fails | `last_result = BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` | -> CARRYING (the instance is still carried) | the install `apply` only | the module bumps and stays in the hands |
| REJECTED | `validate` fails | none | -> CARRYING | the kernel / `validate` | no prompt, or the standard rejection cue |

**S3 — Socket and seam lifecycle as Assembly drives it** (cited; the owners are in brackets).

| Socket [Layout LY-13] | Seam [Graph LS-15/LS-16] | Trigger | Mutators | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FREE` | none | the `weld` completion `apply` | M2, LM2, BM3 | Assembly only |
| `WELDED(seam_id)` | `ACTIVE`, `leak_bp` 10000, wearing in | time (Graph wear-in) | none | the Graph |
| `WELDED(seam_id)` | `ACTIVE`, `leak_bp` 0 | wear-in complete | none | the Graph |
| `CAPPED` | `CUT`, boundary SEALED | the `seam_cut` completion, `cut_mode` SEALED | M3, LM3, BM4 | Assembly only |
| `CUT_OPEN` | `CUT`, boundary RAGGED | the `seam_cut` completion, `cut_mode` RAGGED | M3, LM3, BM4 | Assembly only |
| `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` | `CUT` (tombstone kept with the boundary mode) | nothing; terminal in MVP | none | none |

Illegal transitions: `FREE -> CAPPED`, `WELDED -> FREE`, `CAPPED -> WELDED` (re-weld), any state to `FREE`. A second weld on a used socket returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`. The body-membership consequence follows Layout: a weld merges two bodies (the absorbed body becomes `DISSOLVED`); a cut splits one body into exactly two components because the weld relation is a forest (LY-14: `SAME_BODY` is a hard reject).

### Interactions with Other Systems

Status on 2026-10-26 (Glob of `design/gdd/`): the kernel, Graph, Item DB, Interaction, Layout, Body, Save/Load, Controller, Suit, Emitter State, 2D Lighting, Failure & Trail, Pipeline, Attention and Derelict Authoring all carry the header **Designed** (Item DB, Interaction, Layout, Body, Save/Load, Attention and Derelict Authoring were designed in this orchestrated run, so their rows about Assembly were written before this GDD and carry "provisional" markers that this GDD answers). Sanity and Horror are drafts in flight (`sanity-and-hallucination-rules.md`, `horror-entities.md`; their rows about Assembly agree: Sanity repeats ASK F1, Horror HE-22 repeats MSA-22's no-cut-verb rule). Web Map & Transit, Boarding & Salvage, the Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio, the HUD and in-world instruments, and the Debug Overlay had **no file** then; every contract with them was provisional. (Refreshed 2026-10-27, consistency pass: Web Map & Transit, Boarding & Salvage and Adaptive Audio & Tells are now Designed and their rows below are refreshed.) Interface owners are named in each row; where this GDD fixes a name that another GDD owns, the owner's spelling is used verbatim and any mismatch is a CONFLICT row, never a silent rename.
