| **Boarding & Salvage** (designed) | the install command; the weld offer once docked; the seam cut as the extraction service; `last_result` | the carried-module state | Boarding: the sortie loop and extraction decisions; Assembly: the commands | Boarding must repeat the install precondition (carried, compatible, at a hardpoint); the Item DB's `T_cycle` is "cut + carry + `cap_port` on the stubs + install" (Interaction's and Assembly's). Dependency strength **Hard** (the reference sortie ends in the `htr_a` install, BS-17). `scr_a` has no MVP destination: no vacant scrubber hardpoint and no `remove_module` (MSA-28; Boarding C-BS-3, EC-BS-16), so it comes home as a loose item |
| **Web Map & Transit** (designed) | `weld_offer` (gated on `docked`); weld and cut result via Layout/Graph dirty keys; `last_shape_change_T()` | `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` established before the offer and held while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open; `init_world`; the detached piece persists as a dormant orphan body (record status ORPHAN); no discard in MVP (WM-23); the pod body counted in `MAX_BODIES` | Transit: docking, `init_world`, the orphan record; Body: the `docked` test; Assembly: gates the offer | BDY-27 must-repeat: the PLAYER-origin weld is only offered once `docked` holds. Web Map WM-22 holds `docked` while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open (ASK T1 answered); WM-23 detects weld and cut by a read (`last_shape_change_T()` early-out) |
| **Attention System** (designed) | nothing pushed; `body_of` changes via Layout; the cut-away fact via Layout (locus fallback) | none | Attention (units, locus, breach machine) | C5 closed; verify ASK A1 (a cut must not launder Attention) |
| **Derelict Authoring** (designed) | `socket_plan` / `can_join` consumption | the MVP socket pair, lanes POWER 0 + O₂ 0, offset `(696, 384)`, the one-sided-hatch convention, the fault order | Authoring: content; Layout: schema | DA-10 must-repeat; ASK D1 answered (OQ-DA-31) |
| **Emitter State, 2D Lighting, Pipeline** (designed) | Layout and Body dirty keys only | none | Emitter: rebuild from `SEC/` and `BODY/` (EM-30 `body_id` partition); Lighting: partition by `BODIES_LIVE_MAX`; Pipeline: camera cut on `BODY_TRANSFER` | CONFLICT C6; the PROJECTOR is a P5 subscriber (MSA-39) |
| **Horror Entities** (draft on disk) | layout changes (weld, cut) via Layout dirty keys (`SEC/`, `BODY/`); no cut verb for entities (MSA-22) | none | Horror: the path-cache recompute (HE-22) | HE-22 names Assembly as a Soft neighbour; refresh: HE-22 should cite MSA-14/MSA-20 (weld/cut apply) rather than MSA-22 (the CASCADE-cut rule) |
| **Failure & Trail** (designed) | seam and cut state through the Graph | none | Failure & Trail: renders the weep and breach | The seam weep, the hiss and the `SEAL_STRAIN` creak are its rendering of Graph descriptors |
| **Adaptive Audio & Tells** (Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) | the weld and cut sound moments; the CASCADE tell-window co-ownership | the 10-30 s tell | Audio: sound; Assembly: cue timing | repeat the tell window |
| **Perception Filter / Sanity** (Sanity: draft on disk, repeats F1; Filter: no file) | none | truth-channel tags for bead, cap plate, socket geometry, `CUT_BLOCKED` | Filter | must repeat MSA-35 and the install-prompt rule (it may not fake or suppress an install prompt) *(provisional)* |
| **HUD, in-world instruments, Debug Overlay** (no file) | the cursor data contract via Layout; the bar via `channel_view`; the Overlay reads true `q`, `leak_bp`, seam state | the prompt-plate and linear-bar grammar | Interaction: prompt; Overlay: true values | *(provisional — undesigned)* |

**Tick-order facts the rows above depend on.** Within one tick, P2 (commands: the `begin_*` applies, the completions, install) precedes P3 (hooks). On a Graph tick (`T mod 6 == 0`) the P3 order is `graph.propagate.power` -> `.atmo` -> `.failure` -> ... `emitter.place` -> `interaction.step` -> `assembly.step` (IA-14: the Graph's three hooks run before `interaction.step` on a shared tick, through the `emitter.place` chain). The weld applies in P2, so the first propagate after it (the next tick with `T mod 6 == 0`) sees the bumped `topology_version` and no half-state is observable. A completion submitted in `assembly.step` in P3 of `T` is dispatched in P2 of `T+1`. Body's mutators run after Layout's, which run after the Graph's, inside one `WriteScope`.

## Formulas

Assembly's arithmetic is small on purpose. The Graph owns the seam formulas (`seam_at_weld`, `A_seam`, the SEALED/RAGGED hole and open-end rates), Layout the weld offset and detach re-base, Body the docking invariant and `actors_overlapping`, Interaction the channel clock, ledger and subject budget. This section defines only what Assembly owns (`q`, the two offer predicates, host and mate selection, the cut-mode derivation, the pending TTL, the body-count bound, the shape-change time, the CASCADE deadline) and works each cited formula at MVP values so the numbers a reviewer sees are the numbers a test asserts. Every formula is integer arithmetic (the kernel's determinism rule: anything hashed, replicated or replayed is integer or fixed-point); the only fractional values below are display conversions marked as such. Numbering matches the cross-references in Detailed Design (D.2 to D.12 are cited there); D.13 to D.18 are additions. Every formula that Assembly owns is a registry candidate as a **formula** (name, expression, variables), not only as constants (`docs/consistency-failures.md`: register the formula). MVP data: the pair `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E, `line_px` 720, `start_px` 672) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W, `line_px` 24, `start_px` 288), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER 0 and O2 0, weld offset `(696, 384)` (DA-10), `GRID_24` closure. Where a value is not published by its owner it is marked `[UNV]`.

### D.1 Weld quality

The weld_quality formula is defined as:

`q = clamp( WELD_Q_DEFAULT , Q_MIN , Q_MAX )`, with `Q_MIN = max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))` and `Q_MAX = 990`

Derived: `Q_MIN` is the larger of 400 and the smallest `q` whose `hp_seam = q x 10` (`LSG D.14`) is not below `LEAK_HP` (that smallest `q` is `ceil(LEAK_HP / 10)`, so a `LEAK_HP` that is not a multiple of 10 rounds up and the integer-division ambiguity does not arise; the 400 outer floor holds across the Graph's 2000-6000 `LEAK_HP` range); `Q_MAX` is 10 permille under the no-weep cliff (`q` = 1000), where `A_seam` would be 0 (`LSG D.2` Output Range). The clamp runs once, at data load, never per weld; a clamped value raises the diagnostic `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`. The rule has no other term: there is no `sum q_mods`, no per-weld input and no state read.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` | int (permille) | 400-990, default 850 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | The one weld-quality constant; refines advisor R11 (was 400-1000) |
| `LEAK_HP` | int (bp) | 2000-6000, default 4000 | Graph tuning (cited, locked) | Below this a fluid sub-edge starts in LEAK |
| `Q_MIN` | int (permille) | 400-600 (400 at `LEAK_HP` <= 4000; 600 at 6000) | derived | Lower clamp; moves with `LEAK_HP`, never below 0 |
| `Q_MAX` | int (permille) | 990 | derived (`1000 - 10`) | Upper clamp |
| `q` | int (permille) | `Q_MIN`..990 | calculated | The value placed in the plan handed to `weld_join` and stored on the SeamRecord |

**Output Range:** 400 to 990 inclusive. Two named cliffs sit just outside it. *Below 400:* `hp_seam` falls under `LEAK_HP` and every fluid sub-edge of the seam starts in LEAK mode, a leak with no prior tell (a P2 fairness break). *At 1000:* `A_seam` is 0, the seam has no weep and the weld loses its P1 tell. Within the range the Graph's consequences are physical and cited, not restated (below, `L_seam` 2, cable port caps 3000):

| `q` | `(500 + q/2)` permille of the lesser port | `cap_seam` (W) | `hp_seam` | weep per tile `ceil(16 x 10000 x (1000-q)^2 / 10^10)` | `A_seam` (`L_seam` 2) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 399 (loaded as-is, would clamp to 400) | 699 | 2097 | 3990 (below `LEAK_HP` 4000) | 6 | 12 | leaks at birth: outside the range |
| 400 | 700 | 2100 | 4000 (not below) | 6 | 12 | the lower bound |
| 700 | 850 | 2550 | 7000 (`HP_OK` boundary) | 2 | 4 | legal, tight |
| 850 | 925 | 2775 | 8500 | 1 | 2 | the MVP default |
| 990 | 995 | 2985 | 9900 | 1 | 2 | the upper bound: still weeps |
| 1000 (loaded as-is, would clamp to 990) | 1000 | 3000 | 10000 | 0 | 0 | no weep: outside the range |

**Example:** the data file ships `WELD_Q_DEFAULT = 850`: no clamp, `q = 850`, `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` not raised. A modder or a bad edit ships 1000: the loader clamps to 990, raises `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` once at load, the weld produces `A_seam` 2 and the player still hears and sees the weep.

### D.2 Offer predicates

The weld_offer formula is defined as:

`weld_offer(seat, s) = ( tool_in_hand(seat) == tool_welder ) AND lit(seat, s) AND in_reach(seat, s) AND ( socket_state(s) == FREE ) AND ( |M(s)| >= 1 )`, with `M(s) = { b : can_attach(a, b) == OK AND docked(a, b, frame_shift_px(a, b)) }` and `a` the host socket of D.3

Derived: the prompt `E . Weld` exists iff `weld_offer` is true. The predicate reads no node or edge accessor, calls neither `can_join_static` nor the full `can_join`, and reads no `surge[]` (MSA-9, MSA-12). The carry lockout (a seat carrying an item gets no channelled verb, PC-7, IA-28) is Interaction's selector, not a term here; a carrying seat therefore never sees the weld prompt and `begin_weld` is a `BUSY` backstop.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_in_hand(seat)` | enum | tool class id | Suit (`tool_welder` is class {1, 9}, slot 2, equipped at start) | The equipped tool |
| `lit(seat, s)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Emitter State via Interaction's lit check | The socket subject is lit (lit-only channelled verbs, PC-7) |
| `in_reach(seat, s)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Interaction reach geometry, `REACH_PX` 72 | The socket subject is within reach |
| `socket_state(s)` | enum | `FREE`, `WELDED`, `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN` | Layout (LY-13) | Terminal lifecycle (S3) |
| `can_attach(a, b)` | enum | `OK` or one of eight reasons | Layout (LY-14) | The mating verdict |
| `frame_shift_px(a, b)` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid | Layout `LY D.6` (ASK L3 asks for it as a pure read) | The shift the weld would apply (D.5) |
| `docked(a, b, fs)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Body `BDY D.8` | The two bodies already sit at the weld offset with equal velocity |
| `M(s)` | set of sockets | 0..`MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` x `MAX_SECTIONS` in principle; exactly 0 or 1 in MVP | calculated | The mates that pass both `can_attach` and `docked` |
| `weld_offer` | bool | 0 or 1 | calculated | Whether the prompt exists |

**Output Range:** boolean. Pure and total: no side effect, no module-body state. In MVP `|M|` is 0 or 1 by authoring (one socket pair); with several mates `[VS]` D.3 selects the lowest key. Per-call cost is O(1) over cached static data (`MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US`, set in the spike).

**Example:** the seat holds the welder, stands lit within 72 px of `sock_airlock_e`; the socket is `FREE`; `sock_b_w` passes `can_attach` (facings E and W are opposite, `width_tiles` 2 and 2, `SOCKET_CLASS` 1 and 1, lane multisets equal, no `GEOM_OVERLAP` under Layout's `rects_in` test: the home `spine_corridor` east hull wall is x [672, 696) per Derelict Authoring D.13); B is docked at `frame_shift_px = (696, 384)`: `|M| = 1`, offer = 1, the prompt shows. The same stance with B still 24 px short of its dock (`world(b) - world(a) = (720, 384)`): `docked` is false, `|M| = 0`, offer = 0, **no prompt and no "can't weld" icon**.

The seam_cut_offer formula is defined as:

`seam_cut_offer(seat, s) = ( tool_in_hand(seat) == tool_cutter ) AND lit(seat, s) AND in_reach(seat, s) AND ( socket_state(s) == WELDED ) AND ( seam_state(seam_of(s)) == ACTIVE ) AND ( foot_section(seat) IN keep_sections(seam_of(s)) )`

Derived: `keep_sections` is the component of the weld forest that keeps its `body_id` (the one containing the body's `origin_section_id`, LY-17; in MVP the home-ship side), from Layout's `detach_preview` (ASK L1). There is deliberately **no `CUT_BLOCKED` term**: the prompt is offered, `CUT_BLOCKED` is checked at begin, handoff and completion (D.13), and the clean hatch-presence oracle stays a rejection the player can see (MSA-21).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_in_hand(seat)` | enum | tool class id | Suit (`tool_cutter`, class {8}, slot 1) | The equipped tool |
| `lit`, `in_reach` | bool | 0 or 1 | as above | |
| `socket_state(s)` | enum | as above | Layout | Must be `WELDED` |
| `seam_of(s)` | seam id | `seam_id` allocated by the Graph | Layout `welds[]` | The seam the socket is part of |
| `seam_state` | enum | `ACTIVE`, `CUT` | Layout / Graph | Only an `ACTIVE` seam can be cut |
| `foot_section(seat)` | section id | a section of the seat's body | Body `BDY D.10` | The section containing the seat's foot **origin** (BDY-23) |
| `keep_sections(seam)` | set of section ids | 1..`MAX_SECTIONS` | Layout `detach_preview` (ASK L1) | Sections that keep `body_id` after the cut |

**Output Range:** boolean. **Example:** welded pair, seat standing in the home-ship airlock (a section in the home component): offer = 1. The same seat standing in derelict B's `b_dock`: `foot_section` is in the leaving component, offer = 0 and **no cut prompt exists on the derelict side** (the self-strand trap is unreachable). A `FREE` socket: offer = 0 (state must be `WELDED`); a `CAPPED` socket: offer = 0 (terminal).

### D.3 Host and mate selection

The host_and_mate formula is defined as:

`socket_a = the socket of the section s with kind(s) == HOME ; socket_b = argmin over M of ( socket_key_a , socket_key_b )` with `M` as in D.2; the pair is empty when `M` is empty or neither section is HOME

Derived: if both sections are DERELICT `[VS]`, `socket_a` is the socket on the body with the lower `body_id`. The seat may stand on either side and focus either socket; Assembly derives the ordered pair, so the surviving body is always the home-ship's and the absorbed derelict body is re-based (LY-16 / LY Open Question 21: confirmed).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kind(s)` | enum | `HOME`, `DERELICT` | Graph LS-17 | Section kind |
| `body_id` | int | 0..`MAX_BODIES` - 1 | Layout | The tie-break for a two-derelict pair `[VS]` |
| `socket_key` | string id | stable, ordered by byte comparison | Layout | The tie-break among several mates `[VS]` |
| `M` | set | as D.2 | calculated | Candidate mates |

**Output Range:** one ordered pair `(socket_a, socket_b)` or the empty pair (no offer). Deterministic: the same state always selects the same pair, which replay and MIRROR rely on. **Example:** the seat stands in derelict B's `b_dock` and focuses `sock_b_w`; `kind(derelict_b)` is DERELICT, `kind(home_ship)` is HOME; the pair is `(sock_airlock_e, sock_b_w)` regardless of which socket was focused, and B is the absorbed side.

### D.4 Seam properties at weld (cited from the Graph, worked at MVP values)

The seam_at_weld formula is defined as:

`plan_seam = seam_at_weld( q , cap_a , cap_b , L_seam )`; **owned by the Graph (`LSG D.14`, registry `seam_at_weld`; `LSG D.2` for `A_seam`); Assembly supplies `q` (D.1) and the plan's `L_seam`, restates no expression and adds no term**

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q` | int (permille) | 400-990 | D.1 | Supplied by Assembly |
| `L_seam` | int (tiles) | 1-12 | Layout `width_tiles`, via `socket_plan` | MVP value 2 |
| `cap_a`, `cap_b` | int (loop units per step) | per port | the two joined ports' `cap` (Item DB defs; authored for bare nodes) | POWER lane: `mod_breaker_junction` CABLE ports, 3000 each (Item DB def 12, AC-79). O2 lane: `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` are bare DUCT_JUNCTION nodes whose `cap` no design file publishes: **GAP, symbol `cap_duct`, `[UNV]`**, ASK G6(d) |
| `A_SEAM_MAX`, `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP`, `SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100`, `WELD_DP_WARN`, `LEAK_HP` | int | 16, 10000, 100, 300 dkPa, 4000 | Graph tuning (cited, locked) | Used only inside the Graph's expressions |

**Output Range:** the Graph's own: `cap_seam` 700 to 995 permille of the lesser port across Assembly's `q` range, `hp_seam` 4000 to 9900, `leak_bp` starts at 10000, `A_seam` per `LSG D.2`.

**Example (MVP POWER lane, `q` 850):** `cap_a = cap_b = 3000`; `cap_seam = floor(3000 x 925 / 1000) = 2775 W`; `hp_seam = 8500` (above `HP_OK` 7000 and `LEAK_HP` 4000); `leak_bp = 10000`; `A_seam = 2 x ceil(16 x 10000 x 150^2 / 10^10) = 2 x ceil(0.36) = 2` (a weep of 1 per tile over 2 tiles). The O2 lane: `cap_seam = floor( min(cap_duct_a, cap_duct_b) x 925 / 1000 )`, not worked until ASK G6(d) is answered. Wear-in (cited): `leak_bp` falls 100 bp per 10 s period from 10000, so the weep holds flat at 1 per tile and stops at period 100: `100 x 10 s = 1000 s`, about 16.7 minutes. Surge (cited, `LSG D.14`): home bay at 1000 dkPa against a derelict room at 150 dkPa gives `|dP|` = 850 > `WELD_DP_WARN` 300, so `surge[].warn` is true and the closed hatch creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`); the weld is permitted and moves nothing. Assembly's cue reads `warn` as a boolean only (MSA-15).

### D.5 Weld offset prediction and the actor invariant

The frame_shift_prediction_equal formula is defined as:

`frame_shift_px = offset_a + pos_a - pos_b - offset_b_old` ; the apply asserts `LM2.frame_shift_px == predicted` (else `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`) ; `docked(a, b, fs) = ( vel(a) == vel(b) ) AND ( world(b) - world(a) == fs )` ; `abs(actor) = world(body) + pos_curr`

Derived: the first is Layout's `LY D.6` (attach), the third Body's `BDY D.8`; Assembly computes nothing new, it compares the value predicted in `validate` (V3) with the value LM2 returns (A3) and BM3 consumes (A4). Because `offset_a`, `pos_a` and `pos_b` all lie on the 24-px grid (`GRID_24` lint), so does the result (the 24-grid closure, `LY D.6`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `offset_a`, `offset_b_old` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid; `(0, 0)` for a freshly built single-section body (LY-25) | Layout | Host section offset in the surviving body; absorbed section offset in its own body |
| `pos_a`, `pos_b` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid, local >= 0 | Layout socket reference points: `(line_px, start_px)` for facing E or W | The two sockets' reference points |
| `frame_shift_px` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid, far below `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` 2^22 | calculated (Layout) | The absorbed body's frame origin in the host frame; every absorbed section GAINS it |
| `world(a)`, `world(b)`, `vel(a)`, `vel(b)` | int pairs | Body ranges | `body.state` | Exterior poses and velocities |
| `pos_curr` | int pair (px) | in the actor's body frame | `body.state` | The actor's position |

**Output Range:** exact integers. `delta_abs = frame_shift_px - (world(b) - world(a))` is `(0, 0)` iff the position half of `docked` holds; when it does not hold the weld still succeeds in the simulation and the absorbed hull jumps by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer only (the Pipeline's camera cut is the named fallback).

**Example (DA-10):** `offset_a = (0, 0)`, `pos_a = (720, 672)`, `pos_b = (24, 288)`, `offset_b_old = (0, 0)`: `frame_shift_px = (0 + 720 - 24 - 0, 0 + 672 - 288 - 0) = (696, 384)` (29 x 24 and 16 x 24). With `world(home) = (1000, 500)` and `world(B) = (1696, 884)`, equal velocity: `world(b) - world(a) = (696, 384)`, docked, `delta_abs = (0, 0)`. An actor on B at `pos_curr = (100, 300)` has `abs = (1696, 884) + (100, 300) = (1796, 1184)` before; BM3 gives `pos_curr = (796, 684)` in the host frame, `abs = (1000, 500) + (796, 684) = (1796, 1184)`: equal. Not docked (`world(B) = (1720, 884)`): the difference is `(720, 384)`, `delta_abs = (696, 384) - (720, 384) = (-24, 0)`, the case the offer gate keeps away from the player. (Layout's own `LY D.6` example, `(576, 96)`, is a different socket placement and is not the MVP pair.) A weld followed by a cut of the same seam returns `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px` and zeroes the sub-pixel part, so it is **not** an identity on `pos_curr`; tests assert `abs` and section-local position.

### D.6 Cut mode

The cut_mode formula is defined as:

`cut_mode(seam) = SEALED if ( for all o in openings(seam): area(o) == 0 ) AND ( for all e in fluid_sub_edges(seam): valve(e) == CLOSED ) AND ( for all c in cable_sub_edges(seam): breaker(c) == OPEN ), else RAGGED`

Derived: the three clauses are exactly the as-welded state `weld_join` leaves (`LSG D.14`: opening `area` 0, valves CLOSED, breaker OPEN). The test reads **positions only**, never `hp`, `state` or `served_pm`. It is evaluated once, in the completion `apply` (step A1), and the single result is passed identically to M3 and LM3. No payload, prompt or cue carries or names a mode.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `area(o)` | int | 0..`area_max` | Graph via `seam_view` (ASK G3) | Opening area (the hatch) |
| `valve(e)` | enum | `OPEN`, `CLOSED` | Graph via `seam_view` | PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valve position |
| `breaker(c)` | enum | `OPEN`, `CLOSED` | Graph via `seam_view` | CABLE sub-edge breaker position |
| `cut_mode` | enum | `SEALED`, `RAGGED` | calculated | The `boundary_mode` for M3 and LM3 |

**Output Range:** two values, deterministic. SEALED: nothing leaks, the socket becomes `CAPPED`, tombstones with a cap plate. RAGGED: per former opening a virtual hole `A_CUT` 24 on each side as a hull stub of `hp` 2500 (severity 3 BREACH, EXOGENOUS, exempt from the fairness gate, LS-16), each fluid port an `open_end` leaking at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` 500 until `cap_port`; a 20-tile room decompresses in 382 steps (38 s); the socket becomes `CUT_OPEN`. All cited from the Graph.

**Example (MVP seam: one hatch OPENING, one O2 DUCT sub-edge, one POWER CABLE sub-edge):**

| hatch `area` | O2 valve | breaker | `cut_mode` | what the player saw beforehand |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | CLOSED | OPEN | SEALED | a quiet seam: no creak, no frost, no hiss |
| 64 (open) | CLOSED | OPEN | RAGGED | the leaf standing open with a draught across the threshold |
| 0 | OPEN | OPEN | RAGGED | a hiss at the open valve's lane |
| 0 | CLOSED | CLOSED | RAGGED | the junction's own powered state |
| 64 | OPEN | CLOSED | RAGGED | all three |

A hatch closed but a hallucinated "closed" valve that is truly open: RAGGED; the hiss is the consequence-channel witness the player could have heard (MSA-23). A hatch opened during a cancelled-and-resumed cut makes the resumed cut RAGGED because the mode is re-derived at completion.

### D.7 Channel timing and exposure

The channel_timing formula is defined as:

`T_done = Tb + D - resume_offset` ; `T_apply = T_done + 1` ; `blocks = D / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` ; `exposure_s = ( D - resume_offset ) / TICK_RATE` ; `tool_pushes = D - resume_offset` ; constraint `D mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS == 0`

Derived: the clock, the abort set, the ledger and the noise are Interaction's (IA-14, IA-16, IA-33); Assembly supplies only `D` and consumes `T_done`. Accrual is at `e = T - Tb` = 1 to `D - resume_offset`, so the DONE handoff is the P3 of tick `T_done` and the completion applies in the P2 of `T_apply` (MSA-3). `resume_offset = blocks_done x B` while the ledger entry is valid (`T - abort_tick <= PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`), else 0 (`IA D.6`, cited). A cancel wastes at most `B - 1` accrued ticks (the partial block is not credited), so cancel-to-peek is never free.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Tb` | int64 tick | >= 0 | the P2 tick of `begin_*` | Begin tick (held as two 32-bit integers or a string at the serialisation boundary, SL-2) |
| `D` | int ticks | 240-480, multiple of 6 | `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 / `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Declared duration |
| `resume_offset` | int ticks | 0..`D - B` | Interaction ledger (cited) | Credited ticks from an earlier aborted channel |
| `B` = `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int | 6 | Graph (cited) | Block length |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | Kernel (cited) | Ticks per second |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int | 600 | Interaction (cited) | Ledger lifetime |

**Output Range:** `blocks` 40 to 80; exposure 4.0 s (cut) to 8.0 s (weld at 480) fresh; `T_apply - Tb = D + 1` for a fresh channel. The floor 240 keeps both channels in the LONG bar class (`BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240); the ceiling 480 is set by analogy with IA I-9, not a lock on Assembly.

**Example:** a fresh weld begins in P2 of `Tb` = 437039: `D` 360, `blocks` 60, accrual on ticks 437040..437399, `T_done` 437399, `T_apply` 437400; 360 TOOL pushes, exposure 6.0 s, **no** LOUD at the completion. A fresh seam cut begins at `Tb` = 439759: `D` 240, `T_done` 439999, `T_apply` 440000, 240 TOOL pushes and one LOUD at DONE (pushed by Interaction), exposure 4.0 s. A weld cancelled by movement at `e` = 100 credits block 16 (`resume_offset` 96); a re-begin within 600 ticks has remaining `360 - 96` = 264 ticks (4.4 s), so the total accrued TOOL ticks are 99 + 264 = 363, three more than the fresh 360 (the wasted partial block).

### D.8 Body-count bound

The bodies_after_worst_sequence formula is defined as:

`N(t) = N0 - W(t) + C(t)` with `C(t) <= W(t)`, therefore `N(t) <= N0` for every reachable sequence; `bodies_after_worst_sequence = N0`; lint `N0 <= min( MAX_BODIES , BODIES_LIVE_MAX )`

Derived: a weld merges two bodies into one (the absorbed body is `DISSOLVED`), a cut splits one into two, and every cut needs a prior `ACTIVE` weld, so cuts never outnumber welds. Sockets are terminal (LY-13), so `W <= P`, the number of authored socket pairs. Assembly adds no capacity rule; `can_detach`'s `CAPACITY` reason and a `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` value are deferred to Vertical Slice (Layout Open Question 8).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N0` | int | 1..`MAX_BODIES` | built at load (Web Map `init_world`, Layout) | Live bodies before any Assembly command |
| `W(t)`, `C(t)` | int | 0..`P`; 0..`W(t)` | count of Layout `welds[]` rows ever welded / ever cut | Welds and cuts applied by tick `t` |
| `P` | int | MVP 1 | authored socket pairs | Possible welds |
| `MAX_BODIES` | int | 8 | Layout (cited, locked) | Hard cap |
| `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` | int | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | 2D Lighting (cited) | Cross-body lighting cap |

**Output Range:** MVP `N(t)` is 4 before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut. **Zero margin:** the worst case equals `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` exactly. Any body added outside Assembly (a second pod, a loose piece Web Map keeps alive) or any Vertical Slice socket-economy change exceeds it, which degrades cross-body lighting (stand-ins) without breaking the sim (MSA-41, ASK LT1). **Example:** home + A + B + pod = 4; weld (B dissolves into home): `4 - 1 + 0` = 3; cut: `4 - 1 + 1` = 4; a second weld attempt returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, so `N` stays 4 and `4 <= min(8, 4)` holds with margin 0.

### D.9 Shape-change time and the autosave event

The last_shape_change_T formula is defined as:

`last_shape_change_T = max over welds[] of max( welded_at_T , cut_at_T )`, or `NONE` when `welds[]` is empty ; the cited consumer is `event_due = event_pending AND ( T - last_save_T ) >= AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`

Derived: Assembly publishes the pure read; Save/Load polls it at tick boundaries (SL-13) and Layout's `S/<seam_id>` key in the P5 batch is the notification. The event names are fixed as `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and `ASSEMBLY_CUT` (Save/Load Open Question 24, answered 2026-10-27); until Save/Load enables events the baseline is interval plus QUIT plus initial. Assembly never calls Save/Load.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T` | int64 tick | 0..2^63 - 1; `cut_at_T` unset until cut | Layout `welds[]` | Apply ticks recorded by LM2 and LM3 |
| `last_shape_change_T` | int64 tick or NONE | monotonic non-decreasing in a run | calculated | Never a JSON number: two 32-bit parts or a string at the serialisation boundary (SL-2) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int | 1800 | Save/Load (cited) | Coalescing floor |
| `last_save_T`, `event_pending` | int64, bool | | Save/Load (cited) | |

**Output Range:** `NONE` or a tick. **Example:** the weld applies at `T` = 437400 (`welded_at_T`), `last_save_T` = 432000: `last_shape_change_T` = 437400, elapsed 5400 >= 1800, so `event_due` is true and an autosave follows. The cut applies at 440000: `last_shape_change_T` = 440000. With `welds[]` empty (no weld ever): `NONE`, no event.

### D.10 CASCADE deferral deadline `[VS]`

The cascade_defer_deadline formula is defined as:

`deadline_T = first_deferred_T + CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` ; at each tick `T`: if `actors_overlapping(rects)` is empty then APPLY, else if `T >= deadline_T` then ABANDON, else DEFER

Derived: `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` is specified and tested through a stub submitter; no MVP system submits one (MSA-22). It is preceded by the P2 tell window and by the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s gate (ENDOGENOUS transitions only), and **abandons, never forces**, on expiry (BDY-24).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `first_deferred_T` | int64 tick | | `assembly.state.cascade_deferred` (reserved, empty in MVP) | Tick of the first deferral |
| `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 60-1800, multiple of 6 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 600 `[VS]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | Expiry |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 | Graph (cited) | The fairness gate, a separate and independent knob |

**Output Range:** three outcomes (apply, defer, abandon). The equality of 600 ticks with `W_MIN_WARNING_S` x `TICK_RATE` (10 s x 60) is a coincidence of values, not a contract; no lint ties them. **Example:** `first_deferred_T` = 1000, default 600, `deadline_T` = 1600: the crewmate leaves the rectangles at 1210, the cut applies at 1210 (if the 10 s warning has elapsed); a crewmate who stays through 1600 gets ABANDON at 1600, the seam stays welded, the cut never fires.

### D.11 Pending TTL

The pending_ttl_ok formula is defined as:

`pending_ttl_ok = ( PENDING_TTL_TICKS >= ceil( QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK ) ) AND ( PENDING_TTL_TICKS <= MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION - 1 ) AND ( PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1 < min( WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS , SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS ) )` ; `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T = submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`

Derived: the three clauses are, in order, the kernel's never-spuriously-stale invariant (`KRN D.7`: `max_age_ticks(any type) >= ceil(QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK)`, a boot assertion), Assembly's ceiling that a completion never outlives a begin's own age window, and the **channel-floor constraint**: a new handoff can never meet an unresolved `pending`, because the next DONE is at least one whole channel after the next begin and `NOT_TAKEN` or the apply lands within `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` ticks of the submit. A fresh begin after a DONE has no ledger entry (IA-16 deletes it at DONE), so its remaining time is the full `D`. Both completion types set `max_age_ticks = PENDING_TTL_TICKS`, so the kernel's `STALE` rejection and Assembly's `NOT_TAKEN` land in the same tick order: P2 rejects at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`, then the P3 of that tick writes `NOT_TAKEN`. The result is that `DONE` and `NOT_TAKEN` are mutually exclusive (MSA-6).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | 8-119, default 12 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | Lifetime of a submitted completion |
| `QUEUE_CAPACITY` | int | 1024 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Command queue size |
| `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` | int | 128 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Dispatch cap per tick |
| `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` | int ticks | 120 | Kernel (cited, locked) | The age bound of an action command (a begin) |
| `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 240-480 | D.7 | Channel durations |
| `submit_T` | int64 tick | | `assembly.state.pending.submit_T` | When `assembly.step` submitted the completion |

**Output Range:** boolean at boot (a failing value fails the data lint); the default gives `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T = submit_T + 13`, about 0.22 s after submission. **Example:** `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12: `12 >= ceil(1024/128)` = 8 yes; `12 <= 119` yes; `13 < min(360, 240)` = 240 yes: valid. 7: fails the first clause (a legitimate completion held 8 ticks by queue truncation would be `STALE`). 119: valid, `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T` = `submit_T + 120`. 240: fails the second clause and the third (a new handoff could meet an unresolved `pending`: 241 < 240 is false). Under an injected backlog of 1 to 8 ticks the completion applies exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` is never written.

### D.12 Weld notification batch

The weld_batch_key_budget formula is defined as:

`keys_block = base_block + n_moved + n_actors + n_subject_deltas <= MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` ; checked per block, for each of `graph.state`, `layout.state`, `body.state`, `interaction.state`, `assembly.state`

Derived: the kernel batches one notification per block (CR-10). The Graph's batch is fixed by its owner (`T`, `S/<seam_id>`, `SEC/<a>`, `SEC/<b>`, nothing else); Layout adds `SEC/` and `BODY/` keys for moved sections; Body adds pose and actor keys; Interaction adds only subjects whose registration changed (ASK I1(vii)). Exceeding `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` collapses a block's batch to `ALL` and costs a full rebuild, so the budget is checked, not assumed.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `base_block` | int | graph 4; layout 5; body 2; interaction 0; assembly 1 per seat that changed | the owners' key sets | Fixed keys |
| `n_moved` | int | MVP 1 (derelict B); worst case <= `MAX_SECTIONS` 24 | Layout | Sections whose offset changed |
| `n_actors` | int | 0..`MAX_ACTORS` 8 | Body | Actors transferred by BM3 or BM4 |
| `n_subject_deltas` | int | MVP <= 4 (door faces) | Interaction `sync_section` | Subjects whose registered state changed |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | int | 256 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Collapse threshold |

**Output Range:** MVP weld: `graph.state` 4; `layout.state` <= 6 (the moved section's `SEC/` key coincides with `SEC/<b>`, so 5 is the likely union and 6 the bound); `body.state` <= 3 with one avatar (2 pose rows + 1 actor; <= 10 only at the `MAX_ACTORS` 8 cap); `interaction.state` <= 4; `assembly.state` <= 1 (one key per changed seat; only the welder's seat changes in an MVP weld). Worst case `layout.state` 5 + 24 = 29. All well under 256. **The one failure mode** is `sync_section` marking every subject of both sections (about 98 + 98 fixtures plus loose items and stubs, up to about 190 in the three-section case), which is why the ask is "mark only changed subjects". **Example:** home + B weld with one avatar on B: graph 4, layout 6, body 2 + 1 = 3, interaction 4, assembly 1: five batches, the largest 6 keys. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection on the apply tick are asserted by the Graph itself (LS-24) and cross-checked by AC-MSA-25 and AC-MSA-67; they are not re-owned here.

### D.13 Hatch-cap check (`CUT_BLOCKED`)

The cut_blocked formula is defined as:

`rects(seam) = { hatch_rect_a , hatch_rect_b }` with each of size `( WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH ) x ( width_tiles x TILE_PX )` in the body frame ; `cut_blocked = ( actors_overlapping( body_id , rects(seam) ) != empty )` with `ov( box , r ) = max(0, min(x1) - max(x0)) x max(0, min(y1) - max(y0)) > 0`

Derived: `actors_overlapping` is Body's pure read (`BDY D.11`, cited); Assembly supplies the two rectangles from Layout's hatch geometry (LY-15) and the verdict handling: a `CHANNEL` cut rejects `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at begin, handoff and completion, a `CASCADE` cut defers (D.10). The list includes the cutter. The geometry guarantee that the cutter's own foot box can stand outside both rectangles within reach is a Layout and Authoring requirement (`SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY`, ASK L2), not an Assembly term.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int (px) | 24 | art bible (registry `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`, value 24; Layout's hatch geometry consumes it) | Hatch plate depth |
| `TILE_PX` | int (px) | 48 | Pipeline (cited) | Tile size |
| `width_tiles` | int | 1-12; MVP 2 | Layout | Seam width: the plate is `width_tiles x 48` = 96 px long |
| `foot_box(a)` | half-open rect | 16 x 8 px (`FOOT_BOX_W` x `FOOT_BOX_H`) | Body `BDY D.2` | The actor's footprint at `pos_curr` |
| `REACH_PX` | int (px) | 72 | Controller (cited) | The interaction reach |

**Output Range:** boolean; the list is in ascending `actor_id`. **Example (MVP pair, derived from DA-10 and `LY D.6`; rectangle coordinates are `[UNV]` until Layout publishes the hatch rectangles):** `rect_a` = `[696, 720) x [672, 768)` (the home airlock's east hull plate), `rect_b` = `[720, 744) x [672, 768)` (B's west hull plate after the weld). A crewmate at `pos = (700, 700)` has box `[692, 708) x [700, 708)`; overlap with `rect_a` is `(708 - 696) x 8 = 96 > 0`: blocked. A cutter standing at `pos = (680, 720)` has box `[672, 688) x [720, 728)`: no overlap with either rectangle (8 px of clearance, one foot-box half-width, the margin ASK L2 asks Layout to guarantee) and within `REACH_PX` 72 of the hatch face (16 px away): not blocked. One foot-box further east, `pos = (696, 720)`, box `[688, 704)`: overlap with `rect_a` is `8 x 8 = 64`, blocked; this is exactly what AC-MSA-37 probes.

### D.14 Subject budget at the seam

The subject_budget_at_seam formula is defined as:

`S_total_after = S_total_before + n_subject_deltas` with the Interaction-owned lint `F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `P <= PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE <= 144`, `S_total <= MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`; **owned by Interaction (`IA D.10`, I-7); Assembly supplies only `n_subject_deltas` (door faces added or removed by the weld or cut)**

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_subject_deltas` | int | weld: <= +4; cut: <= -4 plus stubs <= `C` | Interaction `sync_section` | Door faces registered by the weld; removed (and RAGGED stubs created) by the cut |
| `F`, `L_seed`, `C`, `P`, `A`, `H` | int | 0..120, 0..20, 0..18, 0..24, <= 4, <= 2 (`HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX`) | Interaction (cited) | Fixtures, seeded loose items, cap stubs, player drops, actors, horrors |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` | int | 120, 24 | Interaction (cited) | The content lint |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | int | 192 | Emitter State (cited) | The hard cap |

**Output Range:** a content lint, not runtime state. Over the hard cap a subject fails dark, which for a seam door reads as a hallucinated dead door (a P4 breach by accident). **Example (Interaction's registered figures, `legal_loaded_sets`):** S3 (home + B welded) `S_int` 112 + `P` 24 + `A` 4 + `H` 2 = 142, 50 below 192; the seam's door faces are inside the registered S3 count. S4 `{home+B, A}`: `S_int` 144 (24 over `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120, WARN not ERROR; Interaction Open Question 30) + 30 = 174, 18 below 192. Which sections load is the decision of Web Map and Authoring, not the weld.

### D.15 Apply budget split

The apply_budget_split formula is defined as:

`weld_apply_ms = m2 + lm2 + bm3 + 2 x sync + own + verifier_release <= MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` ; `cut_apply_ms` has the same shape with `m3 + lm3 + bm4`; `install_apply_ms = check + m5 + release + clear + sync + own <= MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`

Derived: a documentation formula. The spike reports each term separately so that a failed budget points at an owner. The Graph's M2 alone is locked at <= 0.2 ms Release and sits inside the weld term.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m2`, `m3`, `m5` | float ms | `m2` <= 0.2 (locked) | Graph | Mutator cost |
| `lm2`, `lm3` | float ms | allocation targets, `[UNV]` | Layout | Mutator cost |
| `bm3`, `bm4` | float ms | `[UNV]`; up to `MAX_ACTORS` 8 transfers | Body | Mutator cost |
| `sync` | float ms | `[UNV]` | Interaction `sync_section` | One section re-registration |
| `own` | float ms | `[UNV]` | `assembly.state` writes | Assembly's own work |
| `verifier_release` | float ms | <= 0.05 (INV-1, INV-2, INV-7; O(seams <= 48)) | Assembly | Release-build verifier subset |
| `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | float ms | 0.5, 0.5, 0.2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | T9 | Budgets |

**Output Range:** the sum must not exceed its budget, and the whole weld tick (P2 plus P3) stays under `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, a logging threshold and not a cut-off. **Example (allocation targets, not measurements):** `m2` 0.20 + `lm2` 0.05 + `bm3` 0.05 + `2 x sync` 0.10 + `own` 0.01 + `verifier_release` 0.05 = 0.46 ms <= 0.5. The P5 subscribers (PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail) are outside the apply and have their own budget (`MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, PROJECTOR `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, no frame above `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33).

### D.16 Fault loss window

The fault_loss_window formula is defined as:

`fault_loss_ticks = T_fault - last_save_T` ; `fault_loss_ticks <= AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (10800 + 7200 = 18000 ticks, 5 minutes: the interval autosave is soft-gated, for example while `CHANNELLING` (SL-5), and a soft-blocked interval save may defer up to `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 before it is written anyway, so this sum is the ceiling); enabling the `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` events can only shorten the window, never lengthen it. The name is distinct from Save/Load's registered `loss_ticks` (`T_dead - T_g`, the death-rule loss), which measures a different quantity

Derived: a defect inside an Assembly `apply` moves the kernel `RUNNING -> FAULTED` (CR-9), and the only exit is a player-initiated restore of the last autosave. This is the price of "no rollback" stated as a number so a reviewer can accept or reject it. It is **not** the death rule (SL-7); it shares only the loading path.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | int ticks | 10800 | Save/Load (cited) | Interval autosave |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | Save/Load (cited) | Event coalescing floor (events can only add saves at least this far apart) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | 7200 | Save/Load (registry) | Longest wait of a soft-blocked interval save; after it the save is written anyway and tagged RISKY |
| `T_fault`, `last_save_T` | int64 ticks | | kernel; Save/Load | Tick of the defect; tick of the last autosave |

**Output Range:** 0 to 18000 ticks (5 minutes of play) worst case, 10800 (3 minutes) typical when no soft gate defers the interval save; with events enabled the window shrinks toward the event save but is not computed here. **Example:** a defect in A3 of a weld 2 minutes (7200 ticks) after the last interval autosave: the player loses up to 7200 ticks of play, within the 10800 typical ceiling and the 18000 worst case; the existing save file is never overwritten while FAULTED.

### D.17 Outcome cue gate

The outcome_cue_gate formula is defined as:

`cue_plays = ( last_result.T != last_seen_T ) AND ( T_now - last_result.T <= OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS )`

Derived: presentation consumes `last_result` once. `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so a restore never replays a cue, and a MIRROR join never plays a stale one. The outcome is presented by the verb family's one cue (MSA-34); the DONE outcome has its own completion cues (the bead locks, the burn-through).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `last_result.T` | int64 tick | >= 0 | `assembly.state` | Presenter dedupe key |
| `last_seen_T` | int64 tick | | presenter-local, not sim state | The last value the presenter acted on |
| `T_now` | int64 tick | | kernel | Current tick |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` | int ticks | 12-180, default 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file | Staleness bound |

**Output Range:** boolean per presenter per `last_result` change. **Example:** a `NOT_TAKEN` is written at `last_result.T` = 437412; the presenter last saw 437000; the frame at 437420 has `T_now - last_result.T` = 8 <= 60: the cue plays once. A frame at 437500 after a hitch: 88 > 60, no cue (a stale cue after a hitch would read as a new failure). After a restore `last_result` is empty and no cue plays.

### D.18 Welded-body capacity bounds

The welded_body_capacity formula is defined as:

`C_welded = sum over sections s in body of compartments(s) <= 12` (the Graph's LS-26 bound, read as the welded body, advisor R26) ; `nodes_loaded <= MAX_GRAPH_NODES` ; `edges_loaded + plan_edges <= MAX_GRAPH_EDGES`

Derived: Assembly reads LS-26's "<= 12 compartments at the weld" as the **welded body's** compartment count (advisor R26). Whether LS-26 also bounds the loaded set `{home+B, A}` is the Graph's call (ASK G4). `CAPACITY` is a `can_join_static` reason; at MVP scale it is not at risk.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compartments(s)` | int | per template | Layout / Authoring | Home 5, derelict A 4, derelict B 5 |
| `MAX_GRAPH_NODES`, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` | int | 4096, 8192 | Graph (cited, locked) | Capacity |
| `plan_edges` | int | MVP 3 (1 OPENING + 1 CABLE + 1 DUCT), provisional on the Graph's plan shape | `socket_plan` | Edges the weld adds (nothing else) |

**Output Range:** boolean lint. **Example:** home 5 + B 5 = `C_welded` 10 <= 12; the loaded set including A is 14 (ASK G4). Graph load: about 105 nodes of 4096 and a few hundred edges of 8192 (the Graph's own estimate), plus 3 edges for the weld.

## Edge Cases

Grouped by theme. Each case states the exact outcome and the rule that decides it. "Site 1/2/3" are the three call sites of the one predicate (MSA-7); "the sputter" is the weld family's one non-DONE cue and "the gutter" the cut family's (MSA-34). Ids `E-n` are stable references for the Acceptance Criteria.

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **E-1. If the data file ships `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 1000 or 399**: the loader clamps it to 990 or 400 at load, raises `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` once, and every weld uses the clamped value (D.1). Playing on a q-1000 weld would delete the weld's weep (the P1 tell); playing on a q-399 weld would leak at birth with no tell (a P2 break). The clamp runs at load, never per weld, so no weld can disagree with another.
- **E-2. If `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` or `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` is not a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, or is outside 240-480**: the data lint fails at boot and the game does not start with that file (IA I-2 and the analogy to I-9). A channel that does not span whole Graph blocks would credit a partial block, and a duration below 240 would leave the LONG bar class.
- **E-3. If `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` is outside 8-119, or at or above `min(WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS, SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS) - 1`**: `pending_ttl_ok` (D.11) is false and the boot lint fails. Below 8 a legitimate completion held back by the kernel's per-tick truncation would be `STALE`; at 240 a new handoff could meet an unresolved `pending`.
- **E-4. If a `plan` would take the welded body above 12 compartments or the Graph above `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 or `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` 8192**: `can_join_static` returns `CAPACITY` at site 1 and the player gets the standard rejection cue (structural reasons are IMMEDIATE). The MVP welded body is 10 compartments and about 105 of 4096 nodes, so this is an authoring defect in MVP, not a play outcome (D.18).
- **E-5. If a `begin_*` is pressed while `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for that seat**: it is rejected `BUSY` at site 1 and nothing is queued or opened. Together with the channel-floor constraint (D.11) this makes it impossible for a new handoff to meet an unresolved `pending`: the completion of the previous channel has either applied or resolved to `NOT_TAKEN` within 13 ticks.
- **E-6. If the simulation runs with zero seats (a headless test, a menu state)**: `assembly.step` iterates an empty map, submits nothing and costs the idle budget only; `assembly.state` is `default_state()`. No Assembly path assumes a seat exists.

### Simultaneous and ordering cases

- **E-7. If two seats stand at the two different sockets of the one pair and both reach DONE on the same tick**: `assembly.step` submits two `weld` completions in ascending PlayerId order; the first applies; the second's `validate` fails `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` (both sockets are already `WELDED`), the command is Rejected, `pending` stays set and the second seat's outcome resolves as `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + 13` with the weld sputter. Never a fault. The same socket cannot be contended at all: IA-34 gives the second seat `BUSY` at begin (one job per subject and verb class), so a double DONE needs the two *different* sockets (MSA-11 allows either to be focused); optional ASK I1(x) would close even that.
- **E-8. If the two seats' DONEs fall on consecutive ticks**: the second seat's handoff pre-check (site 2) sees the sockets `WELDED` and writes `REJECTED(SOCKET_NOT_FREE)`, submitting nothing, with the weld sputter. If the first completion is still queued (backlog), both completions are submitted and the first-queued applies, the other becomes `NOT_TAKEN` as in E-7. The welded state is single in every ordering (AC-MSA-59).
- **E-9. If a weld or cut applies in the same tick as a Graph hook tick (`T mod 6 == 0`)**: the apply runs in P2 and the three `graph.propagate.*` hooks in P3, so the first propagate sees the bumped `topology_version` and the new seam; no half-state is observable. Assembly neither registers nor orders against the Graph's hooks.
- **E-10. If an earlier command in the same P2 queue changes the target (a crewmate's salvage completion removes `j_b_main`)**: P2 order is `(queued_tick, host_order)`, so the earlier command applies first; the weld's `validate` then sees the new state, and the full `can_join` in step A0 reads `PORT_ABSENT`. The result is `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`: both orderings are legal and deterministic.
- **E-11. If a breach opens in either section during the weld channel**: the channel does not abort (the abort set is closed, IA-15) and the weld still applies; the new seam opens with hatch area 0, so nothing crosses until the player opens the hatch, and the full `can_join` at apply step A0 reports the larger `surge[]` (no Assembly presenter reads it; the creak is the Graph's `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor, VA-16), never a number. The player who wants to patch first simply moves: the ledger holds the progress for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600.
- **E-12. If a crewmate stands on the absorbed (derelict B) side in an unrelated channel when a weld applies**: BM3 transfers every actor on the absorbed body, which counts as a body transfer, so the Controller clears their `channel_ref` and Interaction aborts their channel with cause `BODY`, crediting any finished blocks (IA-14 row 1). No special case in Assembly; the welder on B sees no displacement when `docked` held.
- **E-13. If a crewmate steps into a hatch rectangle during a seam-cut channel**: the channel continues (a hazard is not an abort cause) and, at DONE, site 2 finds `actors_overlapping` non-empty and writes `REJECTED(CUT_BLOCKED)` with the gutter; the `LOUD` already went out at DONE, the seam stays welded and nothing is submitted. If the crewmate enters in the single tick between the handoff (P3 of `T_done`) and the completion (P2 of `T_apply`), site 3 rejects instead and the seat resolves as `NOT_TAKEN` 13 ticks after submission, with the same cue. "The ship will not close a seam on a crewmate" holds at all three sites.
- **E-14. If the cutter's own foot box overlaps a hatch rectangle**: the cut prompt is still offered (`seam_cut_offer` has no `CUT_BLOCKED` term, D.2) and begin is rejected `CUT_BLOCKED` with the standard rejection cue; stepping back out of the rectangle restores a valid begin. This is why Layout and Authoring must guarantee a legal stance at least one foot-box half-extent outside both rectangles within `REACH_PX` 72 (ASK L2, AC-MSA-37); without it the cut would be permanently self-blocked.

### Target and state changes during the channel

- **E-15. If the target lane node is salvaged before or during the weld channel (`j_main` or `j_b_main`, both def-backed; the bare `dj_*` nodes cannot be salvaged, DA-19)**: begin and the handoff pass (module-body state is never read there), the completion `validate` passes, and step A0 reads `PORT_ABSENT`: `last_result = BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, nothing else mutates, no `weld_join` call, the weld sputters. The cost is the six seconds; the world witness is the empty bay footprint at the lane node (LY-20), reachable within one walk.
- **E-16. If the weld is attempted onto a vacuum, frozen or dead section**: the weld is allowed and moves nothing (fresh seam: hatch area 0, valves closed, breaker open). The closed hatch creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`) or frosts at the rim; the player decides when to open it. The weld is never refused for a hazard, only for structure (P1: the hazard is told by the hatch, not the prompt).
- **E-17. If `docked` stops holding during the 360-tick channel**: with Web Map & Transit's hold (ASK T1) this cannot happen. Without it, the handoff pre-check (site 2) re-tests `docked` and writes `REJECTED(NOT_DOCKED)` with the sputter, costing the six seconds. Under the BDY-27 camera-cut fallback (the offer drops the `docked` term) the weld applies and the absorbed hull jumps by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer only, hidden by the Pipeline's `BODY_TRANSFER` camera cut. Site 3 deliberately does not re-test `docked` (BDY-27: weld legality takes no pose input).
- **E-18. If the target section is dormant (`SECTION_NOT_LOADED`)**: the prompt may still show (the offer reads no join structure) and begin is rejected with the standard rejection cue; no channel opens and no time is spent. Dormancy is a section flag, not module-body state, so it is IMMEDIATE.
- **E-19. If a weld is attempted while the seat carries a module**: no weld prompt exists (the carry lockout, PC-7 / IA-28, is Interaction's selector) and a forced `begin_weld` is `BUSY`. The player puts the module down or installs it; a torch needs two hands.
- **E-20. If the seam hatch is opened during a cancelled-and-resumed cut**: the ledger keeps the progress, but the mode is re-derived at completion, so the resumed cut is RAGGED (D.6). The open leaf and the draught are the witness; the player who wants a clean cut closes the hatch first.
- **E-21. If a RAGGED cut applies**: the Graph creates `A_CUT` 24 holes on both sides (including the home side), hull stubs `hp` 2500 and `open_end` ports; `sync_section` must create `stubs[]` entries for every uncapped `open_end` or the O2-lane breach could never be capped, which would break P2 fairness (ASK I1(vii)). The cut is EXOGENOUS and player-caused, so it is exempt from `W_MIN_WARNING_S`; its tell was the world witnesses before the press. A 20-tile room decompresses in 382 steps (38 s), during which the player caps stubs (V8's follow-up `begin_cap`) or leaves.
- **E-22. If a keep-side cut leaves a crewmate on the leaving piece**: they are stranded on the detached body, which drifts without rotation. Counterplay (MSA-18): the 4 s lit, loud channel is the tell, and a crewmate who opens the hatch from the far side and stands in the passage before completion makes the cut `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (AC-MSA-123); a crewmate who only opens the hatch makes the cut RAGGED. If no one is in the passage the cut proceeds and there is no blanket far-side gate in MVP and Vertical Slice A (Q4: "death is a bitch; it happens"; in solo play a gate is vacuous). Recovery is Web Map & Transit's (ASK T1, ASK T2). Vertical Slice B revisits it as `CUT_STRANDS`: **conditional**, no gate only if Transit ships a recovery path and the far-side tell is confirmed, otherwise a sanity-gated rule like P4(c) (MSA-18, Open Question 2).
- **E-53. If derelict B is welded into the home body** *(added after E-52 and kept last in this group; id kept stable for AC-MSA-122)*: the Graph's section record keeps `kind` DERELICT and so `rate_pm` 2000 (LS-17, LS-25: the rate is keyed on `kind`, and `weld_join`, LS-15, writes no section field). Endogenous wear and overload on B's nodes and edges therefore run at twice the home-ship rate after the weld. Every endogenous transition still passes the `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate, so the tell is kept. Assembly writes no section field and adds no rule (MSA-2). Whether a graft keeps derelict compression is the Graph's call (ASK G7).

### Degenerate strategies

- **E-23. If the player cancels and restarts a channel repeatedly to "peek" at whether it would work**: the offer is state-independent for module-body state, so the peek reveals nothing; each start costs TOOL noise per accrued tick, up to `B - 1` = 5 ticks of each cancelled block are wasted (D.7), and the ledger lets the work resume only inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600. There is no free information and no free progress.
- **E-24. If the player welds, strips derelict B by V8 salvage, then cuts it away**: this is an intended healthy line (the "extraction" fantasy). Each salvage costs noise and leaves open ends to cap (Interaction's), each module is salvage-once (IDB-17), the cut is `LOUD`, and nothing is minted (conservation).
- **E-25. If the player tries to weld, cut and weld again to reset a damaged seam or farm a section**: the second weld returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` with no prompt; sockets go `FREE -> WELDED -> CAPPED | CUT_OPEN` and never back (LY-13). The playtest gate must not require a second weld (AC-MSA-41).
- **E-26. If the player cuts a section holding a hostile "to kill it"**: a RAGGED cut breaches both sides including the home side, so it is self-harm; there is no force or knockback (BDY-28). A cut removes only a threat the player is leaving behind. Using the cut to escape an infested section is healthy P5 play, with the Attention owner's rule that a cut moves the locus and does not reset the meter (ASK A1).
- **E-27. If the player stands on the derelict side of a welded seam with the cutter**: no cut prompt exists, because the seat's foot-origin section is not in the component that keeps its body (`seam_cut_offer`, D.2). The self-strand trap (alone on a drifting, sealed body with no free socket and no recovery path in MVP) is unreachable from a prompt; a forced `begin_seam_cut` is rejected `NOT_ELIGIBLE`.
- **E-28. If the player presses the install prompt repeatedly on a bay**: each press on an occupied slot resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` with no time cost, so a row of bays can be scanned by pressing. The residual one-bit oracle is accepted (Q6: no lockout; the carry to the bay is already the price; revisit at playtest, Open Questions). The opposite direction (a bay that looks occupied but is empty) installs the module irreversibly, so the probe is asymmetric but not a faucet.

### Save, restore, pause and faults

- **E-29. If a restore (load) happens mid-channel**: `interaction.state.post_restore` drops the channel (abort cause `RESTORE`) and `assembly.state.post_restore` clears `pending` and `last_result` after it (`restore_after`); no orphan `pending`, no cue, no half-bead. The world is as of the save.
- **E-30. If a snapshot is taken in the single tick between the handoff (P3 of `T`) and the completion (P2 of `T+1`)**: the snapshot holds `pending` while the channel record is already consumed; the restore clears `pending`, no weld happened, and the player redoes the six seconds. Restore-then-continue is equal to uninterrupted play wherever MSA-38 says it is (no open ASSEMBLY channel record and no `pending`); this tick is one of its two asserted differences (the other is a dropped channel record, E-29), both asserted explicitly by AC-MSA-61. Optional ASK S1(a) (`pending_any()`) closes it.
- **E-31. If an autosave comes due during a self-inflicted RAGGED decompression**: it may be written. SL-5's soft row (a) counts only ENDOGENOUS descriptors and a RAGGED cut's stubs are EXOGENOUS (LS-16, no gate), so the Graph does not defer the save; only the hard DOWNED/DEAD gate applies. A restore there returns the player to the post-cut breach with its stubs to cap, not to a lost run (optional ASK S1(b)).
- **E-32. If the game is paused between the DONE tick and the apply**: nothing advances (no P2, no P3, no hook submits; queued commands wait for the first RUNNING tick, CR-16), the TTL and the ledger are in ticks and stop; resume continues as if there had been no pause.
- **E-33. If the completion is rejected at admission (`QUEUE_FULL`) or in P2**: `pending` stays set, no mutation happened, and `assembly.step` writes `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`, 13 ticks after submission (superseding the "NOT_TAKEN at T+1" of the earlier proposals); the player gets the family's cue, the world is unchanged and they re-hold E. The queue depth is far above any MVP traffic, so this is a defence.
- **E-34. If a completion is held in the queue for k ticks, 1 <= k <= 8 (per-tick truncation)**: `pending` stays until the apply clears it; `DONE` is written exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` never, because the kernel's `STALE` bound and the TTL coincide (D.11).
- **E-35. If a defect makes an Assembly `apply` throw, or the release verifier reports INV-1, INV-2 or INV-7 after an apply**: the kernel moves to FAULTED, the queue drops with `Dropped(FAULTED)`, no autosave is written over an existing save, and the only exit is a player-initiated restore of the last autosave; the loss window (`fault_loss_ticks`) is up to 18000 ticks (5 minutes) worst case, 10800 typical (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` + `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, D.16). This is not Save/Load's death rule (SL-7).
- **E-36. If a restore loads a save in which `welds[]` has an `ACTIVE` row but the Graph has no matching SeamRecord**: INV-1 fails in `assembly.state`'s `post_restore`, the kernel goes LOADING -> FAULTED naming `assembly.state` and INV-1; the save file is untouched; the game never plays on the inconsistent world.

### Hallucination interplay (MVP class (b); classes (a), (c), (d) are Vertical Slice and the rules are written now)

- **E-37. If the seam valve renders CLOSED while it is truly OPEN**: the cut is RAGGED, because the mode is derived from true positions. **Under the MSA-23 fallback this render cannot happen at the seam**: the seam's own valve and breaker sprites are TRUTH and the Filter may not wrap them until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a zero-gradient lane witness; the case stays specified so the derivation is proven independent of perception (a Perception stub that wraps the sprite anyway, AC-MSA-104, is a Filter defect caught by AC-MSA-126). Once the fallback lifts, the lie is falsifiable beforehand by the witness at the lane (the hiss under a gradient, the zero-gradient lane witness the owners confirmed); a player who looked got a clean cut or a decision, and one who did not took a breach that had a witness (MSA-23).
- **E-38. If the hardpoint renders EMPTY while a module is seated**: the install prompt still appears, because it depends only on what the player carries; the press resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` with the install bump and the module stays in the hands. The bay's footprint (truth channel) shows the occupant on a closer look. The cost is a walk at carry gait, not a free oracle.
- **E-39. If the hardpoint renders OCCUPIED while it is truly empty**: the prompt appears, the install succeeds and the module is consumed in a bay the player meant to avoid (irreversible: no `remove_module`). Falsify beforehand with the bay footprint.
- **E-40. If a hallucinated "welded" seam is drawn over a socket pair that is truly `FREE`** (Perception Filter, VS): the weld offer and every verdict read true Layout state, the geometry of the real seam is never wrapped, and the prompt `E . Weld` appearing over a "welded" seam is itself the contradiction that falsifies the lie. The Filter may not suppress or fake the prompt (ASK F1).
- **E-41. If a crewmate in the hatch renders as a horror (class (c), VS-B)**: `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence, not perceived identity, so a deluded player cannot seal a crewmate into a cap; the kept oracle costs one attempt and no time and the Filter must never wrap it.
- **E-42. If the module the player carried home is a phantom (class (d), VS-A)**: the install offer is computed from the true carried def, so no prompt appears at a bay it does not fit; the trip home is one fallback reveal and the cheaper field checks are other owners'.

### Co-op, MIRROR, zoom and engine-facing cases

- **E-43. If a MIRROR client joins while a `last_result` is pending presentation**: `last_result` arrives through `SHARED_SUMMARY`; the presenter acts only if `last_result.T` differs from its last seen value and lies within `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 of now (D.17), so a join never replays a stale cue. `assembly.step` does not run on a MIRROR (no authoritative tick); the crewmate's weld in progress is visible through Interaction's replicated channel record (the human channel, P1).
- **E-44. If a crewmate stands in the hatch to veto a cut (griefing, VS-B)**: the cut is rejected `CUT_BLOCKED` and nothing else happens; the cost to the griefer is standing in a doorway. Recorded for Vertical Slice B; the ship never closes a seam on anyone.
- **E-45. If the player zooms in or out on the integer ladder during a channel**: nothing in the rules depends on zoom; the in-world cursor and bead scale with the world viewport, the prompt plate and linear bar keep their HUD pixel size, offsets stay on the 24-px grid and `abs` is unchanged (R28). Tests run at `k` = 1 and `k_auto + 2` (AC-MSA-64).
- **E-46. If several ticks run in one render frame (catch-up, `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) across a weld**: the apply runs in its own tick; the P5 subscribers (PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail) coalesce to the latest committed state and never replay each tick's change, so the weld frame costs one rebuild.
- **E-47. If the weld happens on a moving body (`vel` up to `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16)**: `docked` is relative (equal velocity and the offset), BM3 conserves each actor's `abs`, the survivor's velocity is unchanged and no force is applied (BDY-28). Moving bodies are exercised only by the spike (AC-MSA-72); MVP play welds a docked pair.
- **E-48. If a weld is followed later by a cut of the same seam**: `pos_curr` is not restored to its pre-weld value (the cut subtracts `frame_shift_px` and zeroes the sub-pixel part); `abs` and section-local position are conserved, which is what tests assert (BDY-23).
- **E-49. If `sync_section` returns with `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW` counted on the weld tick**: the weld stays applied (the call is total and fails dark by design); the defect counter rises and the content lint is the fix. For a seam door this reads as a hallucinated dead door, which is why the lint, not the runtime, is the defence (MSA-16, D.14).
- **E-50. If `assembly.step` and `interaction.step` are registered in the wrong order or one is unknown**: boot fails (CR-3: an unknown hook id or a cycle) rather than reading the DONE handoff a tick late; the `after` edge is covered by a test and printable by the Debug Overlay.
- **E-51. If a glowstick (Chemlight) lights the socket instead of the lamp**: whether it satisfies `lit` for the channel is the Lighting and Interaction owners' rule; if it does, the weld proceeds with the lamp pointed elsewhere, a legitimate "weld by glowstick" line that needs no Assembly special case. The sparks are non-emissive and never the light source.
- **E-52. If the detached piece after a cut holds no actor**: it persists as an orphan Layout body with a Body pose row (Body BDY-4); Assembly neither unloads nor discards it (Web Map & Transit's, MSA-24). It counts toward `MAX_BODIES`, which is why D.8 includes it.

## Dependencies

Dependencies are bidirectional: every row below names what the counterparty must repeat in its own GDD, and the CONFLICT and ASK rows are refreshes owed to neighbour owners. This GDD never edits a neighbour; each row quotes the locked text and proposes the replacement. Status values: **designed** (file carries "Designed"), **designed, asks open**, **undesigned-provisional** (no file; the contract is stated here and the named GDD must repeat it).

**Index edge check.** The index lists seven dependencies for this system (kernel, Graph, Item DB, Interaction, Layout, Body, Save/Load). This GDD adds soft edges the index lacks: Player Controller & Input (PC-7, PC-8), Suit & Equipment (`release_carried`), Attention (no push; a conflict row), Derelict Authoring (content), and a Hard edge for the *offer* only to Web Map & Transit (`docked`). A refresh is owed to the index owner (the index is not edited here).

| Life-Support Graph | Assembly depends on | **Hard** | M2, M3, M5; `can_join_static`, full `can_join`, `seam_view`, `check_install_module`; the M2 return | designed, asks open (G1-G7) | LS-15 / LS-16 / the Graph's dependency row; the Graph's own request that each provisional row be repeated in the dependent GDD |

**What the dependency sets do not do.** There is no circular hard dependency: the Graph is pure data and Assembly mutates it only through the command layer (index soft cycle "Life-Support Graph <-> Modular Ship Assembly"), Layout and Body read nothing from Assembly, and Interaction reads one Assembly view (`weld_offer`, `seam_cut_offer`) injected as a lookup while Assembly calls Interaction's mutators, which is a mutual reference at data level but acyclic by restore and hook order (`assembly.step` after `interaction.step`).

## Tuning Knobs

Every number below is data-driven (a data file, never hardcoded). Assembly owns few knobs on purpose: the Graph owns what a weld does to the world, Interaction what the channel costs the web, Layout where sockets sit. **★ marks a registry candidate** (a cross-system fact another GDD reads or must obey); every new name is `[PLACEHOLDER]` until the playtest gate or the spike sets it. Knobs owned elsewhere are in T18 with a pointer to their source of truth and are never restated with a new value (a different value is a CONFLICT, not a tweak here). Tick values are 60 per second; ticks that must span whole Graph blocks are multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6.

**T17 — Assembly-owned knobs.**

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` ★ | 360 (6 s) | ticks, multiple of 6 | 240-480 | over 8 s: standing lit and still becomes tedium and a punishment, breaks the IA I-9 analogy (a LONG bar is at most 2 x `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`) | under 240: leaves the LONG bar class; the weld becomes a tap and loses its P3 exposure | Interaction's TOOL noise per accrued tick (`U_TOOL`, Attention units; `NOISE_PER_TICK` is the Controller's walk/run/carry table and has no TOOL entry), `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600, D.7, D.11 channel-floor constraint |
| `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` ★ | 240 (4 s) | ticks, multiple of 6 | 240-480 | over 8 s: a panicked escape cut becomes infeasible, the P5 exit verb is lost | under 240: leaves the LONG class; the destructive verb becomes cheaper than the careful one | one `LOUD` at DONE (Interaction), D.7; shares 240 with `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` by coincidence, a distinct name; deliberately faster than the weld, revisit if players use the cut as a free panic button |
| `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` ★ | 850 | permille | 400-990 at `LEAK_HP` 4000 (clamped at load to `[Q_MIN, 990]`, `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`; the effective floor is `Q_MIN` = `max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))` of D.1, so it rises to 600 at `LEAK_HP` 6000 and the data lint keeps 400 as the outer floor at `LEAK_HP` 2000) | 1000: `A_seam` 0, no weep, the weld's P1 tell vanishes (upper cliff) | below 400: `hp_seam` under `LEAK_HP`, every weld leaks at birth with no tell (lower cliff) | Graph `seam_at_weld`, `A_SEAM_MAX`, `LEAK_HP`, `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP`; never displayed; no modifier slot |
| `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` ★ | 12 | ticks | 8-119 and `+1 < min(WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS, SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS)` | up to 119: the cue for a dropped completion arrives late; at or past 239 a new handoff could meet an unresolved `pending` | under 8: violates the kernel's never-stale boot invariant; a legitimate completion could be `STALE`, giving a false `NOT_TAKEN` followed by a late `DONE` | `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK`, `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, D.11 `pending_ttl_ok` |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | 60 | ticks | 12-180 | a stale failure cue plays after a hitch and reads as a new failure | slow presenters miss the cue | `last_result.T`, D.17, Adaptive Audio and HUD presenters |
| `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` ★ `[VS]` | 600 | ticks, multiple of 6 | 60-1800 | a seam condemned for too long while a crewmate stands in the hatch | abandons before a crewmate can clear the hatch | `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (independent), the 10-30 s tell window, D.10; unused in MVP (stub submitter only) |
| `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` ★ (asked of Layout and Authoring) | 8 (one foot-box half-extent) | px | 8-24 | the stance band between the hatch rectangles and `REACH_PX` 72 may be empty, so the cut is unreachable | under 8: the cutter's own foot box can overlap a hatch rectangle and block their own cut at all three sites | `FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `REACH_PX` 72, `WELD_SOCKET` anchor placement, lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY`, AC-MSA-37 |
| `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` | 60 | s | 15-180 | the manual AC asks players to remember a seam they looked at too long ago | the AC becomes a reflex test | AC-MSA-39 only; a manual-AC parameter, not a sim knob, unrelated to `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` |
| `VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` | 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | weld cycles | 2-6 | the Vertical Slice content bill grows with sockets (Layout's three options) | 1 is the MVP economy and fails the gate; the P5 loop stays unrepeatable | AC-MSA-125 only; a Vertical Slice gate parameter, not a sim knob; Layout's `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` lint (CD-GDD-ALIGN) |
| `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.5 | ms (Release) | 0.2-2.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | Graph M2 <= 0.2 locked and inside it; D.15 |
| `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.5 | ms (Release) | 0.2-2.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | adds mode derivation and stub creation; D.15 |
| `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.2 | ms (Release) | 0.1-1.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | one node, one subject; D.15 |
| `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` | 0.02 | ms (Release) | 0.005-0.1 | `assembly.step` runs 60 times a second, so 0.02 ms costs 1.2 ms per second of play | false failures | seat count 0-4 |
| `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` | 8 | ms | 4-16 | the camera cut hides at most a one-to-two-frame hitch | spike fails on the first rebuild | PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail rebuilds (D.15) |
| `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` `[UNV]` | 4 | ms | 1-8 | hitch visible | spike fails | Layout ADR-LAY-03 (PROJECTOR shape); Pipeline C2 / C5 camera cut |
| `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` | 33 | ms | 17-50 | the seam visibly stutters | none relevant | Pipeline camera cut at `BODY_TRANSFER` |
| `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` | set in the spike (working figure 5, `[UNV]`) | microseconds per call | set by the spike | the hot-path read, evaluated every tick for a focused socket, becomes a frame cost | false failures | cached static half, O(1) `docked`; AC-MSA-17 |

*Not tabled, by decision.* `Q_MIN` is derived from `LEAK_HP` (D.1), not a knob. `MSA_COMPLETION_MAX_AGE_TICKS` 2 is rejected (it violates the kernel's `max_age_ticks >= 8` boot invariant). `INSTALL_BLOCKED_LOCKOUT_TICKS` is not adopted (Q6; the residual oracle is an Open Question). The keep-side cut rule and the hatch-cap rule are **rules** (MSA-18, MSA-21), not toggles: a switch that re-enables the self-strand trap is not a tuning choice. There is no `q` modifier, no weld-skill knob and no tool-tier knob (anti-pillar).

| Knob | Value | Owner and source of truth | Owner's safe range | What Assembly relies on |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` | 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per run (MVP); 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A) | Graph | n/a (measured by the Graph's spike S1) | a weld must not push the next `graph.propagate.*` over budget |
| `WELD_DP_WARN` | 300 dkPa | Graph (registry) | 100-900 dkPa | the boolean `surge[].warn` and `SEAL_STRAIN`; never a magnitude |
| `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` | 10000 | Graph (registry) | 0-10000 | the fresh seam's weep |

| `A_CUT`, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` | 24, 500 | Graph (`OPEN_END_RATE_PM` registry) | 8-48 / 100-1000 | the RAGGED breach (D.6) |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | 10 s | Graph | 4-20 s | the CASCADE gate (D.10) |
| `LEAK_HP`, `HP_OK` | 4000, 7000 | Graph (registry) | 2000-6000, 5000-9000 | `Q_MIN` (D.1) |
