| `MAX_GRAPH_NODES`, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` | 4096, 8192 | Graph (registry: `MAX_GRAPH_NODES`; `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` in the Graph tuning table) | 1024-8192 / 2048-16384 | `CAPACITY` (D.18) |
| `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION` | 8 | Graph | 4-16 | `SEAM_LIMIT` |
| `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION`, `MAX_SECTIONS`, `MAX_BODIES`, `SOCKET_CLASS` | 4, 24, 8, 1 (DOCK_STD) | Layout (registry) | 1-8 (never above `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION`), 6-48, 2-16, append-only ids | D.2, D.8 |
| `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` | 36000 | Layout (registry) | 3600-216000 | bead age presentation |
| `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | 2D Lighting (registry) | 2-8 | D.8 zero-margin risk; ASK LT1 |
| `JOLT_PX` | 0 | Body | 0-2 (reduced motion forces 0) | Assembly adds no shake |
| `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`, `MAX_ACTORS` | 16, 8 | Body (registry) | 1-16 (`[PLACEHOLDER]`), 4-16 | spike item, D.12 |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS`, `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` | 600, 6, 240 | Interaction (registry) | per Interaction (cited 180-1200), {1, 2, 3, 6}, 180-300 | ledger, lit recheck, LONG bar floor |
| `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 240 | Interaction (registry) | 180-480 | distinct name from `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | 120, 24, 192 | Interaction; Emitter State | `<= 144 - PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, 12-48, 128-384 | D.14 |
| `REACH_PX`, `FOOT_BOX_W`, `FOOT_BOX_H` | 72, 16, 8 | Controller (registry) | 48-96, 8-24 (even), 4-16 | D.13 |
| `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD` | Attention's units | Attention | 2-6 AU, `30 x U_TOOL` to 400 AU | Assembly pushes none; the price of a weld is `D` TOOL ticks |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | 10800, 1800, 7200 | Save/Load (registry) | 3600-36000, 900-5400, per Save/Load | D.9, D.16 (the fault loss ceiling is interval + max defer) |
| `SPIKE_SEQUENCES` | 1000 | Graph (LSG acceptance count) | `[UNV]` (name not found in the Graph's tuning table or the registry; the Graph's acceptance count is cited, not a tuned value) | AC-MSA-67 |

**Interactions worth tuning together.**

- `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` x Interaction's TOOL units is the P3 price of the first graft: lengthening the channel raises Attention exposure linearly while a movement cancel wastes at most 5 ticks (D.7), so the knob has no cliff, only a slope. Tune it against the playtest signal "the player hesitates, then commits", not against a time target.
- `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` must be re-checked (D.11) whenever `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` or either channel duration is retuned.
- `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` stays fixed in MVP; its range exists so the Graph's q-dependent paths can be tested at 400 and 990, not so that play varies it.
- `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` and `MAX_BODIES` bound D.8; any change to the socket economy (Layout Open Question 1) re-opens the zero margin.

**Playtest targets (not knobs).** Median time from the weld prompt first appearing to the channel beginning at most 45 s; median time from the bead to the first seam-breaker close 60-180 s; at least 4 of 5 players can say beforehand whether a cut will be clean; both the weld line and the strip line taken by at least 2 of 5 players. All `[PLACEHOLDER]`, owned by the playtest script, and listed again in the Acceptance Criteria.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Source: the art-director's proposal (VA-1 to VA-28), reconciled with MSA-1 to MSA-42 and the checkpoint rulings C-P1 to C-P20. ART = `design/art/art-bible.md`; IA = `interaction-system.md`; FT = `failure-and-trail-system.md`; LY = `ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md`; BDY = `ship-body-and-interior-physics.md`; PIPE = `pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera.md`; LSG = `life-support-graph.md`. Priority: **P** = MVP, **V** = Vertical Slice, **A** = Alpha. `[UNV]` = unverified number, measured in the spike. Audio columns state existence and sync only; layers, ducking, fades and triggers belong to the audio-director and Adaptive Audio (undesigned, provisional).*

### Scope: what Assembly's art owns and what it cites

Guard-rail 1 of the rulings applies to art as much as to rules. Assembly owns *which existing piece appears on which transition*, the three outcome cues, the install seat, the in-world socket cursor and the screenshot list. Everything else is cited and never redefined here.

| Visual | Owner (cited, not redefined) | Assembly's share |
|---|---|---|
| Seam bead geometry, fresh and old, straight, corner and T | Layout presentation (LY-15, LY-33); ART 6.1, 8.2 (4-px Hull Grey) | which transition shows which state; the ART 4.4 sampling test |
| Hatch plate (`FREE`), door jambs and leaf (`WELDED`), cap plate (`CAPPED`), ragged piece (`CUT_OPEN`) | Layout (LY-15; no per-state tilesets, ART 2.0) | the look rules for the cap plate and the ragged piece, since ART 6.1 says only "a cap plate and nothing beyond it" |
| Seam weep, `SEAL_STRAIN` bowed rim and rim frost | FT-14 (rows 24, 26; `trail_hull_seamweep_*`, `trail_hull_rimbow_s`, `trail_cool_rimfrost_s`) | the requirement that the weep is visible at birth and that an as-welded seam with no gradient is quiet (MSA-23) |
| Prompt plate, linear LONG bar, needle-mark, work loop, tool sparks, ACK, abort and DONE frames | IA (V-1 to V-12) | the verb tokens and the three post-channel non-DONE cues (MSA-34, C-P10) |
| RAGGED breach: stubs, hiss, alarm sweep, venting trails | Graph (LS-16), FT, IA stub sprite | nothing drawn by Assembly; the ragged plate is Layout's |
| Camera cut on a body transfer; detach translation without rotation | PIPE C2, C5 | the requirement that nothing visible jumps on a docked weld |
| Lane pipes, valve wheel, breaker lever, junction boxes | shared pipe set (ART 3.3, 6.3), IA V3, V4, V5 | the dead-until-connected state of the lanes at birth |
| Carried module sprite, drop prompt | Suit, IA V-13 | the install prompt and the bump and seat frames |

Assembly adds **no** new hue, no new HUD element class, no meter, no icon layer and no new tell vocabulary. If a row below seems to need one, the row is wrong.

### Events: visual and audio feedback

Class is TRUTH or CORRUPTIBLE per the classification table below. Audio rows name the cue's existence and its sync point only.

Six-frame two-handed work loop, **not scrubbed by progress**. **Post-channel non-DONE outcomes** (registry: `seam_cue_vocabulary`; one cue per verb family, no reason text). `last_result` outcomes `REJECTED` (handoff), `BLOCKED` and `NOT_TAKEN` share one cue per family (MSA-34, C-P10), played only when `last_result.T` is new and within `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 of now (D.17). `post_restore` clears it, so a restore or a MIRROR join never replays one. The cue is **identical across reasons**: a distinct cue per reason would be the reason text P4 forbids.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The `FREE` hatch plate is unchanged underneath | one dull, neutral sputter (IA `ia_nothing` is the precedent), identical across reasons | P |
| VA-26 | Seam cut (`CUT_BLOCKED` at handoff or completion, `NOT_TAKEN`, `REJECTED`) | *the flame gutters and the seam holds* | The cutter `use` frames end by a cut; the spark stream thins to nothing over at most 18 ticks. **The cut never uses the weld's sputter** (the bead is already laid; the cut is the verb that fails to separate it). No residue; the door and bead are unchanged | one dull gutter, identical across reasons | P |
| VA-27 | Install (`SLOT_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT` backstop) | *the module bumps against the socket and stays in your hands* | Only the **carried module sprite** offsets 2 art px toward the bay for 6 ticks and returns for 6 ticks (positional, no squash, no overshoot). Body frames untouched. The module stays in the carry frames; the prompt returns. Identical for every reason. A held-prop nudge, not a body recoil, so it stays inside ART 5.2 ("exaggeration is reserved for stagger, recoil and collapse frames driven by the Controller or Suit"); confirm with the IA owner | one dull bump, identical across reasons | P |
| VA-28 | Install DONE (S2 APPLIED) | The carried instance leaves the carry frames; the module appears **seated in the bay's MACHINE footprint** in its donor dialect, sliding at most 4 art px into the mount over 4 frames then still (cause and rest, ART 7.3 logic, no bounce); an optional 4-frame matte dust puff (Graphite mid motes, hue-less). The module's own presenter takes over from the next frame; a machine runs only if the Graph runs it, so the install adds no spin or glow. No flash, sparkle or "installed" text | the install clunk and seat (Assembly-owned sound, IDB), then the rotor turns or does not (the machine's own) | P |

### Animation and motion constraints

1. **World changes are cuts.** Bead, cap plate, ragged piece, jambs and leaf, scar and the fresh-to-old swap appear by a cut on a committed tick. No tween, scale, fade, dissolve or draw-on. The change lands on the P5 frame of the apply; catch-up ticks (`MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) coalesce to the latest state and are never replayed per tick (MSA-39).
2. **Work loop**: six frames, two-handed, tool forward, stiff-weighted (ART 5.2); the same loop, speed and spark cadence for every target and every outcome (duration and cadence depend on the channel, never the target, IA-11c). No squash, smear, overshoot or recoil on abort, completion or any outcome cue.
3. **Facing lock** during a channel; five drawn facings, three mirrored (the tool hand swaps, ART 5 consistency note 8).
4. **Chest-point lint** (ART 5.1, IA art lint): in work, carry and install frames no tool, limb, module or effect covers `anchor_chest_<facing>` or the beam's forward arc. Sparks anchor at `anchor_hand_<facing>`.
5. **Luminance**: nothing on the HUD above 3 Hz (HR-13). World sparks and outcome transients: at most 3 luminance transitions per second. No full-frame flash, ever. Positional stepping is exempt (accessibility-specialist to confirm, as IA already asks).
6. **Camera**: a weld or cut that changes the avatar's body is PIPE's `BODY_TRANSFER` hard cut, never a pan. A docked weld shows no visible `delta_abs`. The detached piece translates without rotation. Assembly adds no shake, impulse or screen effect (BDY-28, `JOLT_PX` 0).
7. **Outcome transients are one-shots**: the weld sputter at most 24 ticks, the cut gutter at most 18 ticks, the install bump 12 ticks. They end by a cut and carry no persistence.
8. **Nothing decorative moves** at the seam (ART 6.4). The bead, cap plate and ragged piece are static. The only world motion Assembly causes is the weep (FT) and whatever the player connects afterwards.
9. **Reduced motion (HR-8)**: the bar fill persists (it is information), the drain and abort snap, the notch hold is kept; spark density may drop but sparks stay; **the weep and trail propagation are never suppressed**; the outcome transients may shorten but the three family cues stay distinguishable by *shape*, not by motion alone (sputter is broken fragments, gutter is a thinning stream, bump is a displaced prop).
10. **Zoom**: the cursor and all world pieces scale with the world viewport on the integer ladder `k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; the plate and bar keep HUD pixel size (IA F7 guard). No piece falls below the ART 5.6 minimum feature at the widest-field step: the 4-px bead and the 2-px cursor stroke are the floor.
11. **MIRROR and restore**: a presenter plays an outcome cue only if `last_result.T` is new and inside the window; a restore clears `last_result`, so a restore never plays a cue and a joiner never sees a stale one (MSA-34).

### Art bible principles and sections that bind Assembly

| ART reference | How it binds Assembly |
|---|---|
| 1, Principle 1 (darkness is honest; light is a decision) | Cursor, prompt and bead are readable only inside a light. The bead is never revealed by a mask; unlit it is dark and real. The welder channel needs a lit target |
| 1, Principle 2 (state lives in the sprite, not the icon) | A welded seam is a bead, jambs and a leaf; a cut seam is a cap plate or ragged piece. No "welded" badge, no weld-quality indicator, no connection panel, no seam health bar. The prompt shows a keybind and a verb, never a condition (7.0) |
| 1, Principle 3 (emission is exposure) | Sparks are non-emissive at MVP, so the weld's Pillar 3 price is the required light plus the TOOL noise, not the sparks. The bead emits nothing |
| 1, Corollary (the lie leaks) and checklist | See the truth-channel table: a phantom seam may exist only as Filter presentation, leaks violet at an edge and never carries the raised bead. Checklist items 1 to 5 run on every asset below |
| 2.3, 2.4 | The home-ship is "foreign dialects under one warm light, seams at every join"; ART 2.4's carried element for the lost state, "a failure trail spreading along a weld seam while you watch", is VA-15 literally |
| 2.6, 2.8 | Boarding rhythm: the weld is a deliberate, low-energy act under a held breath. Breach rhythm: only a RAGGED cut enters it, with the Graph's and FT's sweep and trails; Assembly adds no red |
| 3.3 (the seam is a raised Hull Grey bead, never hidden), 3.5 (hero #3) | The bead is hero shape #3 and "is never hidden". The only change that may draw the eye at DONE is the fresh bead appearing against grey; bar, plate and cursor are supporting. No decorative circle or moving element near the seam |
| 3.4, 7.2 (UI shape grammar, focus is a shape) | The cursor is a bracket with chamfer corners, not a ring or reticle |
| 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.5, 4.7 | Palette and tests below |
| 5.1, 5.2, 5.6 | Chest-point lint, stiff work loop, minimum feature size |
| 6.1 (grafts and the crew's hand) | "A graft keeps its donor dialect in full; the seam is the only thing that is the crew's." "A section the crew cut away leaves a seam with a cap plate and nothing beyond it." Patch plates are Graphite bodies, on grid and off rhythm |
| 6.2 (unshaded albedo, normals) | Bead, cap plate and ragged piece are albedo plus normal plus specular; no baked light direction, no cast shadows, crevice darkening at most one value step. No rust, brass, copper or warm plastics (scorch is Void Steel, kerf is Graphite mid, matte and hue-less) |
| 6.3, 6.5 | Kit: seams straight, corner, T by fresh and old; door states closed and open. **Do not reuse 6.5's door-leaf states `cut` and `welded`** for these meanings (naming collision, Open Questions) |
| 7.0, 7.3, 7.7 (HR-1, HR-2, HR-4, HR-6, HR-8, HR-13) | Prompt and bar on opaque Ground plates; no Alarm Red on any Assembly element; no HUD shake; reduced motion; nothing above 3 Hz |
| 8.2, 8.5, 8.9 | Sizes (4-px bead, 96 x 96 door cell, 96 x 24 hatch footprint, 48 x 48 / 96 x 96 / 144 x 144 trails), naming grammar, acceptance checklist |

### Palette rules

**Sanity palette (ART 4.4, Corollary).** No violet (`#8A3FD1`, `#B07BE6`) and no flesh (`#D9A58C`, `#B5705C`) anywhere in Assembly's presentation, with no exception: the violet gleam and fringe are the Perception Filter's, outside the sprite, on corruptible readings only. The cursor, plate, bar, bead, cap plate, ragged piece, sparks, outcome transients and seat frames are all in the sane palette.

| Element | Colour | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Bead (fresh and old); bolt rhythm and edge bead of the cap plate | Hull Grey `#8C949C` | thin element only, never a plane (4.1). Fresh is brighter and cleaner, old is dulled and scuffed; at least one value step between them |
| Cap plate body, jambs, ragged-piece body | Graphite lit `#5C6670` / Graphite mid `#2A303A` | Hull Grey is never a plane. Kerf is Graphite mid; scorch is Void Steel `#0B1018`; all matte, hue-less |
| Weep | O2 Cyan `#3FD6E8` (mist) | FT's, soft drifting. With Power Amber, the only lane hue at the MVP seam |
| Rim frost | Coolant Ice `#C8E6F5` | FT row 26 under a thermal gradient |
| Lane pipes at birth | Graphite lit, no emission | ART 4.2 "dead loop"; they take their hue only when carrying (POWER lane Power Amber `#F2C230`, O2 lane O2 Cyan) |
| Sparks | Tungsten family: core `#FFE2A8`, body `#FFCC66`, hue at least 38 degrees | never Fuel Ember, Power Amber or Alarm Red; non-emissive |
| Socket cursor | Tungsten fixture `#FFCC66` (UI accent), flat, unlit, non-emissive | not a light, so no exposure and no beam shape; geometry is the primary channel (HR-11) |
| Plate, bar, needle-mark, keycap | IA's palette unchanged: Ground `#1C2128`, border Graphite lit, Paper White `#DDE4EA`, fill Tungsten `#FFCC66` | no loop hue, no Alarm Red |
| Carried module; seat dust | the module's own palette; dust is Graphite mid | |

**Never on any Assembly element:** Alarm Red (a RAGGED breach's sweep belongs to the Graph, FT and the alarm owner); Chemlight `#86B896` (light colour only); the loop hues on the cursor, plate, bar or outcome cues (a hue is a sentence about a system); any new hue (so no new ART 4.7 entry is required).

**Required tests before approval (ART 8.9).**
1. **ART 4.4 sampling test on the fresh bead first.** A fresh bead under chest-lamp Tungsten is the documented near-case (Hull Grey under fixture Tungsten computes to about hue 43, S 56 %, V 55 %). Sample `seam_straight_fresh` and the cap plate's Hull Grey bolts under all four beam shapes; hue must stay at least 36 degrees. "Brighter and cleaner" means higher value, never higher saturation or an orange pull; if drift appears, pull toward yellow, never desaturate the light.
2. **Greyscale pass (4.5).** Bead Hull Grey (L* about 61) against Graphite lit (about 43) is an 18-point gap and must survive achromatopsia; the weep (about 79) against the bead must also separate by motion (drifting mist versus a static raised bead).
3. **25 % silhouette test (3.1)** on the seam in a lit frame: the bead reads as a line with a break at the hatch.
4. **Colourblind (4.7):** at the MVP seam there is no Coolant and no Fuel lane, so Hull Grey against Fuel Ember (L* 61 against 61) cannot occur until a Vertical Slice lane is added; the backup (raised seam versus glossy puddle) is already named. Bowed rim versus rim frost differ in shape; sputter, gutter and bump differ in shape.

### Truth channel versus corruptible

TRUTH means the consequence channel or an anchor, drawn identically on every client and never wrapped by the Perception Filter. CORRUPTIBLE means module-body presentation that a class (b) hallucination may draw wrongly, always with the palette leak.

| Visual | Class | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Seam bead (fresh and old), floor and wall pieces | **TRUTH** | Layout geometry derived from `welds[]` and `welded_at_T`; the age swap is a derived read (LY-33). The Filter never wraps it (MSA-35, LY-34) |
| Hatch plate, jambs and leaf, **leaf position** | **TRUTH** | Layout and Graph geometry and opening area; whether the seam is open is physical, consequence channel, falsified by walking or by the draught |
| Cap plate, ragged piece, scar, "nothing beyond" | **TRUTH** | Layout socket state `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN` |
| Socket cursor and bracket | **TRUTH (meta register)** | A function of the same static inputs as the prompt; never lie-capable; no gleam, glide or tremor at any sanity stage |
| Prompt plate, verb text, LONG bar, needle-mark, notch | **TRUTH (meta register)** | IA's classification; the bar is the actor's own elapsed time and deliberately not a measure of the job |
| Weep, `SEAL_STRAIN` bowed rim and rim frost, draught streaks, hiss | **TRUTH** | Consequence channel (FT rows 24, 26, FT-14); the strain sprite is separate from the door sprite, so the door can lie but the strain cannot |
| Sparks, work loop, a crewmate's chest beam and channel | **TRUTH (human channel)** | The actor's own animation and the crewmate's beam are anchors (ART 1 Corollary) |
| Outcome cues (sputter, gutter, bump), seat frames | **TRUTH** | Driven by `assembly.state.last_result`, a SIM block identical on all clients; carries no reason, so it is not an oracle |
| **Seam-lane valve and breaker sprites:** the wheel position of the seam's own PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valves and the lever position of the seam's CABLE breaker | **TRUTH (fallback, CD-GDD-ALIGN), until the zero-gradient witness is confirmed** | MSA-23: at zero gradient an open valve has no hiss, creak or frost and a closed breaker no flow, so the sprite is the only reachable witness and a class-(b) lie on it would make a RAGGED cut with no witness (fails P1, P2, P4). The Perception Filter may not wrap these sprites until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a consequence-channel lane witness that exists at zero gradient (ASK G8, ASK FT1). When both confirm in writing, this row reverts to CORRUPTIBLE and the next row's witness carries the load. AC-MSA-126 |
| Other lane node and module-body sprites at the seam: junction powered lamp, door-panel lamp, every valve, breaker and gauge that is not the seam's own sub-edge | **CORRUPTIBLE** | Module-body presentation (IA V3, V4, V5, AD-11); may lie under class (b) with the stage-1 violet gleam on the glass |
| Lane pipe emission, flow chevrons, dash-pulse | **TRUTH (immune, Perception Filter PF-5 row 17a, TRUTH_PX; registry `truth_channel_union`)** | MSA-23 needs the cut mode predictable from the world alone; the "seam breaker is closed" and "valve is open" witnesses must not rest only on a corruptible lever sprite. a state the pipe does not show (an open valve at zero gradient) is covered by the seam-lane valve and breaker fallback row above, not by this one |
| Installed module's medium and state sprites | **CORRUPTIBLE** (module body) | Module-body channel |
| Any phantom seam, false "welded" state or phantom bead (hallucination classes b and d, VS) | **CORRUPTIBLE, Filter layer only** | Must leak: a violet fringe at its edge, never the raised-bead geometry, no collision, no occlusion, no weep, no hatch subject. It never reaches `welds[]`, the consequence channel or the bead geometry (MSA-35). The check is cheap: walk into it |

Two hard lines. (1) No Assembly cue, cursor or plate is ever recoloured, shaken or glided under any sanity stage (HR-6, HR-13). (2) `CUT_BLOCKED` stays a kept truth oracle (a real person is in the hatch, a phantom is not an actor); the Filter must never draw a crewmate-as-horror out of the hatch rectangle or hide a real one in it.

### Asset types implied

Names follow ART 8.5 (`<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>[_<variant tokens>]_<map>.png`, variant order loop, piece, state, facing, size, index). Every normal-mapped piece ships `_alb`, `_nrm`, `_spc`, `_msk`. New stems are proposals for the ART 8.13 maintenance pass; the `state` token list gains `capped` and `ragged`.

**Cited, not authored by Assembly:** `seam_straight_fresh_*`, `seam_straight_old_*`, `seam_corner_fresh_*`, `seam_corner_old_*` (T pieces are VS), `env_foundry_door_round_*` (frame and leaf, 96 x 96 cell, states `closed`, `open`), `trail_hull_seamweep_<s|m|l>_*`, `trail_hull_rimbow_s_*`, `trail_cool_rimfrost_s_*`, `pipe_shared_*` (lane pieces and terminal fittings), `tool_welder_*` and `tool_cutter_*` (`held_<facing>`, `use_<facing>`), `char_crew_body_*` tags `work_<facing>` and `carry_<facing>`, the IA spark and stub assets, the IA prompt-plate and LONG-bar components, and the `anchor_hand_<facing>` and `anchor_chest_<facing>` slices.

**New or specified here:**

| Stem (proposal) | Cell | Maps | What it is | Pri |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `env_foundry_hatch_plate_*` | 96 x 24 hatch footprint, one piece | alb nrm spc msk | The `FREE` socket plate, HULL (Layout owns the state; listed because the cap plate must be a deliberate sibling, not a recolour) | P |
| `env_foundry_hatch_cap_*` (state `capped`) | 96 x 24 | alb nrm spc msk | **Cap plate.** Graphite body; Hull Grey only on the bolt rhythm and an edge bead; on-grid but straddling the wall panel boundary and off the dialect's fastener rhythm (the patch-plate rule of ART 6.1: "we did this in a hurry"); rectangular, chamfered, not round. Plain and quiet: a closed door that is never going to open. Must read at 25 % as a plate where a port was | P |
| `env_foundry_hatch_ragged_*` (state `ragged`) | 96 x 24 | alb nrm spc msk | **Ragged piece.** Torn plate edges built from short straight segments at two or more angles, no forks, no taper-to-point filaments, no iridescence (the web's grammar is branching and non-repeating; this must read as human damage, ART 3.3); matte Void Steel scorch, Graphite mid kerf, hue-less. Solid and light-blocking | P |
| `ui_cursor_socket_corner.png`, `ui_cursor_socket_edge.png` | 2 pieces, 9-slice to `width_tiles` x 48 by 24 | none (flat, unlit) | The VA-2 bracket; `ui_` carries no map suffix (ART 8.5). World-space placement, flat material, non-emissive | P |
| `vfx_weld_sputter_*` | 48 x 48, 6 to 8 frames | alb | VA-25 transient: broken bead fragments and irregular spark bursts, non-emissive, no residue | P |

| `vfx_install_seat_*` | 48 x 48, 4 frames | alb | VA-28 optional matte dust puff; the module slide is the module sprite's own offset | P (optional) |
| Verb tokens `verb_seam_cut`, `verb_install` | text tokens on IA plates | n/a | New tokens for the prompt grammar; IA's `verb_cut` is V8 module salvage and must not be reused (ASK to Interaction) | P |
| `seam_t_fresh_*`, `seam_t_old_*` | 48 x 48 / per Layout | alb nrm spc msk | T-junction pieces for multi-socket sections | V |
| CASCADE tell kit | none new | n/a | Reuses hull-stress hairlines and the real-needle tremor (VA-24) | V |

MVP new-piece count for the ART 8.5, 8.6 budget: two hatch states (cap, ragged) plus the plate sibling, one 9-slice cursor (2 pieces) and 2 to 3 VFX strips. Everything else already sits in the Foundry MVP kit (about 50 dialect pieces and about 60 shared, ART 6.3).

**Audio existence list (sync points above).** Weld channel start, loop, cancel, interrupted, done (IA `ia_start`, `ia_loop`, `ia_cancel`, `ia_abort`, `ia_done`, shared with every channel); seam-cut channel (the same) plus a `LOUD` one-shot at DONE; bead settle at VA-14 (the single audible "it is done" for the weld, no sting); cap seat at VA-20; ragged tear at VA-21; install clunk and seat at VA-28; the three non-DONE cues, one per family, dull and neutral (VA-25 to VA-27). The mood the visuals set is stiff, sealed, patient: a hull that creaks before it fails. Each outcome cue is indistinguishable across reasons (P4).

1. `FREE` socket, docked, welder equipped, lit: prompt plus VA-2 bracket, at the lowest ladder step and at `k_auto + 2`.

5. The ART 4.4 sampling sheet: fresh and old bead and cap-plate bolts under all four beam shapes, hue readout at least 36 degrees.
6. The same seam after `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS`.
7. SEALED cut result: cap plate with the bead beside the scar, nothing beyond.
8. RAGGED cut result: ragged piece, stubs, hiss and the Graph's sweep in frame.
9. Outcome strips: sputter, gutter, bump (frame sequences), proving residue-free and reason-independent.

## Game Feel

*Units: 1 tick = 16.7 ms (`TICK_RATE` 60); a render frame at 60 Hz is one tick, at 30 Hz two. Every number is a design target for the presenters, the HUD and the animation budget; the simulation values are the Formulas section's (D.7 for timing, D.9 for the shape-change time, D.17 for the cue gate). No animation frame rate is invented here: ART 5.2 owns the work loop's six frames and Interaction owns the bar's cadence. Assembly's feel is mostly inherited (Interaction's channel feel, the Pipeline's camera cut, Failure and Trail's weep); this section fixes the numbers Assembly adds: two durations, a delay of one tick, three one-shot cues and the discipline of what is not drawn. The Visual/Audio section owns what is drawn; this section owns how long it takes and how it feels to operate.*

### Feel Reference

**Borrow:** Project Zomboid's timed contextual actions (a linear bar, cancelled by moving, no skill-check minigame, no hit-stop) for the *channel*, and the hands-free chest lamp of *Pandorum* (the beam stays on the job while both hands work) for the *exposure*. The quality borrowed is deliberate, stationary, stiff competence under a held breath: pressing is instant, the work takes visible time, leaving is always free, and the finished thing is a fact in the room, not a fanfare. For the cut, the borrowed quality is the clean terrible certainty of *Aliens*-style bulkhead sealing: a loud burn-through and a plate where a door was.

**Anti-references:** (1) a Barotrauma-style repair minigame or any QTE (a UI surface that could leak module-body truth, and a rhythm demand the "I can leave now" test forbids); (2) Space Engineers or Starbase-style welding that **grows geometry with progress** (a drawn bead with no sim state behind it, VA-10, would be exactly that lie); (3) a building-game ghost-placement confirm (the anti-pillar: not a freeform builder); (4) any screen shake, hit-stop or camera punch on the weld or the cut (`JOLT_PX` 0, BDY-28).

### Input Responsiveness

| Action | Max input-to-response | Ticks | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Press `interact` on the weld prompt: ACK pre-play | the same render frame | 0 (at most 1 frame) | keycap drop, bar at fill 0, first work pose; the hand never waits for the sim (PCI) |
| `begin_weld` or `begin_seam_cut` applied, CONFIRMED | at most 1 tick after the press | 1 | `queued_tick` is the next tick; the bar then fills from the committed value; Assembly adds no latency of its own (the begin only opens the channel, MSA-3) |
| First TOOL noise push | the first accrued tick | 1 (`e` = 1) | the web may hear this from tick 1 (D.7); no visible change marks it (P3: shown in the world, never a meter) |
| Immediate reject at begin (`BUSY`, `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `CUT_BLOCKED`, a structural Layout or Graph reason) | ack withdrawn within 1 render frame of the result event | 1 to 2 | the standard IA rejection: dull sound, no shake, flash or red; capped by IA's `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` 2 (proposed) |
| Let go (release) or `cancel_channel` | the channel ends in the same P2 | 1 | the bar drain is cosmetic and never blocks a new press |
| Move intent during a channel | abort before accrual on that tick | 1 | the body moves at once; the move always wins (PC-7). A cancel wastes at most `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` - 1 = 5 accrued ticks (D.7) |
| Hold `E` to completion, weld | 360 ticks to the DONE handoff (6.0 s); the world changes 1 tick later | `D` + 1 = 361 | `T_apply - Tb` = `D` + 1 for a fresh channel (D.7) |
| Hold `E` to completion, seam cut | 240 ticks to the DONE handoff (4.0 s); the world changes 1 tick later | `D` + 1 = 241 | deliberately faster than the weld (the P5 escape verb; revisit if players use it as a free panic button) |
| DONE handoff to the visible world change | one tick, 16.7 ms | 1 | the completion is queued in P3 of `T_done` and applies in P2 of `T_apply`; the apply and the P5 batch land in the same render frame, inside the bar's 12-tick DONE hold |
| Weld applied to the first weep | at most one Graph block, 100 ms | at most 6 | the first `graph.propagate.*` after the apply (the next tick with `T mod 6 == 0`) sees the new seam; FT draws the s-trace from that step |
| Failed-completion cue (`REJECTED` at the handoff) | the handoff tick's presenter frame | 0 to 1 | the handoff itself finds the reason, so there is nothing to wait for |
| Failed-completion cue (`NOT_TAKEN`, a dropped completion) | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` + 1 = 13 ticks after the submission | 13 (217 ms) | the one slow cue; it is a defence path that the MVP queue depth never normally reaches (E-33) |
| Install press (TAP) | depress frame at the press; clunk at the Applied result | 1 | a sound cannot be withdrawn, so it waits for the result; an install is one tick (no channel) |
| `BLOCKED` install cue | the Applied result's frame | 1 | the bump is played from `last_result` (D.17), one frame after the result |

### Animation Feel Targets

| Animation or cue | Startup | Active | Recovery | Feel goal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weld work loop | first pose held from the press (ACK) until CONFIRMED (at most 1 tick) | six-frame loop for the whole accrued length (360 ticks), **not scrubbed by progress** | **0**: a cut to idle on every terminal state (no recoil, no overshoot) | stiff, committed, readable at 50 % size |
| Cut work loop | as above | six-frame loop for 240 ticks | 0 | the same loop, faster bar (6 versus 9 ticks per px) |
| Bar fill (weld) | appears at 0 on ACK | stepped, **9 ticks per art px** (150 ms) over 40 px | DONE holds 12 ticks then cuts | a clock, not a reading of the job |
| Bar fill (cut) | as above | stepped, **6 ticks per art px** (100 ms) over 40 px | DONE holds 12 ticks then cuts | a clock |
| Cancel drain | 0 | 2x fill speed (IA `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`) | cut by the next ACK | "I let go" |
| Abort | 0 | snap to empty at once | break-notch held 12 ticks | "the world stopped me" |
| Weld result: bead and jambs appear | 0: the P5 frame of the apply | **a cut, no animation** (0 ticks) | the pieces persist | quiet finality: the room changed, nothing celebrated |
| Cut result: cap plate or ragged piece | 0: the P5 frame of the apply | a cut (0 ticks) | persists; the detached section translates without rotation | a clean, terrible certainty |
| Weld non-DONE cue (sputter) | 0 | at most **24 ticks** (400 ms): three irregular spark bursts | 0: ends by a cut, no residue | "the bead did not hold", dull and neutral |
| Cut non-DONE cue (gutter) | 0 | at most **18 ticks** (300 ms): the spark stream thins to nothing | 0 | "the seam held" |
| Install non-DONE cue (bump) | 0 | **6 ticks** the carried module sprite offsets 2 art px toward the bay, **6 ticks** it returns (12 ticks, 200 ms total) | 0 | "it did not seat", positional only, no squash |
| Install DONE (seat) | 0 | the module slides at most 4 art px into the mount over **4 frames**, then still | 0 | cause and rest; weight, then waiting for the rotor |
| Fresh-to-old bead swap | 0 | a cut at `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` 36000 (10 min) | 0 | the ship ageing; never noticed in the moment |

### Impact Moments

| Impact type | Duration | Effect | Configurable |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hit-stop (freeze frames) | none | not used at all (BDY-28; the weight comes from sound and the 6 s stillness) | no |
| Screen shake | none | not used; `JOLT_PX` 0 and `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` moves nothing on screen | no (a Pipeline knob; Assembly adds none) |
| Camera move | 1 frame hard cut, only when the avatar's body changes | PIPE's `BODY_TRANSFER` cut; on a docked weld `delta_abs` is zero so nothing visibly jumps; the detached piece translates without rotation | the cut is the Pipeline's |
| Controller rumble | none | not used for the channel; the Adapter owns rumble and gives Assembly no cue | n/a |
| Time-scale slowdown | none | not used | no |
| Weld DONE | 1 tick of world change inside a 12-tick bar hold | the bead settles; the single audible "it is done"; no sting, no flash | audio level only |
| Cut DONE | `LOUD` one-shot one tick before the plate; the plate on the P5 frame | a loud burn-through, then a cap plate or a ragged scar and the Graph's breach for RAGGED | audio mix only |
| First weld (hero beat) | the whole 6 s plus the 1 s after | room in the mix for the bead settling, the first weep hiss and the bar disappearing; a **mixing preference** routed to Adaptive Audio, not a sim rule: no escalation window and no invulnerability is added (P2 already requires its own tell) | Adaptive Audio's |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight**: heavy and deliberate. A weld is six seconds of standing still, lit, with both hands busy; it is the single heaviest verb the player performs at a socket, and the cut (four seconds) is lighter in time but heavier in consequence. The weight comes from stillness and exposure, never from effects.
- **Player control**: high at every moment *before* the DONE handoff (let go or step away at any tick; the cost is at most five wasted ticks and the channel's partial progress is held for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600); **none** in the single tick between the handoff and the apply, by design (one tick, 16.7 ms, the price of an atomic apply).
- **Snap quality**: crisp and binary on the world side. The hatch plate becomes jambs and a bead in one frame; the door becomes a cap plate in one frame; there are no half-states and no transitions. The bar is stepped, never smooth.
- **Acceleration model**: none. The work loop starts at its first pose on the press and stops by a cut; the bar fills at a constant rate; nothing ramps, eases or overshoots.
- **Failure texture**: every failure is dull, short and neutral, and **identical across reasons** (P4): the weld sputters, the flame gutters, the module bumps. There is no red, no text, no shake and no punishment animation. The player learns *why* from the world (the lane node that was salvaged, the module already in the bay, the crewmate in the hatch), not from the cue. A failure after cost (six seconds spent) is the worst case, and it is rare by construction (`PORT_ABSENT`, `SLOT_OCCUPIED`, `NOT_TAKEN`, a hatch entered late); the cost is the time and the exposure, which are the Pillar 3 price, and nothing is lost that cannot be redone.
- **The inert-weld risk (named)**: for six seconds the world does not change (no progressive bead, VA-10), and after the bead appears a weld moves nothing (LS-15). Both are intended (beat 1 then beat 2 of the Player Fantasy) and both are the loudest feel risk in the system. The only legitimate remedies are sim-backed (the leaf and jambs make the hatch look *newly operable*; the creak or frost when a gradient exists; the weep), never a drawn progressive bead, a flourish or an auto-open. Playtested by the targets below; an open question for the playtest gate.

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

Checked by playtest unless an AC id is given (the measurable ones are in the Acceptance Criteria section).

- [ ] The weld reads as heavy and exposed: playtesters volunteer that they felt watched, standing still, and none describes it as "building" (they say "our ship", "I welded that").
- [ ] No playtester asks "did the weld work?" after the bead appears. If they ask during the six seconds, that is acceptable and intended.
- [ ] The bar's fill is within 1 px of the same fraction of the bar's length at 25, 50, 75 and 100 % of `D` (AC-MSA-113).
- [ ] The fresh bead is visible on the first rendered frame after the apply tick, with zero frames of delay (AC-MSA-114).
- [ ] Every non-DONE cue starts within 13 ticks of the failed submission and ends within its stated one-shot length (AC-MSA-115).
- [ ] No hit-stop, shake, rumble or time-scale effect exists on any Assembly path (AC-MSA-119).
- [ ] No weld frame exceeds `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33 ms and the steady-state frame time after the weld is within 5 % of before (AC-MSA-63).
- [ ] No playtester describes any non-DONE cue as "it worked", and none can tell two reasons apart by the cue (AC-MSA-55).
- [ ] No reviewer uses the words "floaty", "laggy" or "unresponsive" for the press, the bar or the cancel.
- [ ] A RAGGED cut is reported as "my fault, I should have closed the valve", not "the game killed me" (Player Fantasy validation target).

## UI Requirements

**Assembly draws no pixel of its own and adds no screen, menu, meter, panel, tooltip or reason text; but it does have real UI *content*, so this section is the contract on what Assembly feeds the surfaces that others draw.** The prompt plate, the linear bar and the carry-drop prompt are Interaction's and the HUD's (HUD & Suit Layer, undesigned, *provisional*); the in-world socket cursor is Layout's socket highlight (Layout presentation data); the outcome cues are drawn by presenters (PRESENTATION subscribers) from one Assembly block. Pillar 1 forbids the screens a ship-building game would have (no blueprint view, no build mode, no placement ghost with rotation, no connection panel, no weld-quality or seam-health bar, no surge number, no "welded" badge, no reason text); the UI of this system is a keycap, a verb word, a bar and the world itself. This is not a "no UI" system: it has an information table, and the HUD GDD must meet it.

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Weld prompt: keycap glyph for `interact` on the current device plus `tr(verb_weld)` | beside the focused `WELD_SOCKET` subject, at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)`, on the HUD layer outside the world SubViewport (Interaction's plate) | once per render frame from `focus_view` | `weld_offer` (D.2) true: welder equipped, lit, in reach, socket `FREE`, `can_attach` passes, `docked` holds. Hidden otherwise, with no "can't weld" state |
| Seam-cut prompt: keycap plus `tr(verb_seam_cut)` | as above, on the seam's hatch frame | as above | `seam_cut_offer` true: cutter equipped, lit, in reach, socket `WELDED` on an `ACTIVE` seam, seat on the keep-side component (MSA-18). Never shown on the derelict side |
| Install prompt: keycap plus `tr(verb_install)` | anchored to the hardpoint's mount; the carry-drop prompt `G . Drop` stays at the foot origin (AD-5 keep-outs) | as above | carrying a def in `compatible_defs(h)`, hardpoint subject focused, reach, lit, not staggered. An incompatible def: nothing |
| Channel bar (LONG class, 40 art px interior) with needle-mark | attached to the plate's long edge on the side facing away from the anchor (Interaction's component) | `fill_px` per committed tick, no interpolation: weld 9 ticks per px, cut 6 ticks per px | the local seat's `channel_view.state` is ACK, CONFIRMED or PROGRESS for an ASSEMBLY-owned channel; terminal frames (drain, snap and notch, DONE hold 12 ticks) are Interaction's |
| In-world socket cursor (four chamfer-corner brackets, Tungsten, flat, non-emissive) | on the focused socket's reference rectangle, in the avatar's body frame, world space | with the prompt, by a cut | exactly the prompt's condition, and only inside the lit area (the honest edge) |
| Outcome cue (sputter, gutter or bump) | at the socket or on the carried module, in the world; **no HUD element** | once per `last_result.T` change (D.17), inside `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 | `last_result.outcome` is `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED` or `NOT_TAKEN` for the local seat; one cue per verb family, identical across reasons, never a string or a number |
| Everything else | nothing | never | `q`, `leak_bp`, `hp_seam`, `cap_seam`, `A_seam`, the `surge[]` magnitude, seam age, weld count, body count, socket state as text, the cut mode and the reason of any non-DONE outcome are **never displayed** (AC-MSA-19) |

**What the surfaces read (and nothing else).** The HUD reads `focus_view` and `channel_view` (Interaction's IA-41 views); the presenters read the local seat's `last_result` from `assembly.state` (a SIM block, replicated as `SHARED_SUMMARY`) and `welds[]`-derived geometry from Layout. Neither reads `pending`, any Graph node or edge, `forced_mode`, `served_pm` or any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block. Assembly publishes two pure predicates for the surfaces (`weld_offer` and `seam_cut_offer`, used as the `eligible` of the `begin_*` declaration rows, MSA-12) and the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` for Save/Load; it publishes no other read view.

**Rules the HUD and Interaction owners must meet for Assembly's verbs.**

- *Labels are verbs, not states (IA-11).* `verb_weld`, `verb_seam_cut` and `verb_install` carry no condition word (no "Weld (docked)", "Cut (sealed)", "Install (compatible)"). `verb_seam_cut` must stay distinct from V8's `verb_cut` (module salvage): an ASK to Interaction to add the two tokens, not to reuse `verb_cut`. English proposals, owned by the HUD GDD: "Weld", "Cut away", "Install". Localisation through `tr()` with StringName keys; the plate grows to `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` then wraps, never truncating.
- *The prompt may be late, never wrong.* Hide is immediate (the same render frame the offer goes false: a prompt for a pair that is no longer docked, or a seat that stepped into the dark, is a lie by commission); show may be debounced by the HUD (IA D.UI-2), because a late prompt omits a hint and the verb works as soon as it is focused.
- *Bar semantics.* The bar is the actor's own elapsed time, `fill_px = floor(progress_pm x L / 1000)` with `L` 40 and `progress_pm` from ticks only (IA D.UI-1). It is **identical for SEALED and RAGGED**, for every `q` and for every module-body state (AC-MSA-88); the channel length is the declared `D` and never varies with the target. A resumed channel starts from its credited offset, not from zero (IA ledger).
- *No reason text, anywhere.* A rejected begin uses the standard IA rejection (ack withdrawn, dull sound); a post-channel non-DONE outcome uses the family cue of MSA-34; neither carries a word. Accessibility announcements, when AccessKit is in scope (Alpha), are fixed phrases keyed by outcome *state* (done, did not hold), never by cause.
- *Cursor and plate are meta-register truth.* They are functions of static inputs, never lie-capable, and never recoloured, shaken or glided at any sanity stage (HR-6, HR-13). The Perception Filter must not suppress or fake the weld, cut or install prompt (ASK F1).
- *Zoom and scale.* The cursor and all world pieces scale on the integer ladder `k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; the plate and bar keep HUD pixel size and are re-projected every render frame; a zoom or HUD-scale change mid-channel changes nothing in the simulation (MSA-40, AC-MSA-64).
- *Reduced motion and flashing (HR-8, HR-13).* The bar fill persists, drain and abort snap, the notch hold is kept; the outcome transients may shorten but stay distinguishable by shape (fragments, thinning stream, displaced prop); no element above 3 Hz.
- *Input devices.* The keycap glyph follows Interaction's device latch (classified by event type); both Assembly channels are hold-to-work verbs cancelled by movement or release on every device; a gamepad user holds the same `interact` action (no combo, no radial menu).
- *Co-op.* Each seat sees its own plate, bar and cursor; a crewmate's channel is visible only as the crewmate's own work loop, sparks and chest beam in the world (the human channel); a MIRROR client plays a cue only per D.17.
- *Engine facts (verified against `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; the reference has no scripting, core, 2D or scene-tree module).* 4.6 dual focus means the plate and bar nodes are non-focusable and mouse-ignoring; 4.7 `CanvasItem` lines carry no feather, so the bar is filled rectangles on the art-px grid (Interaction's ADR candidate); nothing about `Control` theming or world-to-screen projection is asserted here.

📌 **UX Flag — Modular Ship Assembly**: this system has UI content but no screen of its own. In Pre-Production, the specs that must cover it are `design/ux/hud.md` (the three verb plates `verb_weld`, `verb_seam_cut`, `verb_install` and the LONG bar at 9 and 6 ticks per px, via the Interaction UX pass) and the Layout socket-cursor entry in the in-world pattern library (the four-bracket cursor, flat and non-emissive). No standalone `/ux-design` pass is needed for Assembly; stories that touch UI cite those specs, not this GDD.

## Acceptance Criteria

*Ids AC-MSA-01 to AC-MSA-128 keep the pre-assigned meaning of the criterion index (T8) (AC-MSA-46 and AC-MSA-66 are absorbed into AC-MSA-01 and AC-MSA-58; split ids carry letters); the ids that the corrections and the Edge Cases added continue from AC-MSA-73 (AC-MSA-122 covers E-53; AC-MSA-123 to AC-MSA-128 were added by later revisions); ids 113 to 115 and 119, which the QA review had reserved, are reassigned to the Game Feel benchmarks. Every criterion carries a tag and a rule or edge-case reference. **Tags:** `(auto)` an automated test in `tests/unit/modular_ship_assembly/` or `tests/integration/modular_ship_assembly/`; `(lint)` a static scan or data lint; `(review)` a design or code review; `(manual)` a playtest or a retained screenshot in `production/qa/evidence/`; `(spike)` only the throwaway spike (see Spike Plan) can answer it. **Status markers:** `(depends: ASK Gn / CONFLICT Cn)` means the criterion rests on a provisional ask or an open conflict in the Dependencies tables (T14, T15); a failure there means "contract not yet agreed", not necessarily a defect. `[VS]` marks a Vertical Slice criterion whose rule is specified now.*

**Gate levels** (`.claude/docs/coding-standards.md`): Logic and Integration are BLOCKING; Visual/Feel and UI are BLOCKING and need a **retained** screenshot (a captured-and-discarded one is an assertion, not evidence); Config/Data is ADVISORY. Story typing: weld, cut, install and the verifier are Integration stories (several owners' mutators in one apply); D.1, D.6 to D.11, D.17 and D.18 are Logic; the knob and data lints are Config/Data; AC-MSA-68, 71, 113 and 114 are Visual/Feel plus UI.

**Test rules.** Deterministic (no wall clock, no unseeded RNG, a fixed `world_seed`); isolated; assertions on `abs(actor)` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`; fixtures from constant files or factory functions; the doubles are a SYSTEM-origin submitter (tests cannot reach `weld` or `seam_cut` any other way), an Interaction double (except where a criterion says it needs the real one), a Body double with `docked` controllable, a Perception stub, and a Graph double for capacity. **Performance criteria are spike deliverables until the minimum-spec reference machine is named** (Open Questions): the measurement protocol is a Release export build, 300 warm-up ticks discarded, then the stated sample; apply budgets with headless render off, frame-time budgets with render on. Until the machine is named, a performance criterion records its measured value in the spike report and does not gate.

### Fixture and vocabulary block (referenced by the criteria as FB-n)

**FB-1 MVP fixture constants.** Pair `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E, `line_px` 720, `start_px` 672) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W, `line_px` 24, `start_px` 288), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER 0 and O2 0, no COOLANT lane, offset `(696, 384)` (DA-10), `q` 850, cable port caps 3000 W, `L_seam` 2. Sections: home 5 compartments, B 5, A 4. Module fixtures: `mod_heater`, vacant hardpoint `hp_cargo_heater`; lane node `j_b_main` (def-backed, salvageable), `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` (bare, DA-19, not salvageable). `REACH_PX` 72 (boundary rule as Interaction publishes it, inclusive or exclusive: cite IA, do not restate), `FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `FOOT_BOX_H` 8. Coordinates marked `[UNV]` in a criterion are recomputed from Layout's published hatch rectangles before the story starts.

**FB-2 Command rows (T1).** `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `install_module`: origin PLAYER, `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, coalesce NONE, `max_age_ticks` 120, modes CHANNEL, CHANNEL, TAP. `weld`, `seam_cut`: origin SYSTEM only, explicit `actor` field, coalesce NONE, `max_age_ticks` `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12, payload `{actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause}` and `{actor, seam_id, cause}` with `cause` CHANNEL (CASCADE for `seam_cut`, VS). Absent by decision: `remove_module`, `begin_cut`, `abort_channel`, `set_socket_state`, `install_cap_plate`.

**FB-3 Reason order (first failure wins).** Weld: kernel authority; `NO_TOOL`; `OUT_OF_RANGE`; `NOT_LIT`; `BUSY` (site 1 only, including the `pending.kind != NONE` term); `INVALID_TARGET`; `NOT_ELIGIBLE`; Layout's `can_attach` eight (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`); `MSA.NOT_DOCKED` (sites 1 and 2 only); the Graph's nine structural reasons of `can_join_static` (`SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY`). Then, in the `apply` only, `PORT_ABSENT` (DEFERRED). Cut: kernel authority and the actor terms; `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (wrong component); Layout's `can_detach` (`SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE`); `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`. Install: the actor terms and `compatible_defs(h)` (IMMEDIATE); in the `apply` only `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (DEFERRED) and the defect-class backstop `PORT_INCOMPAT`. Layout reasons surface as `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)`, owner reasons keep their owner's namespace, only `CUT_BLOCKED`, `NOT_DOCKED` and the install outcome `SLOT_OCCUPIED` are `MSA.<CODE>`.

**FB-4 Site skips (T2a).** Sites: 1 the `begin_*` `validate`, 2 the handoff pre-check in `assembly.step`, 3 the completion `validate` (authoritative). `BUSY` is evaluated at site 1 only; `NOT_DOCKED` is skipped at site 3 only; every other term is identical at all three sites; the full `can_join` and `check_install_module` run only in the completion `apply`.

**FB-5 Apply call orders, with the real identifiers.** Weld: `can_join` (A0) -> `weld_join` (Graph M2) -> `attach_section` (Layout LM2) -> `rebase_on_attach` (Body BM3) -> `sync_section` for each of the two sections -> the `assembly.state` write. Cut: derive the mode (D.6) -> `cut_detach` (Graph M3) -> `detach_section` (Layout LM3) -> `rebase_on_detach` (Body BM4) -> `sync_section` for each of the two sections -> the `assembly.state` write. Install: `check_install_module` -> `install_module` (Graph M5, `hp_start := condition_bp`) -> `release_carried` (Suit) -> `clear_carry_ref` (Controller, proposed name) -> `sync_section` -> the `assembly.state` write. **Calls Assembly never makes** (MSA-17): `instantiate_section_emitters`, `discard_section_emitters` (Emitter E1, E2), `set_opening_area` (M13), `apply_damage` (M18), `discard_section` (M4), `discard_section_geometry` (LM4), `place_actor`, `remove_actor`, `transfer_actor`, `set_body_velocity` (BM1, BM2, BM5, BM6), `push_stimulus`, and any Save/Load call.

**FB-6 `assembly.state` v1 fields (T6).** `schema_version` 1; `seats` map keyed by PlayerId (0 to 4 entries, never peer id); per seat `pending {kind NONE|WELD|SEAM_CUT, target_ref, submit_T}` and `last_result {verb_id WELD|SEAM_CUT|INSTALL, target_ref, outcome DONE|REJECTED|BLOCKED|NOT_TAKEN, reason 0-99, T}`; `cascade_deferred` (at most one entry, `[VS]`, empty in MVP). Class SIM, `persist` true, `persist_scope` WORLD, `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]`. `T` values are int64 (a string or two 32-bit integers at the serialisation boundary).

**FB-7 Verifier invariants (T7).** INV-1 an `ACTIVE` Graph SeamRecord is exactly one `ACTIVE` `welds[]` row with both sockets `WELDED(seam_id)`. INV-2 a `CUT_SEALED` or `CUT_RAGGED` row is exactly a `CUT` SeamRecord of the same mode with sockets `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`. INV-3 each body's section set is the connected component over `ACTIVE` welds and the relation is a forest (LY-14). INV-4 every live Layout body has exactly one Body pose row, a `DISSOLVED` body none. INV-5 every actor's `body_id` exists and its `pos_curr` lies in that body's floor area. INV-6 `body_count <= min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)`. INV-7 id monotonicity: no `seam_id` or `body_id` at or above the owner's `next_*`, none reused. INV-10 the `pending` target exists and is `FREE` (weld) or `WELDED` (cut), and `last_result.outcome` is in range. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection (the former INV-8, INV-9) are the Graph's own LS-24 assertions and are cross-checked by AC-MSA-25 and AC-MSA-67.

**FB-8 Knobs (T17) with defaults and ranges.** `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (240-480, multiple of 6); `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (240-480, multiple of 6); `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 850 (400-990 at `LEAK_HP` 4000; effective floor `Q_MIN` = `max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))`); `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 (8-119 and `+1 < min(channels)`); `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 (12-180); `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` 600 `[VS]` (60-1800, multiple of 6); `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` 8 (8-24); budgets `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.2, `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` 0.02, `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33, `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (set in the spike, working figure 5 `[UNV]`). `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60 is a manual-AC parameter, not a sim knob.

### A. Shape, block and the two-phase protocol (MSA-1 to MSA-6; D.7, D.11)

- **AC-MSA-01 (auto)** GIVEN the registered command table, WHEN it is enumerated, THEN exactly `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `weld`, `seam_cut` and `install_module` carry Assembly as owner; `remove_module`, `begin_cut` and `abort_channel` are absent; `weld` and `seam_cut` allow SYSTEM origin only. *(Absorbs the former AC-MSA-46.)* [MSA-1, MSA-28]
- **AC-MSA-02 (lint)** GIVEN every constant or formula name Assembly cites from another owner, WHEN compared with `design/registry/entities.yaml`, THEN each name resolves to a registry entry or, for a knob its owner keeps unregistered (for example `A_CUT`, `A_SEAM_MAX`, `SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100`, `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION`, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES`, `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`), to the owner GDD's Tuning Knobs row; the value or expression used in this GDD and in the data files equals that source; an unresolvable name is a failure, not a skip. [MSA-2]
- **AC-MSA-03 (lint)** GIVEN Assembly's source, WHEN scanned, THEN the only block it writes is `assembly.state`; every other state change is a call to a published mutator of FB-5. [MSA-2]
- **AC-MSA-04 (auto)** GIVEN a seat at the docked pair holding the welder, WHEN `begin_weld` applies in P2 of `Tb` = 437039 and the seat stays still and lit, THEN accrual ticks are 437040 to 437399, the DONE handoff is in P3 of 437399, `weld` is submitted then with `queued_tick` 437399, and it applies in P2 of 437400 at age 1 with `last_result = DONE`; the same script for the cut (`Tb` 439759, `D` 240) applies at 440000; exactly `D` TOOL pushes are recorded per full channel. [MSA-3, D.7]
- **AC-MSA-05 (auto)** GIVEN `assembly.state` v1 per FB-6 on a fixture with two seats (one with `pending {WELD, ref, T}`, one with `last_result {INSTALL, DONE, 0, T}`), WHEN snapshot and restore run, THEN the restored block has the same fields except that every seat's `pending` is NONE and `last_result` is empty; WHEN the snapshot is edited to `schema_version` 2 or 0, THEN restore reports a failure naming the block and keeps the pre-restore state hash. [MSA-4]
- **AC-MSA-06 (lint + auto)** GIVEN the `assembly.state` schema, WHEN scanned, THEN no packed-array typed field exists; GIVEN `pending.submit_T`, `last_result.T` and `last_shape_change_T` set to 2^53 + 1 (9007199254740993), WHEN serialised and parsed, THEN the value is recovered exactly and the serialised form is a string or two 32-bit integers, never a JSON number. [MSA-4, SL-2]
- **AC-MSA-07a (auto)** GIVEN the same `assembly.state` and `channel_view`, WHEN `assembly.step` runs twice, THEN the submitted command lists are byte-identical. [MSA-5]
- **AC-MSA-07b (auto)** GIVEN seats 3, 1 and 2 reaching DONE on one tick, WHEN `assembly.step` runs, THEN submissions are in ascending PlayerId order 1, 2, 3 and never two for one seat on one tick. [MSA-5]
- **AC-MSA-07c (auto)** GIVEN the hook registry, THEN `assembly.step` is `(owner Assembly, divisor 1, offset 0, after [interaction.step])`; registering `interaction.step` with `after [assembly.step]`, or leaving `interaction.step` unknown, fails boot; on a MIRROR client the hook does not run. [MSA-5, E-50]

- **AC-MSA-09 (auto)** GIVEN fixture data files fed to the boot validator, THEN `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 7 fails (clause 1: below the kernel floor of 8), 8 passes, 119 passes, 120 fails (clause 2); the registered completion types carry exactly `{actor, target, cause}` (no `q`, no plan, no mode) and `max_age_ticks == PENDING_TTL_TICKS`. Clause 3 (`PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1 < min(channels)`) is dominated by clause 2 at every legal channel duration and cannot be isolated; it is asserted by the formula test of AC-MSA-79. [MSA-6, D.11, E-3]
- **AC-MSA-81 (auto)** GIVEN the registered command table, WHEN each row is read, THEN it equals FB-2 (origin, binding, coalesce, `max_age_ticks`, mode); GIVEN a PLAYER-origin submission of `weld` or `seam_cut`, THEN it is Rejected `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED` and no block hash changes. [MSA-1, T1, T3 V1]
- **AC-MSA-82 (auto)** GIVEN the block registry, THEN `assembly.state` has the class, persistence, scope, `repl_hint`, `schema_version` and ordered `restore_after` of FB-6; `default_state()` has no seats and an empty `cascade_deferred`; `snapshot()` contains no derived cache. [MSA-4, T6]
- **AC-MSA-83 (auto)** GIVEN a valid `begin_weld` (and separately a valid `begin_seam_cut`), WHEN it applies, THEN `open_channel` and `set_channel_ref` are called once each and the state hashes of `graph.state`, `layout.state` and `body.state` are unchanged. (depends: ASK I1) [MSA-3]
- **AC-MSA-84 (auto)** GIVEN a channel at DONE for which another seat has already welded the socket (or the welder was swapped, or the seat went unlit), WHEN the handoff runs, THEN `last_result` is `REJECTED(<reason>)` for that seat, the command queue gained no entry, `pending` is NONE, and `D` TOOL pushes were spent. - **AC-MSA-99 (auto)** GIVEN `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 and a completion held in the queue for 12 ticks, THEN it applies (`DONE`) and `NOT_TAKEN` is never written; GIVEN 13 ticks, THEN P2 rejects it `STALE` at `submit_T + 13` and the P3 of that tick writes `NOT_TAKEN`; repeat at `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 8 and 119. - **AC-MSA-100 (auto)** GIVEN the simulation paused between the DONE tick and the apply for any wall-clock duration, WHEN resumed, THEN no tick advanced during the pause, the TTL and the ledger did not move, and the completion applies on the first running tick exactly as in an unpaused run. [E-32]
- **AC-MSA-106 (auto)** GIVEN zero seats, WHEN `assembly.step` runs 600 ticks, THEN it submits nothing and `assembly.state` equals `default_state()`. [E-6]

### B. Predicates, reasons and the P4 boundary (MSA-7 to MSA-10; D.2)

- **AC-MSA-96 (auto)** GIVEN a P2 queue holding a crewmate's salvage of `j_b_main` with an earlier `queued_tick` than the `weld`, WHEN P2 runs, THEN the salvage applies first and the weld is `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`; GIVEN the reverse order, THEN the weld is `DONE`; a Graph double whose structural verdict at A0 differs from `validate`'s raises `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT` in a debug build. - **AC-MSA-88 (auto)** GIVEN fixtures that vary every module-body field over its range (node `hp` in {0, 3999, 4000, 7000, 10000}, every `state`, every `forced_mode`, `served_pm` 0 and max), WHEN the prompt, the PLAYER-origin `validate` result, the channel duration and the bar length are read for `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut` and the install prompt, THEN they are identical across all combinations. - **AC-MSA-14 (auto)** GIVEN fixtures that violate each structural reason, WHEN `can_join_static` is evaluated, THEN the first failing reason is returned in LS-15 order; GIVEN a salvaged lane node and a structural failure together, THEN the structural reason wins (`PORT_ABSENT` never masks). - **AC-MSA-89 (auto)** GIVEN one fixture per Layout `can_attach` reason, WHEN `weld` validates, THEN it is Rejected `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` and no mutator spy count rises; GIVEN Body's `can_rebase_attach` failing with `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` (and `BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID` if Body publishes it), THEN Rejected in Body's namespace with zero mutator calls. - **AC-MSA-37 (auto)** GIVEN one actor at the maximum legal reach stance (`pos = (680, 720)`, 8 px clearance `[UNV]`), WHEN a SEALED cut begins, THEN `CUT_BLOCKED` does not fire; GIVEN the same actor one foot-box east (`pos = (696, 720)`, overlap 64 `[UNV]`), THEN it fires; Layout's authoring lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` passes on the MVP pair (asserted as a precondition); a manual confirmation covers only "a legal stance exists on the real authored socket". - **AC-MSA-38 (auto, stub submitter) [VS]** GIVEN a `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` and an occupied hatch rectangle, WHEN it is submitted, THEN it defers; WHEN the rectangle clears before `deadline_T`, THEN it applies; GIVEN the rectangle clear at `deadline_T`, THEN APPLY (clear is checked first); occupied at `deadline_T - 1`, THEN DEFER; occupied at `deadline_T`, THEN ABANDON and the seam stays welded, never forced. `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` 61, 5 or 1801 fails load, 60 and 1800 pass. (the 10 s warning owner is an open question, F-12 in Open Questions) [MSA-22, D.10]
- **AC-MSA-121 (lint + auto)** GIVEN the MVP source, THEN nothing constructs `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}`, `cascade_deferred` is empty throughout MVP scripts, and a `weld` with `cause` other than `CHANNEL` is rejected. [MSA-22]
- **AC-MSA-93 (auto)** GIVEN an as-welded seam with equal pressure and temperature, hatch closed, valves closed, breaker open, WHEN 600 Graph steps run, THEN no `SEAL_STRAIN`, rim frost or valve-hiss descriptor is emitted (a quiet seam); GIVEN each of the three RAGGED causes (hatch open, seam valve open, seam breaker closed), THEN a distinct world-witness descriptor exists (open leaf and draught, hiss, powered junction state). **Blocked until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm the witnesses exist (Gaps), including the zero-gradient witnesses for an open seam valve and a closed seam breaker (ASK G8, ASK FT1); AC-MSA-39 cannot pass before this does. This is a BLOCKING dependency (CD-GDD-ALIGN); AC-MSA-126 holds the line in the meantime.** (depends: ASK to Graph and Failure & Trail) [MSA-23]
- **AC-MSA-126 (auto, Filter stub + lint)** GIVEN a welded seam at zero gradient (equal pressure and temperature on both sides, the hatch closed, the seam O2 valve truly OPEN, no `SEAL_STRAIN`, rim frost, hiss or draught descriptor emitted) and a `PERCEPTION_OUT` stub applying a class-(b) lie that renders the seam valve CLOSED, WHEN the fallback of MSA-23 is in force (the witness not confirmed), THEN (i) the Filter's wrapped-sprite registry contains no id of the seam's own PIPE or DUCT valve or CABLE breaker sprites (lint: the registry is empty of seam-lane sprite ids), (ii) the seam valve renders its true position OPEN on every client, (iii) a cut at completion is RAGGED and the player had a reachable witness (the true open-valve sprite) before pressing; GIVEN the same fixture with the seam breaker truly CLOSED, THEN the same three assertions hold for the lever. GIVEN the fallback lifted (both owners confirm), THEN a lane witness descriptor exists for each state at zero gradient (AC-MSA-93) and the seam sprites may be wrapped. [MSA-23, MSA-35, E-37]
- **AC-MSA-39 (manual)** GIVEN five first-time players, each facing four cut scenarios in a randomised order that includes at least one SEALED and one RAGGED of each cause (hatch left open, seam valve open, seam breaker closed) so that "always answer clean" scores at most 2 of 4, WHEN each is asked before pressing `E` whether the cut will be clean (having looked within `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60), THEN at least 4 of the 5 answer all four correctly and no player describes a RAGGED outcome as "random". (depends: AC-MSA-93) [MSA-23]
- **AC-MSA-40 (auto)** GIVEN a cut, THEN the detached body persists as an orphan Layout body with a Body pose row, and Assembly's source calls neither M4 nor LM4. [MSA-24, E-52]
- **AC-MSA-41 (auto)** GIVEN weld, then cut, then a second weld attempt on either socket, THEN the result is `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, no prompt appears and the socket plate shows the cap. [MSA-25, E-25]
- **AC-MSA-109 (auto + lint)** GIVEN a socket in `CUT_OPEN` or `CAPPED`, WHEN a weld is attempted, THEN `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` and no prompt; GIVEN the registered mutators and commands, THEN no `set_socket_state` or `install_cap_plate` exists and sockets reach `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN` only through the cut apply. [MSA-25]
- **AC-MSA-110 (auto, Graph integration)** GIVEN a RAGGED cut, WHEN the next Graph steps run, THEN both the home-side and derelict-side adjacent compartments show a breach per the Graph's cited rates (cross-check the Graph's own figures; do not restate them), `stubs[]` exist for each uncapped `open_end`, and no actor velocity changed (no impulse, BDY-28). [E-21, E-26]
- **AC-MSA-75 (auto)** GIVEN a crewmate in the leaving component at a keep-side cut and no actor with a foot box in either hatch rectangle at completion, THEN the cut proceeds (no blanket far-side gate in MVP and Vertical Slice A) and the crewmate ends on the detached body with `abs` conserved. [E-22]
- **AC-MSA-123 (auto)** GIVEN a SEALED-prepared welded seam, a keep-side cutter begun on a `CHANNEL` cut at tick `t0`, and a crewmate on the leaving component (the FB-block second actor), WHEN the crewmate opens the seam hatch (`area` 64, V4) and a foot box enters a hatch rectangle at any tick up to and including the completion tick `t0 + 240` (the overlap read of the validate that precedes the apply), THEN the completion is rejected `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (`last_result` `REJECTED(CUT_BLOCKED)` or `NOT_TAKEN` per AC-MSA-36), nothing is submitted, `cut_detach` and `detach_section` are never called, the socket stays `WELDED`, the seam stays `ACTIVE`, and the crewmate's `abs` is unchanged. Variants: (i) the same crewmate opens the hatch but stays out of both rectangles, THEN the cut applies RAGGED (the veto is stepping in, not opening); (ii) the crewmate has `abs` farther than 480 px (10 tiles at `V_WALK` 2) from the passage at `t0` and cannot arrive, THEN the cut applies (the honest remainder, recorded not hidden). Coordinates are `[UNV]` until Layout publishes the rectangles (ASK L2). [MSA-18, MSA-21, D.13, E-22]
- **AC-MSA-124 (design gate, `[VS]`)** GIVEN Web Map & Transit's GDD at approval, THEN the approving review records (i) whether a recovery path for crew stranded on a detached body exists that does not depend on the cutter's action (ASK T2) and (ii) whether the seam-cut TOOL loop is audible on the far side of a closed seam (ASK AU1); if either is absent, `CUT_STRANDS` is adopted in this GDD as the sanity-gated rule of MSA-18 before the Vertical Slice exit, and the test is then: GIVEN another seat in the leaving component and a cutter with `harm_eligible` clear, THEN a `CHANNEL` cut is rejected `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` at handoff and completion and never at begin; GIVEN `harm_eligible` set, THEN the cut applies and an `incidents` row attributed to the web is written by Sanity; GIVEN a hallucinated crewmate (no actor in the leaving component), THEN the verdict follows true positions. [MSA-18, E-22, Open Question 2]
- **AC-MSA-76 (auto)** GIVEN an autosave due during a RAGGED cut's decompression, THEN the Graph soft gate does not defer it (no SL-5 soft row (a) descriptor is ENDOGENOUS), the hard DOWNED/DEAD gate still applies, and the restored world holds the post-cut breach with its stubs. [MSA-33, E-31]

- **AC-MSA-50 (auto)** GIVEN a weld cancelled by movement at `e` = 100, WHEN the seat re-begins within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600 (exactly 600 ticks after the abort is the last that resumes), THEN the remaining time is 264 ticks, the DONE handoff is on tick `Tb' + 264`, the `weld` applies one tick later, and total accrued TOOL ticks are 363; GIVEN a re-begin at 601 ticks, THEN a fresh 360. - **AC-MSA-51 (auto, fault injection)** GIVEN a stub mutator that throws inside the weld's A3 and then A4, the cut's A2, A3 and A4, and the install's A2 and A3, and a forced verifier failure, THEN each leaves the kernel FAULTED with the command `Dropped(FAULTED)`, no write over the existing save, and `request_restore` returning a state-hash-equal world; GIVEN an autosave at `last_save_T` and a fault injected 7200 ticks later, THEN the restored world hash equals the autosave's, the lost span (`fault_loss_ticks`) is 7200 ticks, which is at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` + `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 18000, and while FAULTED an autosave attempt writes nothing. [MSA-31, D.16, E-35]
- **AC-MSA-52 (auto)** GIVEN a fixture violating each of INV-1 to INV-7 and INV-10 (FB-7), THEN the verifier reports exactly that id (eight fixtures); the release subset after each apply is INV-1, INV-2 and INV-7. [MSA-32]
- **AC-MSA-53 (auto)** GIVEN a save with an `ACTIVE` `welds[]` row and no Graph SeamRecord, THEN restore fails: the kernel is FAULTED with the block id and the INV id in the diagnostic, no tick runs, and the save file bytes are unchanged. [MSA-32, E-36]
- **AC-MSA-118 (auto)** GIVEN a restore, WHEN `assembly.state`'s `post_restore` runs, THEN all eight invariants run hard and **before** the seat records are cleared (INV-10 reads the as-restored `pending`), each fixture violating one of them fails the restore: the kernel is FAULTED with the block id and the INV id in the diagnostic, no tick runs, and the save file bytes are unchanged; and the verifier changes no block (hash before equals after); a save captured in the handoff-to-apply tick (target still `FREE` or `WELDED`) restores without an INV-10 failure. - **AC-MSA-54 (auto)** GIVEN a weld at T = 437400 and a cut at 440000, THEN `last_shape_change_T()` is 437400 then 440000, NONE when `welds[]` is empty, and is two 32-bit parts or a string at the serialisation boundary; `event_due` is true at an elapsed gap of 5400 and false below `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1800. - **AC-MSA-55a (auto)** GIVEN each post-channel non-DONE outcome per verb family (handoff `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)` or `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`, `NOT_TAKEN`), THEN the cue id is the same within a family and differs between the weld, cut and install families, and no presenter-facing event carries a reason string or number. - **AC-MSA-56 (auto)** GIVEN `last_result.T` = 437412, WHEN the presenter runs at 437420, THEN the cue plays once; at 437472 (60 ticks after `last_result.T`) it plays and at 437473 (61) it does not; at 437500 it does not; two presenter frames with the same `last_result.T` play it once; after a restore (empty `last_result`) it never plays; a MIRROR join plays no stale cue. - **AC-MSA-59 (auto)** GIVEN two seats, THEN the outcomes are exactly: (a) on the two sockets of the one pair with DONE on the same tick: one `DONE`, and the second seat's `last_result` is `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + 13`; (b) on consecutive ticks with the first completion already applied: the second seat's handoff writes `REJECTED(SOCKET_NOT_FREE)` and submits nothing; (c) on consecutive ticks with the first completion still queued (backlog double): both are submitted, exactly one `DONE`, the other `NOT_TAKEN`; (d) two seats on the same socket: the second `begin_weld` is `BUSY` (IA-34). - **AC-MSA-60 (auto)** GIVEN a restore mid-channel, THEN no channel record, no `pending`, no `last_result`, no orphan and no cue remain. [MSA-38, E-29]

- **AC-MSA-61 (auto)** GIVEN a snapshot at every tick of a weld-and-cut script, WHEN restored and continued, THEN the state hash equals the uninterrupted run at every tick where no ASSEMBLY channel record is open and no `pending` is set; while a channel is open the only difference is the dropped channel record and its ledger entry; at the handoff-to-apply tick the only difference is `pending`. - **AC-MSA-101 (auto)** GIVEN four ticks run in one render frame including the weld apply tick, WHEN the frame completes, THEN each P5 subscriber double (PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail) was invoked once with the final committed state and never with an intermediate one. [E-46, MSA-39]
- **AC-MSA-63 (spike)** GIVEN the weld frame on the reference machine, THEN P5 subscribers cost at most `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, the PROJECTOR at most `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, no frame in the weld frame and the following 3 frames exceeds `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33, and the mean frame time over 300 frames starting 60 frames after the weld is within 5 % of the mean over the 300 frames before it. - **AC-MSA-108 (spike)** GIVEN the reference machine, WHEN timed over 100 cuts and 100 installs, THEN p99 of the cut apply is at most `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5 and of the install apply at most `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.2, each reported per D.15 term; and the whole weld tick (P2 plus P3) is under `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 (a logging threshold, so the assertion is "no logged overrun"). - **AC-MSA-64 (auto)** GIVEN the same weld-and-cut script, WHEN run with the camera at `k` = 1, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2`, THEN the canonical `state_hash` is identical at every tick, the `abs` of every actor is identical and every offset is a multiple of 24 px; and GIVEN the script rerun with a carried module, 0 and 4 seats, a CASCADE stub submitter present, after a restore, and as a MIRROR, THEN node count is conserved, edges are +3 then -3 tombstoned, body count never exceeds N0 and `abs` is conserved at every tick in each variant. - **AC-MSA-65 (lint + auto)** GIVEN the worst MVP sequence (home + A + B + pod), THEN `bodies_after_worst_sequence` = 4 and `4 <= min(MAX_BODIES 8, BODIES_LIVE_MAX 4)`, reported as zero margin; the path 4 -> 3 -> 4 holds; GIVEN five live bodies against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4, THEN the surplus body's lights draw as `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins and the sim state hash is unchanged (Lighting's criterion, listed because the zero margin makes it reachable). (depends: ASK LT1) [MSA-41, D.8]
- **AC-MSA-67 (auto, nightly; also the spike's second run)** GIVEN 1,000 seeded weld/cut sequences of 1 to 8 operations over test-only fixture templates (a three-pair chain; seeds from `rng_derive`; a fixed seed list in the repo plus a rolling seed, the failing seed printed and added to the fixed list; runtime cap 10 minutes so the suite is never skipped), WHEN the verifier runs after every operation, THEN zero violations of INV-1 to INV-7 and INV-10, zero FAULTED, no id reused, N(t) <= N0, and node and edge conservation closes (the Graph's LS-24 assertion, cross-checked). - **AC-MSA-79 (auto)** GIVEN the exact-output table of Assembly-owned integer formulas, WHEN each worked example runs as a unit test with the stated inputs, THEN the stated outputs are produced exactly: D.1 (as AC-MSA-18); D.2 booleans; D.3 pair; D.5 `(696, 384)`; D.6 the five rows; D.7 (437039 -> 437399 -> 437400; 439759 -> 439999 -> 440000; resume 264; total 363); D.8 (4, 3, 4); D.9 (437400, 440000, NONE, `event_due` true at gap 5400); D.10 (1000 + 600 = 1600); D.11 (12 valid; 7, 120, 240 invalid); D.12 counts; D.17 (8 plays, 88 does not); D.18 (10 <= 12). D.4, D.14, D.15 and D.16 are cited or documentation formulas and are tested through their owners' APIs (D.4 by AC-MSA-94). [Formulas]
