### I. Lines of play and the playtest gate

- **AC-MSA-69 (manual)** GIVEN five playtesters, THEN at least two take the weld line (a completed weld) and at least two take the strip-without-weld line (at least one completed V8 salvage on derelict B before any weld attempt); if either is below 1 of 5, re-tune the welded section's payoff or exposure. [MSA-42]
- **AC-MSA-125 (Vertical Slice GATE, auto + manual)** GIVEN the Vertical Slice build and its content script, WHEN a scripted player runs weld, cut, weld, cut, weld (`VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 3 consecutive weld cycles, range 2-6), THEN every weld finds a `FREE` home-side socket through exactly one of Layout's three options (a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket keeping its history; more home sockets; derelicts that bring three or more sockets), `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` does not fire, and the option chosen is recorded in Layout and this GDD. The criterion is not evaluated at MVP (the MVP playtest gate still must not require a second weld, MSA-25); **it blocks the Vertical Slice exit**: `/gate-check` for the Vertical Slice fails while it is unmet. [MSA-25, Open Question 7]
- **AC-MSA-127 (manual)** GIVEN five playtesters shown, with no HUD and no explanation, four seams in randomised order (two fresh, welded under 60 s ago; two settled, past the end of LSG D.14's wear-in, about 16.7 minutes), WHEN each is asked "newer or older?" using the weep or the bead alone, THEN at least 4 of the 5 answer all four correctly and none cites a number or an indicator. A failure is a tuning input to the Graph and Failure & Trail (Open Question 11), not a defect of Assembly. [Player Fantasy Beat 2, Open Question 11]
- **AC-MSA-128 (manual, co-op)** GIVEN two-seat co-op playtests of the first weld and the first cut, THEN the Fellowship targets of Validation targets (3 of 4 first welds with the second seat standing off the beam or on the far side without being told; at least half of the pairs use "we" language) are recorded, with no Assembly rule added for either. A miss is a playtest finding, not a mechanic request. [Player Fantasy, co-op beat]
- **AC-MSA-70 (manual)** GIVEN five playtesters with instrumented telemetry events (prompt first appears, channel begins, bead appears, first seam-breaker close, each with a tick), THEN the median interval from the weld prompt first appearing to the channel beginning is at most 45 s, and the median interval from the bead to the first seam-breaker close is within 60 to 180 s inclusive. Both are `[PLACEHOLDER]` playtest targets: a failure is a tuning input, not a defect. (depends: DA OQ-DA-31, which answered ASK D1; the DA-10 repeat remains) [MSA-42]

### Cross-system criteria recorded here (owners outside this GDD)

These belong in the owners' lists; Assembly records them so the tester knows they exist. None gates Assembly's story.

- **Lighting (ASK LT1):** five live bodies against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4 degrade to `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins with the sim hash unchanged (AC-MSA-65 repeats it).
- **Interaction and Lighting (E-51):** GIVEN `lit` satisfied by a glowstick alone, THEN the weld proceeds with no Assembly special case.
- **Derelict Authoring (ASK D1, answered by OQ-DA-31):** GIVEN a new game, THEN V3 (`j_cryo`) and V4 are exercised on `home_ship` before any sortie, so no dock gate exists; `weld_offer` reads no player history (a content-script check on the fault order, not a runtime read).
- **Attention (ASK A1):** GIVEN a cut after the locus has moved, THEN the Attention level is not reset.
- **Per-ASK contract tests:** one criterion per row of T15 is added when the owner accepts the ask (ids are assigned then); until then the rows are the Gaps below.

### Gaps / untestable as written

1. **O2-lane numbers (`cap_duct` `[UNV]`, ASK G6(d)):** no O2-lane value of D.1 or D.4 can be asserted; the POWER-lane half is tested (AC-MSA-18, AC-MSA-94).
2. **Cap and decay-clock carry-through on install (IDB-18, ASK G5):** `install_module` has no such parameters; there is no criterion until the Graph and Item DB rule. Listed as blocked, not dropped.
3. **The cut witnesses (AC-MSA-93), BLOCKING:** the open-leaf draught, the seam-valve hiss, the powered-junction state and, above all, a witness for an open seam valve and a closed seam breaker **at zero gradient** (where there is no hiss, creak or frost) must exist as consequence-channel descriptors; until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm them, AC-MSA-39 cannot pass, MSA-23 is a promise, and the Perception Filter may not wrap the seam-lane valve and breaker sprites (fallback, AC-MSA-126).
4. **Performance criteria (AC-MSA-17b, 23, 62, 63, 108):** spike deliverables until the minimum-spec reference machine is named (Open Questions).
5. **AC-MSA-29b** is Interaction's and Derelict Authoring's lint: S0 to S3 pass (68, 100, 112, 112 against 120) and S4 (144) reports WARN, never ERROR; non-gating on Assembly.
6. **AC-MSA-48 and Attention's AC-AT-72 agree (C5 closed 2026-10-27):** both assert zero `LOUD` at weld completion and exactly one at seam-cut DONE.
7. **Hatch-rectangle and stance coordinates in AC-MSA-36 and 37 are `[UNV]`:** recomputed from Layout's published rectangles before the story starts.
8. **Layout key names in AC-MSA-22** are provisional until Layout enumerates them (D.12 gives only counts).
9. **AC-MSA-09 clause 3** (`PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1 < min(channels)`) is dominated by clause 2 and cannot be isolated at legal durations; it is covered by the formula test of AC-MSA-79.
10. **Per-ASK contract tests** (open_channel, `seam_view`, `detach_preview`, `can_join_static`, the Transit hold, the Filter lint) have no criterion until the owner accepts the ask; the Dependency status table above is the list.
11. **D.10's 10 s warning for a SYSTEM cut (F-12):** it is not stated which system enforces `W_MIN_WARNING_S` for a CASCADE cut (the Graph's gate covers ENDOGENOUS transitions only); AC-MSA-38 is `[VS]` until this is clarified.
12. **Everything that needs a hallucination class (a), (c), (d)** (AC-MSA-105) is Vertical Slice and tested through stubs now.

## Spike Plan

*This system is the riskiest MVP build item (index risk PR-SCOPE) and the kernel's stress test (kernel Interactions row 5), so it is the first spike. The spike is **throwaway** (concept: 4 to 8 weeks) and answers questions the design cannot: cost on a real tree, determinism under a moving body, and whether the atomic apply holds. It runs **headless first** (kernel H10: `Kernel.step()` with no scene tree), then once in a scene for the frame-time items. The spike produces numbers and a go or no-go; it does not produce shippable code, and its measured values are written back into T9, T17 and the Acceptance Criteria by the author who reads the report. Performance numbers are recorded on the reference machine (Open Questions) and are spike deliverables until it is named.*

**Engine coverage statement.** `docs/engine-reference/godot/` contains `animation`, `audio`, `input`, `navigation`, `networking`, `physics`, `rendering` and `ui` modules and **no scripting, core, 2D, tilemap or scene-tree module**. Every engine behaviour below that concerns `Node2D` re-parenting, Y-sort tie rules, `TileMapLayer` batching after a merge, `Dictionary` iteration order or integer precision in a build is therefore `[UNV]` until the spike measures it. The only engine facts verified from the reference are that `TileMapLayer` replaces `TileMap` (breaking-changes), that 4.6 added scene-tile rotation, that setting one element of a packed array no longer calls the property setter in 4.7 (the reason `assembly.state` holds no packed array and every mutator marks its dirty keys explicitly), and that an override of a method with a typed return inherits that type and needs an explicit `return` (the reason command handlers are plain-data records, not an inheritance hierarchy).

| # | Spike item | Method | Pass | Fail | Criteria it feeds |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | Weld on a moving body with the Graph on its cadence | home + B, body velocity up to `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16, `graph.propagate.*` at divisor 6, weld 100 times in a loop (reset each run); headless, then in a scene | zero FAULTED; `abs` of every actor conserved exactly when docked; whole weld apply p99 at most 0.5 ms Release with `weld_join` alone at most 0.2 ms; the D.15 split reported per term | any FAULTED, any `abs` drift, or the apply over budget on the reference machine | AC-MSA-23, 72, 78, 116 |
| S2 | 1,000 seeded weld and cut sequences | **test-only fixture templates** (Layout's `fixture_airlock` chain of at least three socket pairs, because MVP content has one pair and sockets are terminal); operations: weld, cut SEALED, cut RAGGED, advance k ticks, open or close the seam hatch; seeds from `rng_derive(world_seed, stream, counter)`; 1 to 8 operations; the verifier after each operation | zero verifier violations, zero FAULTED, conservation closes (LS-24), ids never reused, N(t) at most N0; runtime under 10 minutes | any violation | AC-MSA-52, 67, 118 |
| S3 | PROJECTOR cost of "two hulls become one" | two scenes at MVP size (home 5 compartments + B 5); measure the weld frame and the next 10 frames under each shape option below | PROJECTOR at most `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 ms on the weld frame; no frame above `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33; steady-state frame time after the weld within 5 % of before; no Y-sort or batching regression | any budget exceeded | AC-MSA-63 |
| S4 | Weld, cut, weld again | the exact script, in the scene | the second attempt returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, no prompt appears, the cap plate is on the socket | any other result | AC-MSA-41, 109 |
| S5 | Camera cut at `BODY_TRANSFER` | weld with the avatar on the absorbed body at every zoom level 1 to `k_auto + 2`, docked and (for the fallback) not docked | no visible `delta_abs` jump when docked; screen position preserved where layout allows; under the fallback the cut hides the jump | a visible jump | AC-MSA-71 |
| S6 | Fault injection | a stub mutator that throws in the weld's A3, in A4, in the cut's A2 to A4, in the install's A2 and A3, and a forced verifier failure | the kernel enters FAULTED, the command is `Dropped(FAULTED)`, `request_restore` of the last autosave restores a hash-equal state, the save file is untouched | a partial state survives restore | AC-MSA-51, 53 |
| S7 | Save and restore equivalence | snapshot at every tick over a weld-and-cut script, restore, continue | hash-equal to the uninterrupted run at every tick where no ASSEMBLY channel record is open and no `pending` is set; the two permitted differences (a dropped channel record; `pending` at the handoff-to-apply tick) asserted explicitly | divergence anywhere else | AC-MSA-60, 61 |
| S8 | Packed-array and dirty-mark probe | after each Assembly mutator, compare the dirty-key set to the expected set (D.12) | exact match; no block collapses to `ALL` | a missing or extra key | AC-MSA-22 |
| S9 | Cut and install apply cost | 100 cuts (SEALED and RAGGED) and 100 installs | cut p99 at most `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5; install p99 at most `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.2 | over budget | AC-MSA-108 |
| S10 | `weld_offer` hot path | 100,000 calls on a focused socket with the static half cached | the measured mean is written into `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (working figure 5 us `[UNV]`) | the offer needs a node or edge accessor to be correct | AC-MSA-17b |
| S11 | Subject budget at the seam | count `S_total` and `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW` for `{home + B}` and the loaded set `{home + B, A}` | `S_total` at most 192 for `{home + B}` with the door faces; the loaded-set figure reported for ASK G4 | overflow in `{home + B}` | AC-MSA-29a, 102 |

**PROJECTOR shape options to evaluate in S3 (behaviour only, no recipe; the decision is ADR-LAY-03).** (a) a per-body scene root, with absorbed sections re-parented under the surviving body's root; (b) per-section roots stay in place and only the body-frame offset changes (no re-parenting); (c) per-section roots stay and the PROJECTOR recomputes section positions in world space each frame. Test (b) first: it has the fewest scene mutations on the weld frame. Which is cheapest is `[UNV]` until measured.

**Order and gates.** Run S1, S3 and S6 first: they are the three that can sink the design (determinism of the atomic apply, the render cost of the merge, and the failure model). If S1 or S6 fails, the atomic one-tick command is not safe as designed and the fallback is to split the apply across ticks behind a Graph-held "welding" state, which re-opens MSA-14, the Graph's LS-24 and the kernel's CR-9 and is escalated to the technical-director. If S3 fails for every option, the fallback is a one-frame fade-free hide behind the existing camera cut (S5) with the PROJECTOR rebuild spread over the hold of the DONE bar, and the budget is renegotiated. S2, S7 and S8 then run continuously as regression tests (AC-MSA-67 and 72 are tagged nightly), and S4 to S5 run once per scene change. S1 to S3 and S6 are the exit gate for starting the MVP build of this system.

**What the spike does not answer.** It does not answer the feel of the six stationary seconds, the readability of the cut witnesses or the first-connect tension (those are playtest items, Open Questions), and it does not validate the Graph's, Layout's or Body's own spikes, which it only consumes.

**ADR candidates fed by this plan** (none exists yet): the atomic weld, cut and install apply order and failure model, including the verifier's placement (CR-9); channel hosting (Interaction's record with `owner_system`, `open_channel`, the DONE handoff); the PROJECTOR shape after a weld (ADR-LAY-03, spike-gated); read-view injection for `eligible` predicates (Interaction to Assembly); block data as a Resource versus a RefCounted for `assembly.state`; the test-only fixture-template policy for the 1,000-sequence property test.

## Open Questions

The routed CONFLICT and ASK rows (C1 to C13, G1 to G9, L1 to L4, B1, I1, S1, T1, T2, F1, F2, FT1, AU1, D1, K1, A1, LT1) live in T14 and T15 of the Dependencies section and are not duplicated here. This table lists what needs a **decision** or a **measurement**. User-decision items come first; each carries the recommended answer the GDD already assumes. Targets: *playtest* (the MVP playtest gate), *next GDD* (the named neighbour's authoring), *ADR* (the architecture pass), *spike* (the Spike Plan).

|---|---|---|---|

Recommended: accept the three-verb slice as the MVP definition and amend the concept's wording; the alternative (weld only) leaves a salvaged `mod_heater` with no bay and the staged power-overload fault with no resolution. | user (producer) | before the MVP build; amend `game-concept.md` |
| 2 | **Far-side crewmate on a keep-side cut (USER DECISION, VS-B; CONDITIONAL, CD-GDD-ALIGN).** A seam cut can strand or decompress a crewmate on the leaving piece. Vertical Slice B: the lean "no gate" (the user's "death is a bitch.
